    τ ≈ 1.551i (0.575). D at k = 7 peaks at τ ≈ ½ + 2.224i (0.791), with further critical points at 2.226i and
    ½ + 1.091i.

**What it shows (a READING).**
- **The weave's three parity sectors, taken together, leave τ free.** Their joint determinant is the constant 4, by
  Jacobi's identity. That is the weave's own one-loop statement of "not forced which".
- **Every other canonical functional is extremal only at the fixed points or at the cusp.**
  - The symmetric sums and T's norm are minimized exactly at ω. AM–GM against the flat product proves it, and the
    census finds no other critical point.
  - The untwisted determinant is maximized at ω (Osgood, Phillips and Sarnak).
  - i is a saddle of each.
  - The growing ones run to the cusp.
- **So a canonical selection gives ω or the cusp.**
  - If the principle minimizes the weave's energy-like functionals, τ = ω. Main's tagged postulate then has a weave
    reading: the minimum of the three parities' symmetric determinants. But there W50's couplings are degenerate.
  - If it maximizes a partition function, τ runs to the cusp, where the couplings become hierarchical in powers of
    q^{1/8} and the count depends on the cusp's end data (W45 to W48).
  - Realistic flavour needs τ away from both, and nothing canonical supplies that. Forced to choose, not forced
    which, now with the reason: the joint determinant is flat.
- **The coupling norms point elsewhere, as a question.** If the principle extremized a coupling's strength, the
  lowest cusp-form couplings would put τ at a generic point on a reflection line (≈ 1.551i or ≈ ½ + 2.224i).
  - There the residual group on T is trivial, so the masses are non-degenerate.
  - The lines are fixed by τ ↦ −τ̄ and τ ↦ 1 − τ̄, which modular flavour models read as generalized CP. Whether the
    weave's couplings would then conserve CP is not computed here.
  - No such principle is on record, so this is named, not claimed.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; every cell as predicted); a WEAVE result. The canonical functionals fix
τ only at ω or the cusp. 0 of 19.

## W52. The free numbers counted: at the geometry's weight 11 of the 13 flavour numbers are free, and the lowest couplings are excluded by a zero-diagonal identity (`the_free_numbers_counted.py`; `W52_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- The owner's order (2026-10-09): "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers". W51 settled the first half: τ
  is free.
- The rule (`W52_RULE.md`, 670ad24e7) was committed before the code.
- **The setting.** On the ruled branch, given Λ (GENESIS FK11), with holomorphic couplings in τ alone (W50) and one
  flavour-blind Higgs per sector.
  - Each sector's Yukawa is a combination of the forms the weave fixes at its weight.
  - The parameters are τ (two reals) and each sector's coefficients, less one phase per sector:
    P = 2 + Σ (2d(k_s) − 1).
  - The count is the rank of the 13 × P Jacobian of the 13 flavour numbers: nine log masses and the CKM's four.
- **Weave or thread.** The forms are equivariant under the weave's whole group, τ is a state of the weave, and every
  weight assignment in {1, 3, 5, 7}³ is read in both frames on record: T ⊗ T, the Standard Model's fields, and Sym² T,
  E₆'s 27³. Weave-type.

**Disclosed.** In review before the run, the scan was found to seed its random points from Python's hash() of the
assignment, which changes from one process to the next. The seeds were made deterministic, by assignment index.

**The result (COMPUTED; C1 and C4 as predicted; C2 and C3 FAILED as worded).**
- **C1 (as predicted).** The coupling spaces have dimensions 1, 2, 4, 5 (T ⊗ T) and 1, 1, 3, 3 (Sym² T) at
  k = 1, 3, 5, 7.
- **C2 (FAILED).**
  - At 68 of the 128 assignments the rank fell below the predicted min(P, N_var).
  - The three random points agreed everywhere. The dropped singular values were 10⁻¹⁰ to 10⁻¹⁶ against kept ones of
    10⁻⁴ or more, so these are structural degeneracies, not noise.
  - Fact 4's "generic" was wrong. The computed ranks are the count, as the rule's last line says.
- **C3 (FAILED in one half).**
  - T ⊗ T at k = 3 for all three sectors has rank 11, as predicted.
  - Sym² T at k = 3 has rank 4, not 5, with |V| a phase matrix to 10⁻¹⁵.
- **C4 (as predicted).** The smallest P with mixing that is not a phase matrix is 7 in T ⊗ T, at (1, 3, 1) and
  (3, 1, 1), and 5 in Sym² T, at (1, 3, ·) and (3, 1, ·), with rank P there.

**Post hoc** (`the_free_numbers_counted_posthoc.py`; written after the read-out and labelled; W52's own output is
unchanged). Two degeneracies, derived by hand after the run, account for every computed rank.
- **P1, the zero-diagonal identity.**
  - Take a symmetric coupling with zero diagonal, the O₊ shape [[0, x, y], [x, 0, z], [y, z, 0]]. The invariants of
    Y^† Y satisfy t₂ = t₁²/4.
  - So the masses obey **m₃ = m₁ + m₂ exactly**. The O₊ forms at k = 1 and 3 satisfy it to 3 × 10⁻¹⁶.
  - A sector whose coupling is O₊ alone therefore has one free ratio, not two.
- **P2, the dependency pattern.** The CKM and the up and down ratios depend only on τ and the up and down sectors' own
  coefficients, and each sector's ratios only on τ and its own.
  - The generic rank is then 3 (the scales) plus the largest matching between those observables and the parameters
    they depend on, with P1 applied.
  - That model reproduces all 128 computed ranks.
- **P3, the quark sector at the geometry's weight.** With u and d both at k = 3, the quark sector's eight shape
  numbers (four mass ratios and the CKM's four) come from six parameters: τ, and each sector's complex ratio of its two
  forms. That leaves two relations.
  - The scan shows these whatever the leptons' weight: (3, 3, 5) and (3, 3, 7) also have rank 11.
  - With u or d at k ≥ 5 the relations go: (5, 3, 3) and (3, 5, 3) have rank 13.
- **Extra read-outs (no prior).** Along x = 0 the O₊ forms' singular values run from (1, 0.66, 0.34) at y = 1.2 to
  (1, 0.97, 0.03) at y = 3. Each obeys m₃ = m₁ + m₂: two nearly equal heavy masses and one light one, the opposite of
  the observed pattern. The antisymmetric form alone always has (1, 1, 0).

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts).**
- **The lowest couplings are excluded by the masses.**
  - A coupling that is O₊ alone forces m₃ = m₁ + m₂. That is the only coupling at k = 1 in both frames and at k = 3
    in E₆'s.
  - The record already met this texture on another route. `docs/THE_DESTINATION_LEDGER_2026-09-06.md`, rows 6–14
    (B1273, B1276), found that the zero-diagonal |ε_ijk| texture of its closing gives m₃ ≤ m₁ + m₂, violated ×136 for
    the up quarks, ×43 for the down quarks and ×17 for the charged leptons.
  - So every structure with such a sector is excluded: C4's smallest structures, and the E₆ frame at the geometry's
    weight. Two routes now meet the same wall.
  - The record's cure was a diagonal source. Here the wall is escaped by weight: by the antisymmetric piece O₋ at
    k = 3 (in T ⊗ T only), or by the diagonal piece D from k = 5.
- **The count at the geometry's weight (W45's T at 3/2, a weight-0 Higgs, so k = 3).**
  - In the Standard Model frame, **11 of the 13 flavour numbers are free**: τ's two reals, the three scales, and the
    up, down and lepton sectors' complex ratio of their two forms.
  - Both predicted relations sit in the quark sector: eight quark shape numbers from six parameters.
- **In the E₆ frame no structure survives below 13.** Once P1 removes the weights 1 and 3, every remaining structure
  (k ≥ 5 everywhere) leaves all 13 free.
- **The tally of the 19** (a reading on the record's typing):
  - at the geometry's weight in the Standard Model frame: 11 flavour numbers, the three gauge couplings and v and m_H
    from the reader, and θ_QCD as a bit (I-18, unearned);
  - so at most 17 of the 19 are free on the ruled branch, given Λ, if that structure fits the data. In the E₆ frame,
    all 19.
- **Why each is free.**
  - τ: the weave's joint determinant is flat (W51). The space of vacua is supplied and the point withheld, the
    record's torsor pattern (`docs/THE_FORCED_AND_THE_FREE.md`).
  - The coefficients: the weave fixes each form's shape, not its size.
  - The weights: nothing fixes the Higgs's.
  - The two relations come from the shape of the k = 3 couplings (zero diagonal, O₊ ⊕ O₋) and from τ being shared by
    the up and down sectors.
- **What decides it next.** Whether the measured quark masses and CKM satisfy those two relations is a fit (W53). If
  they do, the principle predicts two quark-sector numbers. If not, the geometry's weight is excluded, and the count
  rises to 13.
- *Added after W53:* the fit excludes the geometry's weight, by the masses alone (χ²_min = 163 at a 10% allowance),
  and post hoc the quark masses alone exclude (3, 3, 5) and (3, 3, 7) as well. No structure on record both fits and
  predicts a flavour relation, so the count is 13 if any structure fits at all.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; C1 and C4 as predicted; C2 and C3 failed as worded, both explained
post hoc by P1 and P2, 128 of 128); a WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ. 0 of 19.

## W53. The fit at the geometry's weight: excluded by the masses alone, because near the cusp the lightest mass is the same in every sector (`the_fit_at_the_geometrys_weight.py`; `W53_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_fit_at_the_geometrys_weight_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- The owner's order (2026-10-09): "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers". W52 counted: at the geometry's
  weight the Standard Model frame leaves 11 of the 13 flavour numbers free, if that structure fits the data.
- The owner chose the fit's data: common-scale masses, "Quark masses run to one scale (M_Z) from a cited table, added
  to the record with its source."
- The rule and its data (`W53_RULE.md`, `received/HZ2021_running_masses_MZ.json`, 2d3ea1842) were committed before
  the code.
- **The data.**
  - Masses: Huang and Zhou 2021 (Phys. Rev. D 103, 016010, arXiv:2009.04851v2), MS-bar at M_Z in the full Standard
    Model, Tables 2 and 3, transcribed with the PDF's sha256.
  - CKM: PDG 2025, |V_us|, |V_cb|, |V_ub| and |V_td| (W44's transcription). |V_td| stands in for J, whose sign is a
    convention.
- **The fit.** Y_s = e^{c_s}(F̂₊(τ) + ρ_s F̂₋(τ)), W52's two k = 3 forms, with 11 parameters against 13 observables.
  - The observables are compared in logs, with σ_i = √(δ_i² + a²), δ_i the datum's error and a = 0.10 the running
    allowance (0.05 and 0.20 reported).
  - The fit passes if χ²_min ≤ 5.99 (two degrees of freedom).
  - τ ranges over the fundamental domain with y ≤ 6. The search is the rule's: a grid, the sectors' own ratio fits,
    and bounded least squares from 260 starts per objective.
- **Weave or thread.** The forms are the weave's (equivariant under its whole group), τ is a state of the weave, and
  the claim is about the joint structure. Weave-type.

**Disclosed.** In review before the run, the per-sector ratio fits were found to leave log|ρ| unbounded on the
Levenberg–Marquardt path, where an overflow would crash the SVD. It was clipped at ±30. Nothing else changed after the
rule.

**The result (COMPUTED; one run; F2, F3 and F4 went the way their priors leaned; M0 and F1 FAILED as worded).**
- **F3, the fit: it fails, by orders of magnitude** (the prior was 10% that it passes).
  - χ²_min = 1887 at a = 0.10, against 5.99. At a = 0.05 it is 5957, and at a = 0.20 it is 484.
  - The best compromise, at τ ≈ 0.450 + 2.681i, gives up the up masses: ρ_u ≈ 1300 makes Y_u nearly the
    antisymmetric form, so m_c/m_t = 0.998. And |V_us| = 0.996, so the CKM is near a permutation.
- **F2 (as predicted): the masses alone cannot be fit at one τ.**
  - The masses-only χ²_min is 163, at τ ≈ −0.10 + 5.20i. Eleven parameters for nine numbers, and still no fit: the
    obstruction is structural, not a count.
    *(Corrected 2026-10-14 by main's B1637: main's optimizer reaches 20.6 near Im τ = 7.1, so 163 was an
    overestimate from a local search. The full fit's exclusion stands, and it rests on the CKM.)*
    *(W79, the same day: main's evaluator keeps about two digits near Im τ = 7.1, so the 20.6 is in doubt too.)*
    *(W80, the same day: with this seat's precise forms swapped into main's optimizer, unchanged, the masses-only
    (3, 3, 3) reaches 319, not 20.6. So 20.6 was an artifact of main's evaluator, and this seat's 163 stands,
    pending main.)*
  - Since the full χ² is at least its masses part, this alone excludes the structure.
  - At that point each sector has one ratio near κ q^{1/4} ≈ 8 × 10⁻⁴: the up sector's m_c/m_t (0.80 × 10⁻³ against
    3.7 × 10⁻³), the down sector's m_d/m_b (0.82 × 10⁻³ against 0.94 × 10⁻³) and the leptons' m_e/m_τ (0.74 × 10⁻³
    against 0.28 × 10⁻³). The largest pulls are m_c −7.6, m_t +7.3, m_e +5.6 and m_τ −4.2.
- **F4 (as predicted): the cusp's universality.** At τ = 5i, the 624 grid points with m₂/m₃ ∈ [0.005, 0.05] have
  m₁/m₃ between 1.047 and 1.149 × 10⁻³, against the rule's κ q^{1/4} = 1.07 × 10⁻³.
- **F1 (FAILED as worded).**
  - The down and lepton sectors alone are reached exactly (to 10⁻¹⁵). The down sector is reached at y ≈ 3.19 or 5.11,
    and the leptons at 2.47 or 5.91: the rule's hand values were 3.2 or 5.1, and 2.45 or 5.85.
  - The up sector alone came closest at τ ≈ −½ + 4.206i (the hand value was 4.2), with a log residual of 0.036, not
    10⁻⁶. That point sat on the search box's edge x = −½ (post hoc P4).
- **M0 (FAILED in one part).**
  - (a) to (d) held: one O₊ form and one O₋ form; ρ_T unitary to 10⁻¹⁵; the shape observables unchanged under
    τ → τ + 1 and τ → −1/τ to 3 × 10⁻¹²; the Jacobian's rank 11 at three points.
  - (e) failed. In W52's recorded scan, T ⊗ T at (3, 3, 5) and (3, 3, 7) also have rank 11, not 13.
  - The rule's claim was wrong, and the record already had the fact. W52's P3 and relay §57 say the two relations
    persist whatever the leptons' weight, since the quark sector at (3, 3, k_e) is (3, 3, 3)'s.
- **Extra read-outs (no prior).**
  - The grid's best χ² (a = 0.10) by band of y: 3079 (0.87–1.5), 2224 (1.5–2.5), 2056 (2.5–3.5), 3528 (3.5–4.5) and
    6479 (4.5–6). Masses only: 1549, 523, 231, 293 and 186. Away from the cusp the hierarchies are out of reach.
  - Starts reaching the global minimum: 2 of 260 (13 observables, a = 0.10), and 11 of 260 (masses only).
  - Consistency: the 13-observable minimum is not below the masses-only one.

**Post hoc** (`the_fit_at_the_geometrys_weight_posthoc.py`; P1 to P3 written during the run, when its progress line
showed M0 failing; P4 after the read-out, for F1; labelled; W53's own output is unchanged).
- **P1.** In W52's recorded scan, the assignments without an O₊-alone sector that fall below rank 13 are exactly
  T ⊗ T at (3, 3, k_e), k_e = 3, 5, 7, each at rank 11. All three share one quark sector.
- **P3, the quark masses alone.** Six log masses against eight parameters give χ²_min = 41.4, at τ ≈ −½ + 4.51i (21
  of 260 starts reach it). There the up sector's m_c/m_t (2.4 × 10⁻³ against 3.7 × 10⁻³) and the down sector's
  m_d/m_b (2.3 × 10⁻³ against 0.94 × 10⁻³) are pinned together, as the universality says. The hand estimate, made
  the same way as the rule's, was 41.
- **P2, the quark sector with the CKM.** χ²_min = 1873 at a = 0.10 (4383 at 0.05, 481 at 0.20). Each is below W53's
  full minimum, as it must be.
- **So (3, 3, 5) and (3, 3, 7) fail too.** Any structure (3, 3, k_e) has χ² at least its quark masses' part, which is
  at least 41.4.
- **P4, the up sector alone with x free.** Its closest approach is 0.042, at τ ≈ 0.45 + 4.20i, no better than F1's
  0.036. So F1's failure is not the box's: neither search reaches the up sector's two ratios exactly. Both miss by
  about 4% in log, well inside the 10% allowance.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts; given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **The weave's modular structure at the geometry's weight is excluded by the data, and not narrowly.** The masses
  alone miss by χ² = 163 at a 10% allowance, with more parameters than numbers. The full fit misses by 1887.
- **The reason is the shared τ.**
  - Near the cusp the k = 3 couplings put the lightest mass over the heaviest at κ q^{1/4}, the same in every sector
    whose hierarchy is set there (F4). Each sector alone is reached at its own τ (F1).
  - Away from the cusp the hierarchies are out of reach (the grid's bands).
  - The weave gives every sector one τ (W51). Each sector alone fits (the up sector to about 4%); together they
    need three different τ.
- **The count.** With P1 to P3, no structure on record both fits the data and predicts a flavour relation.
  - Every structure with a sector at weight 1 (or, in E₆'s frame, at weight 3) fails m₃ = m₁ + m₂ (W52).
  - The three at rank 11 fail here.
  - Every other one has rank 13 and predicts nothing.

  So the weave's modular structure predicts no flavour relation the data allow. Its free flavour numbers are 13, if
  some structure with u or d at weight 5 or more fits at all. The tally is then all 19 of the 19 free on the ruled
  branch, given Λ, or 18 if I-18 is earned.
- **What stays open.** Whether any structure with u or d at weight ≥ 5 fits at all, where the coefficients outnumber
  the relations. If none does, the modular structure is excluded as the Yukawas' source on the ruled branch.
- *Added after W54:* five rank-13 structures fit (all in T ⊗ T, every sector at weight 5 or 7), with 23 or more
  parameters; every structure with a sector at weight 3 fails. So the modular structure survives, and the count is 13.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule and its data first; one run; F2, F3 and F4 went the way their priors leaned; M0(e) and
F1 failed as worded; post hoc P1 to P4); a WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ. 0 of 19.

## W54. The rank-13 structures: five fit, every sector at weight 5 or 7, and none predicts anything (`the_rank_thirteen_structures.py`; `W54_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_rank_thirteen_structures_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- The owner's choice (2026-10-09), after W53 excluded the three rank-11 structures: "Test the rank-13 structures."
- The rule (`W54_RULE.md`, 0b1b37503) came before the code. The script (aa130c8ca) was committed during its one run,
  before the read-out.
- **The set.** Every structure on record not yet excluded, 32 in all: T ⊗ T with each k_s ∈ {3, 5, 7} and
  (k_u, k_d) ≠ (3, 3) (24), and Sym² T with each k_s ∈ {5, 7} (8). They have rank 13, so they predict no relation; the
  question is whether any fits at all.
- **The fit.** W53's data, observables and tolerance (a = 0.10). A sector's Yukawa is Σ α_i F̂_i over W52's basis at
  its weight. A structure fits if χ²_min ≤ 5.99.
- **The search.** At a fixed τ the quark part depends only on α_u and α_d, and the lepton part only on α_e. So:
  - a grid of 164 τ in the fundamental domain;
  - at each, the best quark part for each of 12 quark weight pairs (16 starts) and the best lepton part for each of 5
    lepton weights (8 starts);
  - then a joint refinement of each structure from its 8 best grid points.
- **Weave or thread.** The forms are the weave's, τ is a state of the weave, and the claim covers every structure in
  the set at once. Weave-type.

**The result (COMPUTED; one run; H0 and H2 as predicted; H1 and H3 FAILED as worded).**
- **H2 (as predicted): five structures fit, all in the Standard Model frame.**
  - (5, 7, 5) and (5, 7, 7) fit exactly (χ²_min ≈ 10⁻²¹), at τ ≈ 0.20 + 2.93i and 0.00 + 2.88i.
  - (7, 7, 5) fits at 0.88, and (5, 5, 5) and (5, 5, 7) at 5.33.
  - They have P = 23 to 27 real parameters for 13 numbers, so they predict nothing.
- **H3 (FAILED as worded).** (7, 7, 5) fits, but (7, 7, 7) reached 6.38, just above 5.99, and (7, 7, 3) 147. Post
  hoc P1 shows (7, 7, 7)'s value is the search's.
- **H1 (FAILED as worded).** μ₇ ≈ 10⁻¹⁶ and μ₃ = 0.50, 0.15, 0.14, as predicted. But μ₅ ≈ 10⁻¹⁶ too: the weight-5 span
  holds rank-one couplings at a generic τ, against the rule's count (post hoc P2).
- **H0 (as predicted).** The dimensions (2, 4, 5 and 3, 3); the shape observables unchanged under τ → τ + 1 and
  τ → −1/τ to 4 × 10⁻¹²; the Jacobian of (7, 5, 5) of rank 13.
- **Extra read-outs (no prior).**
  - **Every structure with any sector at weight 3 fails**, χ²_min ≥ 110: with a quark sector there, ≥ 273; with the
    leptons there, 110 to 198. The leptons at weight 3 reach at best 1.1 on the grid, and only on W53's curves.
    *(Corrected 2026-10-14 by main's B1637: with the leptons at weight 3, main fits (5, 7, 3) at χ² = 1.86 near
    τ ≈ 0.36 + 6.79i, and (3, 7, 7) reaches 6.72. Both lie near Im τ 6.7 to 6.8, which this grid did not resolve.
    W78 refines the grid there. Main also fits (7, 5, 5) and (7, 5, 7) exactly, where this search failed at 26 and
    28; this search found (5, 5, 5) and (5, 5, 7), which main's missed.)*
    *(W78, the same day: this seat's model does not reach main's weight-3 fits even at main's τ, and its up sector
    alone at weight 3 has no exact fit below Im τ = 8, where main's has one at 6.66. The two seats' weight-3 models
    differ; which is the record's is open with main.)*
    *(W79, the same day: the two models are the same. Main's forms keep only two or three digits near Im τ 6.7, so
    main's weight-3 fits there are most likely numerical artifacts, and this sentence of W54 most likely stands. Main
    is asked to re-run them.)*
    *(W80, the same day: the seat re-ran them. Main's own optimizer, with this seat's precise forms swapped in, gives
    (5, 7, 3) 356 and (3, 7, 7) 834, so the sentence stands for the 13, pending main. The up sector alone does fit
    exactly at weight 3, but at Im τ = 8.19, not at main's 6.66.)*
  - **Every Sym² T structure fails**, χ²_min between 94 and 199.
  - (7, 5, 5) and (7, 5, 7) fail at 26 and 28: u at 7 with d at 5 does not fit in this search, while u at 5 with d at
    7 fits exactly.
  - The quark pairs' best grid values: (5, 7) 5.8, (5, 5) 11, (7, 5) 30, (7, 7) 58, every pair with a 3 at least 273,
    Sym² T's 137 to 268. The leptons at 5 and 7 reach their ratios exactly on the grid.
  - No minimum sits on the box's edge y = 6.

**Post hoc** (`the_rank_thirteen_structures_posthoc.py`; written after the read-out, for the two failed cells;
labelled; W54's own output is unchanged).
- **P1, (7, 7, 7).** From (7, 7, 5)'s τ, the quark pair (7, 7) refined over τ and its coefficients (48 starts) fits
  exactly, Q = 3 × 10⁻²³, at τ ≈ 0.0006 + 1.019i. The leptons at weights 5 and 7 fit exactly there. So (7, 7, 5) and
  (7, 7, 7) both fit exactly; W54's search fell short at (7, 7, 7).
- **P2, the weight-5 rank-one couplings.**
  - At each of H1's three τ the weight-5 span holds rank-one couplings: three found from 40 starts, against six at
    weight 7.
  - One of them uses D and the first O₊ form alone, so it is symmetric: a aᵀ.
  - The rule's count treated the pieces as generic. A symmetric rank-one coupling in D ⊕ O₊ is a square, which the
    count missed. (A reading, not computed here: the weight-5 symmetric space would hold the square of a
    weight-5/2 triplet form.)

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts; given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **The count of 13 is realised.** The weave's modular structure can carry all 13 flavour numbers: in the Standard
  Model frame, with every sector at weight 5 or 7, u at 5 or both quark sectors at 7. It does so with 23 or more real
  parameters for 13 numbers, so it explains none of them.
- **The geometry's weight is excluded in every structure.** Every structure with a sector at weight 3 fails, the
  rank-11 ones by W53 and the rest here. The weight that fits is not the one W45's reading gives; it is a choice.
  *(Corrected 2026-10-14 by main's B1637: excluded as the quarks' weight only; with the leptons at 3, (5, 7, 3)
  fits on main's search. W78 checks it.)* *(Withdrawn the same day after W80: on precise forms main's own optimizer
  gives (5, 7, 3) 356, so the geometry's weight is excluded for every sector again, pending main.)*
- **The E₆ frame fails at every weight on record:** weights 1 and 3 by m₃ = m₁ + m₂ (W52), and 5 and 7 here.
- **The count, for the owner's question** ("we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers"):
  - τ: free; nothing canonical fixes it (W51), and the fits sit at non-special points.
  - The 13 flavour numbers: all free. Every structure that predicts a relation is excluded by the data (W52, W53), and
    the ones that fit predict none.
  - With the three gauge couplings, v and m_H supplied by the reader and θ_QCD a bit only if I-18 is earned: all 19 of
    the 19 free on the ruled branch, given Λ (18 if I-18 is earned).
- **What could change it.** A principle that fixes the weights, τ or the coefficients; weights above 7, untested here,
  only add coefficients.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; H0 and H2 as predicted; H1 and H3 failed as worded, both explained post
hoc); a WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ. 0 of 19.

## Reading W49–W54 together (READING; the owner asked to stop and think before the next arc, 2026-10-13)

Nothing here is computed. Each point cites the arc it rests on. Weave or thread: the points are about the joint
structure (W49 to W54 are weave results); the tick's geodesic in point 4 is one thread's, the lowest closed geodesic,
and is labelled so.

1. **The weave fixes shapes, not sizes.**
   - Forced: three, alike (W1–W4); the triplet T, the three even theta constants (W50); chirality under the weave's
     group (W21, W22); the coupling forms by piece and weight, with a zero diagonal at the lowest weights (W50, W52).
   - Free: every number tried. τ (W51), the weights (W52), the coefficients (W54); earlier the hand (W24–W29), the
     cusp's unit (W47, GENESIS FK10) and the dictionary Λ (GENESIS FK11).
   - Each time a freedom was removed it reappeared elsewhere. That is the record's torsor pattern: the space of vacua
     supplied, the point withheld (`docs/THE_FORCED_AND_THE_FREE.md`).
2. **Where the weave is most itself, the data reject it.**
   - Its group alone gives degenerate masses (W38). Its fixed points i and ω give degenerate pairs (W50). With the
     inner automorphisms, mixing is a permutation (main's B1620). Its own weight with one τ gives one hierarchy for
     every sector (W53).
   - It fits only with enough coefficients to predict nothing: 23 or more for 13 numbers (W54).
   - So far, the structures that predict are wrong, and the ones that fit predict nothing. The world sits far from
     every symmetric point the weave offers.
3. **The failure that teaches: the sectors are not told apart.**
   - At weight 3, all fields in T (Λ) and one τ (W51) give every sector the same light-to-heavy ratio, κ q^{1/4}
     (W53's F4).
   - The data give up quarks, down quarks and leptons different hierarchies, each wanting its own τ (W53's F1).
   - The fits that work give the sectors different weights (W54), and nothing in the weave supplies them.
   - So what tells the sectors apart is not in the weave. It has to come from the gauge side or from the dictionary
     (GENESIS FK11). The next arc asks whether the gauge side on record does.
4. **The weave's numbers come as identities, not as fits.**
   - Jacobi's e₃ = 4 (W51); m₃ = m₁ + m₂ for the symmetric zero-diagonal coupling (W52); a symmetric rank-one
     square at weight 5 (W54, post hoc); main's golden spectrum 1 : φ⁻¹ : φ⁻² on the principle's own closed geodesic
     (B1636, a thread's locus).
   - These are exact and forced. None matches a measured number yet; the second is broken by the data ×17 to ×136.
   - If the principle forces a number, it will most likely show as such an identity, at a place the principle itself
     marks, not at a fit's best point.
5. **On method.** The hand counts assumed generic forms and failed in three arcs running (W52's C2, W53's M0(e),
   W54's H1). Each time the weave was special exactly where a generic count misses it, and those special places are
   where the identities of point 4 live.
6. **What it means for the next step.**
   - "What fixes τ, the weights and the coefficients" inside the weave is W51's question, answered no: the canonical
     selectors pick ω or the cusp, and the data reject both.
   - Two sharper questions remain: (a) does the gauge side on record tell the sectors apart (the owner's choice,
     2026-10-13, next); (b) where does the principle mark a point (the tick's geodesic and its identity).

## W55. Does the gauge side on record tell the sectors apart? No: it ties the down quarks to the charged leptons, which the data exclude, or splits them only with new free numbers (`the_gauge_side_and_the_sectors.py`; `W55_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- The owner's choice (2026-10-13), after "Reading W49–W54 together": (a), does the gauge side on record tell the
  sectors apart? The reading's point 3 found that nothing in the weave does.
- The rule (`W55_RULE.md`, 397e92bfc) came before the code.
- **The frames.**
  - F-MC's E₆ cubic: THE_CLAIM §1, matter mass in the cubic 27³ ⊃ 16·16·10, with the doublet in the 10 ⊂ 27.
  - SO(10)'s 16·16·10 and minimal SU(5), both inside it.
  - The Standard Model frame of W52 to W54.
- **Weave or thread.** The claims cover every structure each frame allows, at every weight and every τ; the frames are
  the record's, cited. Tags: on the ruled branch; given Λ (GENESIS FK11), every matter field in T at 3/2 (W45).

**Disclosed.** After the run, two docstring lines were rewrapped to the line limit. The script was rerun and its output
is byte-identical.

**The result (COMPUTED; one run; every cell as predicted).**
- **K1, the tie's bound.** A structure that gives the down quarks and the charged leptons equal in-sector ratios cannot
  fit their six masses.
  - The exact minimum over the shared ratios and both scales is χ² = 106 at a = 0.10 (the rule's hand estimate was
    about 60, since the two ratios share the heaviest mass's residual).
  - It is 249 at a = 0.05 and 32 at a = 0.20.
- **K4.** The bound reaches 5.99 only at a running allowance of a* = 0.48: the tie would need a 48% allowance.
- **K2, the Kronecker lemma.** With one flavour matrix λ serving every coupling, the down-type mass matrix on
  (d, D) × (d^c, D^c) is P ⊗ λ, and the charged leptons' is Pᵀ ⊗ λ. Their singular values are the products σ_a(P)·σ_i(λ),
  to 1.3 × 10⁻¹⁴ on 400 random cases. So the light down quarks and charged leptons both carry λ's ratios.
- **K3, the Standard Model frame's tie.** With every matter field at 3/2, the down quarks and leptons share H_d, so
  k_d = k_e. Of the fitting structures (W54 with its post hoc), exactly (5, 5, 5), (5, 7, 7) and (7, 7, 7) satisfy
  it, with P = 23, 27 and 29.

**Scope, stated more exactly than the rule did.** The lemma needs one flavour matrix for every coupling. That holds
when every vacuum value sits in one flavour-blind 27, or in several whose couplings are proportional. Each further
Higgs 27 with its own coupling, and each flavour direction in T, can split the sectors, at the price of new free
numbers.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts; given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **The answer to (a) is no.** The gauge side on record does one of two things:
  - **it ties the sectors, and the data exclude the tie.** In F-MC's E₆ cubic with one flavour matrix (the rank-closing
    ⟨1⟩ and ⟨ν^c⟩ included), in SO(10) with one 10_H and in minimal SU(5), the down quarks and charged leptons share
    their ratios: χ² ≥ 106. With one flavour-blind 27 for both doublets, the up quarks share them too and V is a phase
    matrix;
  - **or it splits them only through the Higgs, with new free numbers**: further Higgs 27s with their own couplings,
    flavour directions in T, or, in the Standard Model frame, the doublets' weights, where the tie k_d = k_e leaves
    three fitting structures with 23 or more parameters.
- **So nothing on record tells the sectors apart in a way that fixes a number.** The count stays W54's: all 19 of the
  19 free on the ruled branch, given Λ (18 if I-18 is earned).
- **What it locates.** What tells the sectors apart is the Higgs sector's flavour structure: where the Higgs sits
  relative to T, with what coupling and what weight. That is a dictionary datum (GENESIS FK11), and neither the weave
  nor F-MC supplies it.
- **For F-MC's own frame.** "Matter mass in the cubic" (THE_CLAIM §1) with one flavour matrix meets the classic
  down–lepton mismatch of unified theories and is excluded by it. It survives only with Higgs fields that carry flavour
  or their own couplings (W39 named the 351′), which is new input.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; every cell as predicted); a WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ.
0 of 19.

**Added after W56 (a correction to this section's scope remark and reading; W55's cells and its answer stand).**
- The sentence "Each further Higgs 27 with its own coupling, and each flavour direction in T, can split the sectors"
  holds in the Standard Model frame only, and there only through free coefficients.
- In F-MC's E₆ cubic the 6 × 6 down-type and charged-lepton matrices have equal spectra for any Higgs content in 27s,
  any flavour structure and any neutral vacuum values (W56's K1, exact). So in that frame no 27 can split them.
- "It survives only with Higgs fields that carry flavour or their own couplings" should read: it survives only with
  Higgs content beyond the 27, such as W39's 351′.
- W55's answer to (a) is strengthened, not changed: nothing on record tells the sectors apart in a way that fixes a
  number.

## Reading: the seed and the field (READING; the owner's framing, 2026-10-13)

Nothing here is computed. It reads W49–W55 in the owner's own terms, which the owner asked to have recorded.

**The owner's framing (2026-10-13).** "My initial idea was that existence is an interaction between a seed (principle)
and a wave, and the interaction between gives reality. Similar principle as nature/biology. Seed and field."
- This second term is not the one clarified on 2026-10-07, when the owner's "wave" was read as "weave"
  (`SM_TO_CC_AND_CODEX_2026-10-07_THE_WEAVES_INSTRUMENT_AND_LAWS.md` §1). Here the owner names a field as the seed's
  counterpart.

**The mapping (a reading, not a result).**
- **The seed is the principle:** the grammar (the moves L and R) and the act that generates (GENESIS PF1–PF3).
- **A thread is not the seed.** It is one growth of it: one object the principle allows, one closed path of the moves
  (THE WEAVE rule).
- **The weave is the seed's full expression:** every allowed move acting together on the shared ground, the two
  records.
- **The field, in the owner's sense, is not yet an object of the record.** Two things in W49–W55 behave as a field's
  state would:
  - τ, the shape of the shared ground (the two records' torus). The principle leaves it open, and W51 found the seed
    indifferent to it: the weave's joint determinant over its three parities is exactly 4 for every τ (Jacobi).
  - The Higgs field's value and its couplings to the generations. W55 found that these decide what tells the sectors
    apart, and nothing on record sets them.

**What W49–W55 say in these terms.**
- **The seed fixes the form, not the sizes.** Three generations, their symmetry and the coupling shapes are fixed;
  every size is left open. As a seed fixes a body plan and the soil and light set how the plant grows: genotype and
  environment make the phenotype.
- **The 19 numbers are where the field's state enters.** On the owner's view that is expected, not a failure. Reality
  comes from the interaction, and so far only the seed side has been studied: exhaustively, in W49–W55, with one
  consistent answer.
- **What the record calls the torsor pattern** ("the space of vacua supplied, the point withheld",
  `docs/THE_FORCED_AND_THE_FREE.md`) reads here as the seed supplying the space and the field supplying the point.

**What it changes.**
- **"Free" is reframed.** The 19 are not missing from the principle; they belong to the field's side of an interaction
  the record has not yet built.
- **The next question gets a precise form.** What is the field, and what does its interaction with the seed select? In
  GENESIS's terms that is the selection of the vacuum point (GENESIS FK9: what picks τ and the Higgs state), and
  perhaps the act and its register (GENESIS FK12).
- **(b) gets a role.** The tick's geodesic is where the seed itself marks a place in τ's space, with exact identities
  there (main's B1636). If seed and field meet anywhere, a place the seed marks is a natural first candidate.

**The caution.** This becomes physics only if the field is specified too, ideally by the same principle, and its
interaction with the seed then selects the numbers. A field equal to "whatever the measurements say" leaves the 19 free
and the analogy a description.

**Weave or thread.** The seed, the field and their interaction are weave notions: a field compatible with the seed has
to be shared by every thread, as the two records are. A field attached to one thread would be a thread result.

## W56. The Higgs carrying the generation index: it cannot copy a generation, and as the dual triplet it rescues the geometry's weight but predicts nothing; no Higgs in 27s tells the down quarks from the charged leptons (`the_higgs_and_the_generation_index.py`; `W56_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **The owner's request (2026-10-13).** Look for a field compatible with the seed, by the same minimality ("Reading:
  the seed and the field"). The owner shared notes of 2025 for this; they were read at the owner's request and are not
  banked. Then: "contemplate once more and do as you recommend".
- **The contemplation, recorded in the rule.**
  - 15 of the 19 numbers are couplings of one field, the Higgs: the 13 flavour numbers and the 2 of its potential.
  - W52 to W55 kept the Higgs flavour-blind, and W55 located the missing datum in its place relative to T.
  - So the first field to test is the Higgs, placed in each way the seed itself allows.
- **The standard a candidate field must meet** is written in the rule: nothing added; shared by every thread; acted on
  by the seed's moves; its interaction picks τ and the Higgs state (GENESIS FK9); a testable number.
- **The order.** The rule (`W56_RULE.md`, d8599653d) came before the code. The script (d1367ed81) was committed before
  its one run, and the output (24915c6e0) before this record.

**Disclosed.**
- **The pre-run review** removed a dead line and added a guard for an empty coupling space. The guard did not fire.
- **A convention read before the rule:** ρ_T(S)² = i·1, from W50's matrices. The parity predictions follow from it by
  hand, and the output stores it.

**The result (COMPUTED; one run; every cell as predicted).**
- **K1, the E₆ identity.**
  - **The setting.** The explicit trinification cubic, I₃ = det Q_L + det Q_R + det L − tr(Q_L L Q_Rᵀ), polarised. 200
    random cases, each with one, two or three Higgs 27s, random flavour tensors and random vacuum values in all five
    neutral components of L.
  - **The identity holds.** The charged leptons' six singular values are the down-type ones times c, to 6 × 10⁻¹⁴. c = 1,
    the same to 6 × 10⁻¹⁴ in every case.
  - **The control breaks it, as it must.** Multiplying one neutral component by −3 in the lepton block only breaks the
    identity in every case; the smallest spread is 0.17.
  - **A check of the construction.** The cubic's charge-violating entries vanish, to 10⁻¹⁶.
- **K2, the parity and the dimensions.** The holomorphic coupling forms by weight:

| k | 1/2 | 3/2 | 5/2 | 7/2 | 9/2 | 11/2 | 13/2 | 15/2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R_G = (conj ρ_T)^⊗3: the Higgs like a generation | 0 | 1 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 10 | 0 | 14 |
| R_D = conj ρ_T ⊗ conj ρ_T ⊗ ρ_T: the Higgs in the dual triplet | 0 | 0 | 3 | 0 | 8 | 0 | 12 | 0 |

  - **The parity holds exactly.** Every form sits on the predicted parity.
  - **n = 8 at 9/2.** The hand estimate was about 10, less its usual overshoot.
  - **The control** gives T ⊗ T at k = 3, 5 and 7 as 2, 4 and 5, W52's values.
- **K3, the census at the geometry's weight** (Standard Model frame; W53's 13 observables at a = 0.10):

| (H_u, H_d) | P | χ²_min | τ at the minimum |
|---|---|---|---|
| (B, B): W53, recorded | 11 | 1887 | — |
| (D, D) | 55 | 10⁻²⁴ (exact) | 0.200 + 3.152i |
| (B, D) | 39 | 10⁻²³ (exact) | 0.209 + 4.229i |
| (D, B) | 27 | 27.7 | 0.001 + 5.496i |

  - **(D, B) fails on d and e, as predicted.** Its pulls are d −3.0, b +2.4, e +2.4 and τ −2.6; every quark pull and
    the CKM are zero. Both sectors are flavour-blind at k = 3 on one τ, and they want different hierarchies.
  - **The box was not the limit.** No minimum sits on its edge.
- **K4, the count.** Rank 13 at all nine points, with the 13th singular value over the largest between 0.003 and 0.05.
  None of the structures predicts a flavour number.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts; given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **The Higgs cannot be a fourth copy of a generation.** At the generations' weight the coupling has no form at all.
  The multiplier's parity forbids it (K2).
- **The Higgs's place was what the geometry's weight lacked.**
  - With a flavour-blind Higgs the geometry's weight fails (W53).
  - Once H_d carries the dual-triplet index it fits exactly, whether H_u is flavour-blind or dual.
  - The piece W53's structure missed is the d–e split: (D, B), where only H_u carries the index, still fails on d and
    e.
- **But it predicts nothing.**
  - P = 39 or 55 for 13 numbers, at rank 13.
  - The freedom sits in the couplings: 8 complex coefficients for each dual-triplet sector, against 4 reals for the
    Higgs's direction.
  - So a field that only selects the direction cannot make this predictive. A principle that fixes the couplings
    themselves is needed.
  - The count stays all 19 of the 19 free on the ruled branch, given Λ (18 if I-18 is earned).
- **The d–e wall.**
  - **The Standard Model frame.** Under Λ the down quarks and the charged leptons transform alike and share H_d. So only
    their free coefficients split them, in every placement.
  - **F-MC's E₆ cubic: no Higgs content in 27s splits them**, because their spectra are equal exactly (K1, c = 1).
    - This extends main's B1340 to the whole 27, with its vectorlike d–D and L–L′ mixing, any number of 27s and any
      vacuum values. B1340 found M_d = M_eᵀ from 10·5̄·5̄_H in SU(5), and said "separating them needs a Georgi-Jarlskog
      Higgs sector the record does not supply". The record's `docs/GUT_REQUIREMENTS_LEDGER.md` lists the mass
      relations as "partial".
    - The literature states the same for E₆. Babu, Bajc and Susič (JHEP 05 (2015) 108, arXiv:1504.00904) call it "the
      unwanted mass relation M_D^T = M_E" when the 45-type doublet of a 351′ has no vacuum value.
    - W55's scope remark is corrected in its section.
  - **The data's pattern** is Georgi–Jarlskog-like (W55: ×3.1 and ×3.4).
    - The seat's literature research (2026-10-13) found the latest GUT-scale fit's double ratio
      (y_μ y_d)/(y_s y_e) = 10.58 ± 0.11 (MSSM; Antusch, Hinze and Saad, arXiv:2510.01312). Georgi and Jarlskog's
      factors give 9, and the best alternative pair of group factors, 10.125, the authors put above 4σ.
    - Read so, the data ask for a continuous splitting rather than one group-theory factor. That is literature, not
      computed here.
- **What it means for the field.** The field the data ask for must do two things. It must fix the couplings, not only
  a direction. And it must carry the quark–lepton distinction that the seed and its frames keep symmetric. No Higgs
  placement the seed offers at the geometry's weight does either.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; every cell as predicted); a WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ.
0 of 19.

**Added after W57 (a correction to W56's rule; the owner approved it on 2026-10-13).**
- **The rule's third leg of the d–e wall** says: "The moves fix a flat U(1) holonomy (y_a, y_b) on the shared fibre only
  when it is trivial … So no Wilson line on the fibre can split quarks from leptons either." The first sentence is right
  (L forces y_a = 1 and R forces y_b = 1). The conclusion is wrong.
- **Why it is wrong.** Invariance under the moves is the test for being *forced*, not for being *allowed*. τ is not
  invariant either: it is the field's state, a point of the moduli space and not a fixed point of the moves.
  - By the same standard a flat Wilson line on the fibre is an allowed state of the field. It is a point of U(1)² taken
    modulo the moves.
  - Together with τ it is the elliptic variable z = β − ατ of the fibre's Jacobi forms, the theta functions the record's
    zero modes are built from (W21, W41).
  - What the moves forbid is a *forced* Wilson line, the question W31 asked.
- **What it reopens.**
  - A Wilson line along a direction that tells quarks from leptons shifts their zero modes on the fibre differently: the
    hypercharge, or B − L in span(Y, χ) (THE_CLAIM §1). Such a line commutes with the Standard Model and breaks only the
    unified relations.
  - So it could split the down quarks from the charged leptons continuously. That is the kind of splitting the data's
    double ratio asks for, about 10.5 rather than Georgi and Jarlskog's 9.
  - The literature's version is F-theory's hypercharge flux, which leaves the holomorphic couplings unchanged and
    changes the wavefunctions (the seat's research, 2026-10-13).
  - Not computed here. The seat's planned arcs C1 (couplings as overlaps) and D1 (the one-loop potential on τ and the
    Wilson line) take it up.
- **The d–e wall, restated.** Nothing the record *forces* tells the down quarks from the charged leptons. What can is:
  - free coefficients, in the Standard Model frame;
  - Higgs content beyond the 27, in F-MC's frame;
  - a state of the field on the shared fibre, such as a Wilson line, which is allowed and not forced.

**Added after W57 (a target, recorded for C1; the owner approved it on 2026-10-13).**
- **The double ratio** (y_μ y_d)/(y_s y_e) is a number the d–e splitting must reproduce. It is free of the overall
  scales of the down and lepton sectors, and its source calls it "highly stable under RG running and threshold
  corrections".
- **The value.** 10.58 ± 0.11 from the PDG 2024 inputs (Antusch, Hinze and Saad 2026, eq. 4.7, read by the seat from
  the PDF).
  - Georgi and Jarlskog's (1/3, 3) gives 9, excluded.
  - The best rational pair of single-operator coefficients, (4/9, 9/2), gives 10.125. The source puts it more than
    4σ off.
- **The record's own value**, by arithmetic from W53's data (`received/HZ2021_running_masses_MZ.json`, PDG 2020
  inputs): 10.58 ± 1.19. It agrees, with a wider error, since m_s and m_d are less certain there.
- **The file.** `received/AHS2026_double_ratio.json` holds the printed equations, the paper's sha256 and both values.
- **Its use.** It is C1's first target: any Wilson line or overlap that splits d from e must give it. A target is
  data, not a result. 0 of 19.

## Reading: the seed of minimality and the field of maximality (READING; the owner's framing, 2026-10-13)

Nothing here is computed. The owner asked to have it recorded and agreed that a typicality test should follow, rule
first (W57).

**The owner's words (2026-10-13).** "Could it be that the field is the opposite maybe, a field of maximality compared to
the seed of minimality. A sea of potential where anything is allowed."

**Where physics already works this way.**
- **The sum over histories.** Quantum theory adds every possible history with a phase e^{iS}. Almost all of it cancels,
  and what cannot cancel is the classical world. A maximal sea plus a cancellation that does not complete is close to
  the principle's own wording (GENESIS PF1, "existence as a frustrated cancellation").
- **Every coupling the symmetries allow.** The Standard Model has as few fields as possible but every coupling its
  symmetries allow, each with any value. That is why the 13 flavour numbers are free. Minimal fields with maximal
  couplings is the working rule of field theory. The seat's literature research (2026-10-13) found it stated as the
  "most general renormalizable" potential and Weinberg's effective-field-theory reading.
- **Anarchy** (Hall, Murayama and Weiner, 2000). Couplings are drawn from the most uninformative invariant measure. It
  predicted large neutrino mixing, which was then found. It fails for the quarks, whose hierarchies are not typical of
  it.

**Where the record already holds it.**
- **GENESIS PF2:** the remainder is "a description that cannot be exhausted".
- **The weave rule** takes every thread the grammar allows, none hand-picked: two letters generate infinitely many
  threads.
- So the seed is minimal in its rules and maximal in what it allows. The owner's proposal carries the same maximality to
  the field's states.

**What W49–W56 say in these terms.**
- **The fits use all the freedom there is.** The structures that fit have rank 13 and no flavour relation (W54). A Higgs
  carrying the generation index brings eight couplings per sector (W56).
- **The world sits far from every special point the seed offers** (Reading W49–W54, point 2): i, ω, the cusp, every
  symmetric texture. A selected point would be special; a draw from a sea would look like this.
- **But the data are not structureless.** They show hierarchies of 10⁻⁵, a d–e pattern like Georgi and Jarlskog's, and
  |V_us| ≈ √(m_d/m_s).
- **So the honest form is a division of labour.** The seed fixes the shapes and the exponents, exactly. The field
  supplies the rest maximally, and that part is statistical.

**What it changes.**
- **The interaction may select a measure, not a point.** The fourth item of W56's standard ("picks τ and the Higgs
  state") becomes "picks τ and the Higgs state, or a measure on them".
- **"Deriving the 19" can mean deriving their distribution** and showing that the observed values are typical under it.
- **Nothing need be added.** The seed supplies its own measures:
  - the hyperbolic measure on τ's space, the only one its moves keep;
  - at each τ, the unitarily invariant Gaussian on the couplings the seed allows there.

**The caution.**
- "Anything is allowed" predicts nothing unless the measure is the seed's own and the test is stated before it is run,
  against a null without the seed's structure (anarchy). Otherwise it is the observer-selection move that the reviews
  of the owner's 2025 notes warned against.
- **The owner's further question (2026-10-13):** how can one be sure the observed values are the right target, if
  observing affects what is observed? Three guards answer it, and the test builds them in:
  - **Lawful dependence on the scale.** Compare dimensionless ratios at a stated scale, and repeat at a second scale.
  - **The analyst's own bias.** The rule comes first and there is one run.
  - **Observer selection.** Report a version conditioned on a world that can hold observers, labelled and separate. It
    is never used to rescue the main test.

**Weave or thread.** The measures are the weave's, invariant under its whole group, and the claim concerns every state
at once. A test of it is weave-type.

## W57. The field of maximality: the world is not a typical draw from the seed's own measure, though the seed makes hierarchy common where anarchy never does (`the_field_of_maximality.py`; `W57_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_field_of_maximality_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- **The owner's framing (2026-10-13):** a field of maximality beside the seed of minimality, "a sea of potential where
  anything is allowed". It is recorded in the reading above, and the owner agreed to a typicality test.
- **The question.** Is the world a typical draw from the seed's own invariant measure?
  - The measure: W56's four Higgs placements at the geometry's weight, τ hyperbolic over the whole domain, and the
    invariant Gaussian on the couplings the seed allows at τ.
  - The null: anarchy.
- **The order.** The rule (`W57_RULE.md`) and the second-scale data (`received/HZ2021_running_masses_1e12.json`, the
  same paper and PDF as W53's) came first, in 9d1c308d2. The script (74baa58e2) was committed before its one run, and
  the output (ae4648ba8) before this record.

**Disclosed.**
- **The pre-run review** made three changes:
  - the floors were made safe against NaN;
  - a flag was written as a plain bool;
  - draws so deep in the cusp that a form underflows are set to the rule's floor. These are 0.07% of the seed's draws.
- **After the run,** the post-hoc script's docstring was rewrapped to the line limit. It was rerun, and its output is
  byte-identical.

**The result (COMPUTED; one run; T4 as predicted; T1, T2 and T3 FAILED as worded).**
- **The controls.**
  - The vectorised k = 3 forms equal W53's.
  - The fraction of τ with y > 6 is 0.1594, against the measure's 3/(6π) = 0.1592.
  - Anarchy's mean \|V_us\|² is 0.3334, against Haar's 1/3.
- **The ladder.**
  - No draw of either model came within 32σ of the data in all ten numbers at once, in 10⁶ draws each.
  - At 64σ, the first rung with 20 acceptances, both accept about 40% (421 470 and 406 894).
- **T1 FAILED as worded.** R = 1.04 at 64σ, a rung with no resolving power.
- **T2 FAILED as worded.** p = 0.18 at the same rung. The data's count is 421 470; the pseudo-data's median is 841 068.
- **T3 FAILED.** The two-sided marginals under the seed's measure:

| | u | c | d | s | e | μ | V_us | V_cb | V_ub | V_td |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| p | 0.118 | 0.0015 | 0.189 | 0.0016 | 0.164 | 0.004 | 0.507 | 0.099 | 0.197 | 0.217 |

  The first-generation ratios and the CKM are typical. The three second-generation ratios are not.
- **T4 held.** Anarchy's two-sided p for log(m_u/m_t) is 2 × 10⁻⁶: no anarchy draw is as hierarchical.
- **The read-outs (no prior).**
  - **The four placements.** Each alone looks the same at 64σ: R 0.89 to 1.40, and p 0.34, 0.085, 0.18 and 0.005 for
    (B, B), (B, D), (D, B) and (D, D). In a dual-triplet sector, no unfloored draw has a mass ratio as small as the
    data's.
  - **The second scale (10¹² GeV)** gives the same picture: R 1.03, p 0.18, and marginals within 0.003 of these.
  - **The observer-conditioned version** keeps 63 656 seed draws (6.4%) and no anarchy draw, so it cannot be compared.

**Post hoc (labelled; written after the read-out; the same draws regenerated from W57's seeds; no priors).**
- **P1, the nearest draw.** The data's nearest seed draw is 36.5σ away; the worst observables there are m_c/m_t (36.5)
  and m_u/m_t (34.7). The nearest anarchy draw is 42.4σ away. By placement: (B, B) 37.2, (B, D) 38.5, (D, B) 36.5,
  (D, D) 43.7.
- **P2, typicality without a box.** A typical draw's nearest other draw is 0.8σ to 6.3σ away (median 2.0). The data's is
  36.5σ, beyond all 200 pseudo-data: p_nn = 0.005, the test's floor.
- **P3, among the hierarchical draws** (every m₁/m₃ < 10⁻²):
  - the first-generation ratios become typical (p 0.50 to 0.94);
  - the second-generation ratios stay low (0.023, 0.024, 0.037);
  - so do V_us, V_ub and V_td (0.004 to 0.005).
- **P4, the shares.**
  - **All three m₁/m₃ below 10⁻²:** 6.4% of the seed's draws, 25.1% of (B, B)'s, about 0.1% of each placement with a
    dual-triplet sector, and none of anarchy's.
  - **All six mass ratios within a factor 10 of the data's:** none, in every model.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts and the labelled post hoc; given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **The owner's hypothesis in its simplest form is excluded.** The observed flavour pattern is not a typical draw from
  the seed's own measure:
  - it lies farther from every draw than any typical draw does;
  - no draw matches the six mass ratios even within a factor 10;
  - the second-generation ratios are atypical one by one.
  The rule's reading applies: something selects within the sea.
- **But the seed's structure does carry hierarchy.**
  - Its flavour-blind sectors make a world with three deep first-generation hierarchies common (25%). Anarchy never
    makes one (0 of 10⁶).
  - The first-generation ratios and the CKM are typical of the seed's measure, and the up quarks' hierarchy is not
    typical of anarchy.
  - So the first step of the hierarchy is the seed's own: the cusp's exponents.
- **What is not the seed's is the second generation and the small mixings.** This is W53's F4 again: m₂/m₃ in the
  observed range needs special coefficients. It matches W56, which found the freedom in the couplings. The selection
  the data ask for acts on the coefficients, not on τ alone.
- **The observer's selection does not explain it.** Among worlds that can hold observers, the second-generation ratios
  and V_us, V_ub and V_td stay atypical. By the rule this could not have rescued T2, and it does not.
- **On method.** The joint box test was underpowered. The hand estimate of the seed's acceptance was wrong by orders of
  magnitude, because ten numbers at once are a needle for both models. The nearest-neighbour statistic of the post hoc
  is the one to state in advance next time.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; T4 as predicted; T1, T2 and T3 failed as worded, explained post hoc and
labelled); a WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ. 0 of 19.

## W58. The level-four preprint checked: its triplet is the record's T, its generations are not the record's three, its numbers stand, its significance does not (`the_level_four_preprint_checked.py`; `W58_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_level_four_preprint_checked_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- Step A3 of the plan the owner approved on 2026-10-13: check a level-four modular-symmetry preprint with the record's
  own code (Barger, arXiv:2609.09262v1).
- The preprint reads the quark and lepton masses as charges on a ℤ₄ clock. It puts the generations in an S₄ triplet,
  fixes ε = 14/75 and τ = 1.0685 i from the charged leptons, and claims the six quark masses and the CKM matrix "with
  no continuous parameter".
- The record's T is an S₄ triplet twisted by a character, built from the even theta constants (W21, W38, W50). So the
  two had to be compared, and the numbers checked (B1517).

**The order.**
- **The rule first.** The rule (`W58_RULE.md`) and the transcribed claims (`received/B2026_level_four_claims.json`, with
  the PDF's sha256) were committed before any code (c5f343d14). What the seat had seen before writing the rule is
  listed there, with the priors it touched.
- **The script.** Committed before its one run (c5cb9b4f2). The pre-run review fixes are disclosed in that commit.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread (89d00b7a7).
- **A labelled post hoc** script, committed before its own run, traces the two cells that failed as worded.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; five cells as predicted, K4 and K7 failed as worded).**
- **K1, the triplet (as predicted).** η's multiplier comes out at −1/8 and 1/24 of a turn at S and T. Of its 24 powers,
  exactly k = 3 and k = 15 untwist ρ_T to a representation of Γ₄ ≅ S₄. Both are irreducible.
  - k = 3 gives T-charges {0, 1, 3} and traces −1, 1, 0 at S, T and ST: the cube's rotations, the preprint's 3.
  - k = 15 gives T-charges {1, 2, 3}: its 3′.
  - So the record's T is the preprint's triplet, up to η³'s multiplier.
- **K2, the generations' lines (as predicted).**
  - The preprint's third generation (T-charge 0) is a parity line: the one carrying θ₂².
  - Its first and second generations are (e₁ ± ζe₃)/√2, mixtures of the parity lines carrying θ₄² and θ₃². The slot
    pairing agrees with W50's.
  - In its basis the S-move has moduli ((½, ½, 1/√2), (½, ½, 1/√2), (1/√2, 1/√2, 0)). So the moves mix its
    generations, where they permute the record's.
- **K3, the selection theorem (as predicted).** Scanning 3 ≤ N ≤ 12, a solution exists only at N = 4: a = (0, 2, 3),
  with c = 2 or c = 3. Only c = 2 is a shift by a character of S₄ (1′). The theorem's "fixes the charges" needs that
  reading of its "one group element".
- **K4, the numbers (failed as worded).** 39 of the 41 printed claims reproduce to the printed digit. The two theta
  routes agree exactly. The two that do not:
  - m_c: 0.6242 GeV against the printed 0.6239;
  - |V_td|: 0.008600 against the printed 0.008599.
  - The misses are 0.05% and 0.015%, and neither changes any comparison.
- **K5, the double ratio (as predicted).**
  - 2/ε = 10.714 lies −1.2σ from the target 10.58 ± 0.11, and −0.1σ from the record's own value.
  - The Georgi–Jarlskog plaquette (2/(ε Im τ))^{1/2} = 3.167 lies −0.08σ from the record's 3.144 ± 0.274 at M_Z.
- **K6, the look-elsewhere (as predicted).**
  - Table XIV reproduces on both dictionaries. For example, the anchor gives 0.24% and 7.99% against the printed 0.2%
    and 8%, and P1 gives 6.4% and 87.2% against 6% and 87%.
  - The anchor's own dressing (2θ₃)⁴ lies outside the paper's form-value box. On the smallest box that holds it (510
    prefactors), a random number lands within 0.02% of some element 24% of the time.
  - On the families the postulates were drawn from, the joint chance of the four landings is 16%. The text's 10⁻⁶
    (6 × 10⁻⁷ here) is the declared family's.
- **K7, the record's own data (failed as worded).**
  - At M_Z all six quark relations hold within 0.4σ on Huang and Zhou's values, a data set independent of the
    preprint's.
  - The lepton anchor, with ε re-derived from those leptons, misses by 2.7σ at the data ratio's error.
  - At 10¹² GeV, P1 fails by 107σ and the double ratio holds (−0.14σ), as predicted.

**Post hoc (labelled; written after the read-out; W58's JSON unchanged).**
- **PH1, K4's two misses, traced.**
  - The printed m_c is computed from the measured m_t (169.85 GeV) rather than the predicted one (169.92): it gives
    0.62393.
  - The printed |V_td| is computed from the rounded |V_ub| and θ₁₃/θ₂₃: it gives 0.0085995.
- **PH2, the anchor's precision.** K7's miss is the fixing of ε.
  - **With ε = 14/75** the anchor holds on both data sets: −0.05σ on the preprint's Table I and −0.25σ on the record's
    data, at the data ratio's error.
  - **With ε taken from the data** by the preprint's own Eq. 23, it misses by 2.0σ on its own Table I and by 2.7σ on
    the record's data.
  - **Why.** The anchor depends on ε about four times as strongly as ε depends on the masses (dT/d ln m = 2.68, −6.36
    and 3.68 for e, μ and τ). So its honest precision is the error carried through ε: 0.145% on the preprint's Table I
    and 0.29% on the record's data, not 0.02%. At that precision it holds (−0.37σ and −0.26σ).
  - **So** the preprint's 0.02% holds only for the rational ε = 14/75, which it read off the same data as a continued-
    fraction convergent. Its statement that every result is unchanged at the 10⁻⁴ level with the measured ε does not
    hold for the anchors: T moves by 5 × 10⁻⁴.
- **PH3, the anchor's coverage at its honest precision:** 1.7% on the declared family, 43% on the form-value family and
  91% on the anchor's own box.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts and the labelled post hoc).**
- **The structure is shared.** A route that starts from the masses lands on the record's T, the same level-four triplet
  up to η³'s multiplier, with couplings from the theta constants of the same torus. The record reached T from the
  weave (W21), with no data. The convergence is worth recording. It is not evidence for either: the level-four triplet
  is a natural home for three generations in modular flavour models.
- **The basis is not shared.**
  - The preprint's generations are T's eigenlines, the basis the cusp's residual ℤ₄ singles out. The record's three are
    the parity lines, alike under the moves.
  - In the record's terms, the preprint's three are what the parity lines become once a state of the field has broken
    the weave's group toward the cusp (W50). They share one line, θ₂²'s, the preprint's third generation.
  - Its hierarchy-by-charge is a choice of basis that the weave does not make.
- **The numbers stand; their significance does not.**
  - As arithmetic it is right: 39 of 41 printed claims reproduce, and the other two trace to its own inputs and
    rounding. Its mass relations also hold at M_Z on the record's independent data.
  - Its coefficients are selections from a family that covers every number at percent precision.
  - Its strongest landing, the anchor, is tested at 0.15%, not 0.02%, once ε is not fixed by hand.
  - On the families actually used, the four best landings have a joint chance of 16% (28% with the anchor at its
    honest precision).
  - Its relations other than the 1–2 double ratios hold at one scale, as it says itself.
- **So it is not a derivation the record can adopt.** 0 of 19.
- **What it gives the record.**
  - **A candidate state,** τ = 1.0685 i, 0.033 from i in the disc's coordinate u. It is none of W51's critical points
    (i, ω, 1.551i, ½ + 2.224i, 2.226i).
  - **Two numbers for C1.** The double ratio 2/ε = 10.714 (−1.2σ from the target) and the plaquette
    (2/(ε Im τ))^{1/2} = 3.167.
  - **A mechanism.** In it, d and e differ by one automorphy factor (Im τ)^{1/2} on the light down quarks, a difference
    of modular weight. After A1, that is the kind of continuous d–e splitting a state of the field on the fibre can
    supply. W59 (couplings as overlaps) is where the record tests it.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, with what was seen before it disclosed; one run; five cells as predicted; K4 and
K7 failed as worded and were traced in a labelled post hoc). It checks an outside claim; K1 and K2 concern the weave's
T. 0 of 19.

## Reading: couplings as overlaps on the shared fibre (READING, C0 of the approved plan; 2026-10-13)

Nothing here is computed by a script. Points 2 and 3 are proved by hand below, from W49's computed T = ℓ ⊗ M. The
plan (approved by the owner, 2026-10-13) asked for this design reading before W59, "couplings as overlaps", whose first
target is the double ratio. It covers the candidates, equivariance and convergence at the puncture.

**Weave or thread?** Weave-type: the zero modes are the weave's T (W21, W49), on the fibre every thread shares. Given Λ
(GENESIS FK11) for T as the generations.

1. **An overlap on the fibre factorizes** (from W49).
   - The three generations are one zero mode carried three times: T = ℓ ⊗ M, with ℓ the spin doublet's holomorphic
     line and M the three imaginary quaternion units.
   - An overlap is an integral over the fibre of zero modes and a Higgs mode, contracted by pairings the local system
     allows. If the Higgs mode is itself a section of a local system built from the weave, its M-content is a constant
     tensor N.
   - So every such coupling is Y_rs = c · N_rs: one fibre number c, the same for every pair of generations, times a
     tensor on M.
   - This is W49's "a datum from the local system and the surface alone is a scalar on M", extended to cubic couplings.
     The fibre adds no generation dependence. Any generation dependence sits in N, the Higgs's flavour content: W56's
     freedom.
2. **A Higgs neutral under the common point gives zero** (proved here, in the T ⊗ T reading of W39).
   - The common point's only invariant pairing ρ_Q ⊗ ρ_Q → ℂ is the antisymmetric ε.
   - A cubic coupling of two holomorphic modes needs a Higgs of type (0, 1). Read as spinors that is a gauge field's
     fibre component (gauge–Higgs). Read as one-forms it is a Beltrami differential, a variation of τ. Either way the
     Higgs's channel contracts ℓ with ℓ.
   - In the neutral channel the integrand carries ε(ℓ(z), ℓ(z)) = 0 at every point, whatever N is.
   - So the fluctuations of the field's state that are neutral (of τ, or of a Wilson line common to all) do not couple
     the three alike. A nonzero coupling needs the Higgs in the symmetric channel Sym² ρ_Q = ad ρ_Q, which for the
     quaternion group is the sum of the three parity characters χ_p. So **the Higgs must carry a parity.**
   - This is W56's "the Higgs carries the generation index", now forced by the geometry rather than chosen, since the
     parities are the units that make up M (W49, E1).
   - In B1615's T̄ ⊗ T reading the neutral channel survives, with ε(ℓ, ℓ′) ≠ 0, and gives N alone: three degenerate
     generations unless N breaks the weave's group.
3. **No normalizable massless Higgs carries a parity** (proved here).
   - For a non-trivial character χ of F₂, H¹(F₂; χ) is one-dimensional: Z¹ = ℂ², B¹ = ℂ.
   - The puncture loop c = [a, b] has χ(c) = 1. A cocycle z restricts to z(c) = (1 − χ(b)) z(a) − (1 − χ(a)) z(b).
     That vanishes on coboundaries and not on the other class.
   - So the parabolic part is 0, and so is the L² cohomology (in two dimensions the L² condition on one-forms is
     conformally invariant, as in W21).
   - The one class in each parity channel is a boundary class. A massless Higgs that carries a parity lives at the
     puncture's end, as an end condition: GENESIS GAP2 for the fibre's puncture, of the kind GENESIS FK10 names at the
     weave's cusp. It is not forced by the moves.
   - **Convergence.** The zero modes have square-root poles (W21), so ℓ ⊗ ℓ ~ z⁻¹ and the overlap with any bounded
     Higgs profile converges (∫ |z|⁻¹ d²z is finite). Whether a boundary class's profile is bounded at the puncture is
     to be computed.
4. **Where generation dependence on the fibre could come from.**
   - **Within one sector.** With the Higgs in a parity channel p, the fibre number becomes c_p(τ), one per channel. So
     the overlaps give three numbers, not one. Near the cusp the parity channels behave differently (θ₂ against θ₃ and
     θ₄; W53's exponents), so c_p(τ) could be hierarchical. The record does not know these numbers.
   - **Between sectors (the d–e split).** A hypercharge or B − L Wilson line on the fibre twists ℓ differently for
     d-type and e-type fields (A1). That changes their c_p(τ) differently per channel, so their spectra can differ,
     which a common rescaling cannot do. This is the concrete form of A1's third exit.
   - **Splitting the three apart.** A flavour Wilson line in Aut(𝕎) = U(3) acting on M would give the three lines
     different wavefunctions. That is allowed, not forced, and adds up to six real numbers.
5. **What follows for W59.**
   - C1 as first planned ("overlaps at a common state, the double ratio first") cannot reach the target without a Higgs
     mode, and the fibre supplies none that is normalizable and carries a parity.
   - **The fork.**
     - (a) Accept the Higgs's boundary class at the puncture as a tagged end condition. It is one class per channel and
       the moves permute the channels, so one normalisation serves all three. The couplings are then numbers,
       Y = Σ_p h_p c_p(τ) N_p, with the state (τ and the Higgs's direction h) as the only freedom. W59 would
       compute c_p(τ), then the hypercharge twist and the double ratio.
     - (b) Take the Higgs from outside the fibre. Then the couplings are W53–W56's modular structure again, with the
       coefficients free.
     - (c) Go to D1 first, to see whether a potential selects a state at all.
   - Which fork to take is the owner's ruling. Fork (a) adds an end condition the moves do not force, of the kind the
     owner kept open for the cusp ("Keep FK10 open", 2026-10-09).

**Status.** READING. Points 2 and 3 are proved by hand, point 1 follows from W49's computed T = ℓ ⊗ M, and points 4
and 5 are design. Nothing is computed by a script. 0 of 19.

## Reading: the one-loop potential on the shared fibre (READING, the design of D1; 2026-10-13)

Nothing here is computed by a script. The owner ruled "D1 first" after C0 (2026-10-13; recorded in
`docs/THE_OWNERS_RULINGS_2026-10-08.md`). This reading sets out what the potential on the field's states (τ and a
Wilson line w) can and cannot say before W59 computes it. Each claim is marked: **[E]** exact for every potential
with the stated symmetry, **[D]** derived here by hand from cited theorems, or **[P]** planned for W59.

**Weave or thread?** Weave-type: the sectors are the weave's local systems on the shared fibre, all at once. The field
content (which fields, how many, bosons or fermions) is a dictionary datum (GENESIS FK11) that the weave does not fix.
So the potential is stated sector by sector, and a claim about it holds for every content or names the content it
needs.

1. **What the potential is.** Integrating out the fibre's towers gives V = Σ_s ± n_s v(Δ_s): one term per sector s,
   with sign + for bosons and − for fermions. Here v is the fibre's own one-loop term, the regularized log det (two
   dimensions), or the six-dimensional Casimir sum (ζ at s = 3).
2. **[E] The symmetric points.**
   - **τ.** i and ω are stationary for every modular-invariant potential (relay §38).
   - **w, at every τ.** V(w) = V(−w), since conjugate bundles have one spectrum. So the four 2-torsion lines are
     stationary.
   - **w, at τ = ω.** The order-3 rotation of the hexagonal torus permutes the three parities (W40, W50). So a content
     that treats the parities alike (THE WEAVE's rule) is invariant under it. Its fixed points, 0 and two 3-torsion
     points, are then stationary too.
3. **[D] The sectors' two-dimensional determinants, in closed form.**
   - **Trivial sector:** det′ Δ = A · y|η|⁴ (Kronecker's first limit formula), with y = Im τ and A the area.
   - **A parity sector χ_p with a U(1) line w acting with charge q:**
     det Δ = |θ_p(qw | τ)/η(τ)|² · exp(−2π q² (Im w)²/y). This is Kronecker's second limit formula (Ray and Singer).
   - **The three parities jointly:** θ₂(z)θ₃(z)θ₄(z) = η³ θ₁(2z)/θ₁(z), so the product of the three is
     |θ₁(2qw)/θ₁(qw)|² · exp(−6π q² (Im w)²/y).
     - At w = 0 it is 4 (Jacobi): W51's flat joint determinant.
     - 4 is also the degree of the unbranched four-fold cover on which the parities become trivial.
   - **The common point ρ_Q** (the matter's sector, with its −1 at the puncture).
     - ρ_Q's image is the quaternion group, so it becomes trivial on W21's genus-3 cover Σ_τ. That cover has degree 8
       and is branched only over the puncture, with four cone points of angle 4π.
     - Its Jacobian is isogenous to E_τ times an abelian surface that does not vary, since W21's period map is
       constant.
     - Kokotov's formula: det Δ_Σ = C · Area · det Im 𝔹 · |τ_B|². Here τ_B is the Bergman tau function on the stratum
       H(1, 1, 1, 1). It is holomorphic and nowhere zero along the family. It has weight ½: weight 1 from det(C𝔹 + D),
       and −½ from the stratum's homogeneity p = ½. So τ_B ∝ η.
     - At fixed area, with ζ_Σ(0) = −3/2 (each cone point of angle 4π gives −1/8; the zero mode gives −1),
       det Δ_Σ ∝ y^{1/2}|η|². This is modular invariant, as it must be.
     - The cover's spectrum splits by the quaternion group's representations, with ρ_Q twice. Dividing out the trivial
       and parity factors leaves **det Δ_{ρ_Q} ∝ (y|η|⁴)^{−1/4}.**
   - **So, without a Wilson line,** every scalar sector's two-dimensional determinant is a power of y|η|⁴ or a parity
     theta constant, and the parities together give a constant.
     - For a content that treats the parities alike, V(τ) = N · log(y|η|⁴) + const, with N fixed by the content.
     - Its only interior stationary points are i (a saddle) and ω (y|η|⁴ is largest there; W51).
     - So τ sits at ω, runs to the cusp, or is flat (N = 0, as with supersymmetry). It is never at a generic point,
       such as W58's 1.0685 i.
   - **The limits of this.**
     - A Dirac sector with zero modes brings in their norm, W41's invariant g(τ). Its τ-dependence is to be computed.
     - The six-dimensional Casimir sum is not a determinant. For the matter's sector it has no closed form here.
     - The Kokotov step is a derivation, not a computation. W59 checks the steps it can: the genus, the cone angles,
       the ζ(0) bookkeeping, and the periods of the fixed abelian surface.
4. **[D] Where a Wilson line can split the down quarks from the charged leptons at a symmetric point.**
   - **The charges.** In units of 6Y: Q 1, u^c −4, d^c 2, L −3, e^c 6, H 3.
   - **A 2-torsion line.** Each field sees its charge times the line, modulo the lattice: the doublets ½, the
     singlets 0. That is the same for quarks and leptons. So **no 2-torsion hypercharge line splits d from e.**
   - **A 3-torsion line.** 6Y mod 3 is 1, 2 and 2 for Q, u^c and d^c, and 0 for L, e^c and H. So the quarks are
     twisted and the leptons and the Higgs are not.
     - This is the ℤ₃ in the Standard Model's ℤ₆ (W29). Such a line acts on every field as the colour centre does. It
       breaks no symmetry and twists only the quarks.
   - **The 3-torsion lines are stationary for every content that treats the parities alike only at τ = ω** (point 2).
   - **So the record's one route to a d–e split at a symmetric state is τ = ω with a 3-torsion hypercharge line**,
     the colour centre's. Whether it is a minimum depends on the content.
   - **The tension.** At ω the couplings W50 computed are degenerate. Whether the line lifts the degeneracy is to be
     computed.
5. **[P] W59, D1's computation (rule first).**
   - Check point 3's closed forms numerically: Kronecker's formula against a direct ζ sum, the joint identity, and the
     cover's bookkeeping.
   - Map the stationary points of the abelian Wilson-line potential on the Jacobian, at τ = ω and at a generic τ:
     - for the hypercharges above, in the Higgs's parity sector and in each parity sector;
     - with both signs.
   - At τ = ω on the 3-torsion line: each sector's characteristics, and whether the line is a minimum for either sign.
   - The couplings at that state need C0's fork (a), which waits for the owner.

**Status.** READING. Point 2 is exact. Points 3 and 4 are derived by hand from cited theorems: Kronecker's limit
formulas, Jacobi's identities, and Kokotov's determinant formula with its tau function's weight. Nothing is computed by
a script. 0 of 19.

## W59. The one-loop potential on the shared fibre (D1): τ goes to ω or the cusp in every closed-form sector; at ω a charged boson's line goes to the colour centre's 3-torsion points, which only coloured fields can see (`the_one_loop_potential_on_the_fibre.py`; `W59_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_one_loop_potential_on_the_fibre_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- The owner ruled "D1 first" (2026-10-13), after C0.
- The design reading ("Reading: the one-loop potential on the shared fibre") marked its claims exact or derived.
  This arc computes what has closed forms now.
- The matter's sector with a Wilson line, and its six-dimensional term, are named as open and not guessed.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W59_RULE.md`, 311f0cd96). What the seat had worked by hand before writing it is listed there.
- **The script.** Committed before its one run (39c6a5125). The pre-run review made line wraps only.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread (3c4a98399).
- **A labelled post hoc** script, committed before its own run, checks K4 against the truncation and corrects one
  read-out.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; K1, K2, K3 and K5 as predicted; K4 failed as worded).**
- **K1, the twisted determinant (as predicted).** The Chowla–Selberg route and the theta route agree to 1.1 × 10⁻¹⁵ at
  eight samples. So det Δ_w = |θ₁(w | τ)/η(τ)|² e^{−2π(Im w)²/y}, the seat's hand derivation. The three parity sectors
  multiply to 4 to 9 × 10⁻¹⁵ at five τ.
- **K2, the matter determinant's checkable steps (as predicted).**
  - The quaternion cover has genus 3, counted both over E and over ℙ¹.
  - w⁴ = cubic has exactly the differentials dx/w², dx/w³ and x dx/w³.
  - ζ_Σ(0) = −3/2 = −1 − p.
  - y^{1/2}|η|² and (y|η|⁴)^{−1/4} are invariant under S and T to 6 × 10⁻¹⁶.
