  - The matter's two-dimensional determinant (y|η|⁴)^{−1/4} therefore stands as derived, not computed.
- **K3, τ (as predicted).** In the fundamental domain up to y = 6:
  - log(y|η|⁴) is stationary only at i (a saddle) and ω (its maximum);
  - E(τ, 3) is stationary only at i (a saddle) and ω (its minimum), and grows toward the cusp: 130, 440 and 1042 at
    y = 4, 6 and 8;
  - the direct lattice sum checks the Chowla–Selberg E(τ, 3) to 9 × 10⁻¹²;
  - the joint parity sum is −(15/16) E(τ, 3) to 6 × 10⁻¹⁴.
- **K4, the line, for a joint-parity charged boson (failed as worded).** The global extrema went as predicted; the fine
  structure did not.
  - **At τ₀** exactly the four 2-torsion points, with the minimum at (½, ½), as predicted.
  - **At τ₁** six points. The global minimum is at (½, ½), but (½, 0) is a shallow local minimum (Hessian eigenvalues
    0.63 and 284), with two saddles beside it at (0.4998, 0.0281) and its mirror.
  - **At ω** twelve points.
    - The maximum is at 0, and the global minimum is at both 3-torsion points, equal at −1.7255, as predicted.
    - But the three nonzero 2-torsion points are local minima (−1.5530), not saddles, with six saddles between them
      (−1.5496).
  - **For a fermion** the minimum is at 0 in all three cases, as predicted.
- **K5, who sees which line (as predicted).** At all eight nonzero 3-torsion lines exactly Q, u^c and d^c are twisted.
  At all three 2-torsion lines exactly Q, L and H are. The colourless fields' sums at a 3-torsion line equal their sums
  at 0 exactly.
- **A read-out with a bug.** The Higgs's lines were computed for every local minimum, so the lines derived from the
  2-torsion minima are wrong (ninths where sixths belong). Those from the two 3-torsion minima are right. Corrected in
  PH2.

**Post hoc (labelled; written after the read-out; W59's JSON unchanged).**
- **PH1, the fine structure is not the truncation.**
  - From N = 48 to 240, the Hessian at the 2-torsion points stays positive: at ω 2.60 → 2.61 (with 160), at τ₁ 0.624 →
    0.637 (with 284).
  - At N = 96 the stationary set at ω is the same twelve points.
  - At τ₁ the post hoc's coarser grid missed the shallow minimum at (½, 0) itself but found its two saddles.
  - So the seat's hand estimate was wrong. Its leading shell left a zero in the Hessian at the 2-torsion points, and
    the next shells decide.
- **PH2, the Higgs alone at ω, corrected.**
  - The Higgs's global minima put 3w at a 3-torsion point, so w lies on one of eighteen lines: (1/9, 1/9) or
    (2/9, 2/9), plus ⅓ℤ².
  - On every one, d^c and e^c see different twists. At w = (1/9, 1/9) the twists are Q 1/9, u^c 5/9, d^c 2/9, L 2/3,
    e^c 2/3 and H 1/3 (each component): the quarks in ninths, the leptons in thirds.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts and the labelled post hoc; given Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **τ is ω, the cusp, or flat, in every sector with a closed form, in two dimensions and in six.**
  - Every such sector's τ-dependence is a multiple of log(y|η|⁴) or of E(τ, 3). Both are stationary only at i (a
    saddle) and ω.
  - The parity sectors' bosons give +(15/16) E, as twisted fields do; untwisted bosons give −E.
  - So for a content that treats the parities alike, τ sits at ω when the net coefficient of E is positive, and runs
    to the cusp when it is negative. The sign is the content's, a dictionary datum.
  - The matter's two-dimensional term is of the same kind (derived). Its six-dimensional term is open.
  - No sector selects a generic τ such as W58's 1.0685 i.
- **The line.**
  - At ω, charged bosons in the parity sectors go to the 3-torsion lines. At a generic τ they go to a 2-torsion line,
    which cannot split d from e (K5). Fermions go to no line.
  - The colour centre's 3-torsion line is invisible to every colourless field (K5). So only coloured fields can select
    it, and they sit in the matter's sector ρ_Q, whose determinant with a line is not computed here.
- **A discrete d–e split appears, if the Higgs's term dominates.** The Higgs alone (charge 3, a boson carrying a parity,
  as C0 requires) goes to lines where the quarks see ninths and the leptons thirds, so d^c and e^c differ (PH2).
  Nothing here shows that the Higgs's term dominates.
- **The tension stays.** At ω the couplings W50 computed are degenerate. The state the potential prefers is the state
  where the couplings, as computed so far, give no hierarchy.
- **What decides next.** The matter's sector with a line: the genus-3 cover's theta function through Fay's formula
  (W60). Then the couplings at that state need C0's fork. 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, with what was worked by hand disclosed; one run; K1, K2, K3 and K5 as predicted;
K4 failed as worded, with the global extrema as predicted and the fine structure checked against the truncation in a
labelled post hoc; one read-out's bug corrected post hoc). A WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ, sector by
sector, with the content a dictionary datum. 0 of 19.

## W60. The quark sector with a Wilson line: the quarks resist the colour-centre line, and the full potential puts the line at a half-period, where the matter sees nothing and one parity copy of the Higgs becomes massless (`the_quark_sector_and_the_line.py`; `W60_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_quark_sector_and_the_line_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- The owner ruled "W60: quark sector" (2026-10-13), after W59.
- At ω the colour centre's line is seen only by the quarks, which live in the matter's local system ρ_Q. ρ_Q's spectrum
  with a line had not been computed.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W60_RULE.md`, 560c86dff). It fixed the method: the lattice, N, K, t₀, the grid and the special
  points.
- **The script.** Committed before its one run (bcc1396b9). The pre-run review gave the abelian control the reference
  (½, ½), since the untwisted line has a zero mode and Kronecker's form is undefined there. The matter keeps φ = 0.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread (752ce95ff).
- **A labelled post hoc** script followed. Its first launch crashed before writing anything (a scalar conversion) and
  was fixed (cf6cd4d8a). Its one run is the one recorded.

**The method.** The spectrum of ρ_Q ⊗ L_φ on the punctured fibre at τ = ω.
- **The lattice.** A triangular one, with the puncture's −1 as a half flux in the one triangle around it.
- **The potential.** From heat-trace differences: two-dimensional (Δ log det) and six-dimensional (ΔV₆).
- **The units.** Everything is in the same heat-trace units as the exact abelian values.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; P2, P3 and P4 as predicted; P1 failed as worded).**
- **P1, the abelian control (failed as worded).** At four of its five points the lattice matches the exact values:
  - Δ log det against Kronecker to 2.6 × 10⁻¹⁰;
  - ΔV₆ against the lattice sum to 1.4 × 10⁻⁴ relative at N = 192, and 5.5 × 10⁻⁴ at N = 96.
  - The fifth point, (½, 0), and the reference (½, ½) are both 2-torsion points. The order-3 rotation makes them
    equivalent at ω, so both values are exactly 0 and the relative error the rule asked for is 0/0.
- **P2, the symmetries (as predicted).** Period ½, φ → −φ and the order-3 rotation hold on the lattice to 4 × 10⁻¹⁴
  in the spectra and 3 × 10⁻¹¹ in the potentials.
- **P3, the matter's two-dimensional term (as predicted).** The fermion's term is lowest at φ ≡ 0 and highest at
  (⅙, ⅙) ≡ (⅓, ⅓): 0.0256, against 0.0227 at the quarter points.
- **P4, the matter's six-dimensional term (as predicted).**
  - The fermion's term is lowest at φ ≡ 0 and highest at (⅙, ⅙) ≡ (⅓, ⅓): 0.512, against 0.455 at the quarter
    points.
  - No point of the N = 96 grid is lower than φ = 0.
  - So the quarks, as fermions, resist the colour-centre line.
- **The read-outs.**
  - **Convergence.** The lowest eigenvalues change by 0.02% to 0.27% from N = 96 to N = 192.
  - **The hand estimate's shape.** The doubled-winding form correlates with ΔV₆ on the grid at −0.9999995. Odd
    windings have trace 0 in ρ_Q, so the line enters the matter only through doubled windings.
  - **The stated content's net term against w = 0** (quarks, and the Higgs in the three parity sectors jointly):
    - +36.9 at the colour-centre line w₀, where the Higgs is blind;
    - −126.5 and −125.5 at the Higgs's own lines (1/9, 1/9) and (2/9, 2/9).
    - So the Higgs's term outweighs the quarks' at those lines, against the rule's "which the counts make unlikely".

**Post hoc (labelled; written after the read-out; W60's JSON unchanged).**
- **PH1, P1 re-scored with absolute errors.** It holds: the largest |ΔV₆ − exact| is 0.0043 at N = 192, against
  values up to 34.
- **PH2, the matter's ΔV₆ as a cosine series of period ½.**
  - It fits the grid to 2 × 10⁻⁹.
  - Its N = 96 values lie within 0.6% of the N = 192 points.
- **PH3, the net six-dimensional term over every line,** for stated contents (fermions −2 per state, a complex doublet
  +2 per parity sector):
  - **With a Higgs** (in the three parity sectors jointly, or in any one), with or without the leptons, the global
    minimum lies on the 2-torsion lines w with 3w equal to the Higgs's parity. Its value is −147.6 in every case.
    - Jointly there are three such minima, one per parity.
    - They lie below the Higgs's own lines (−126) and the colour-centre line (+36.9).
  - **Without a Higgs** the minimum is at w ≡ 0, with value 0, degenerate with the 2-torsion lines.
  - **At those lines** the matter's potential is exactly 0. A half-period line is equivalent to no line for ρ_Q
    (ρ_Q ⊗ χ_p ≅ ρ_Q), so the quarks and the leptons see nothing. d^c and e^c see the same effective twist: no d–e
    split.
  - **The Higgs gains** because one of its three parity copies sees χ_p ⊗ L_{3w} trivial: that copy becomes untwisted.

**What it shows (a READING on the computed facts and the labelled post hoc; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; the content a
dictionary datum).**
- **The quark sector resists the colour-centre line, as predicted.**
  - In ρ_Q the line acts only through doubled windings, where the holonomy is −1. That is also why the matter's
    potential has period ½.
- **D1's route to a d–e split at a symmetric state closes, as the rule's reading predicted, and for a reason it did
  not state.**
  - The minimum is a half-period line, which the matter cannot feel at all.
  - The rule's sub-claim that the Higgs's term would not dominate was wrong at the Higgs's own lines. It does not move
    the minimum.
- **What the minimum does supply: a massless Higgs that carries a parity.**
  - At the selected line one parity copy of the Higgs has a trivial local system, so it has a normalizable massless
    mode on the fibre: the constant (0, 1)-form.
  - C0 found that no such mode exists without end data (its point 3). Here the line supplies it, with no end
    condition. The Higgs's local system is χ_p ⊗ L_{3w}, and the line's half-period cancels the parity's signs.
  - Which parity is chosen is free among three: forced to choose, not forced which. The choice picks one of the units
    of M, so it splits the three alike into one and two.
- **So C0's fork can be revisited without a new postulate.**
  - At τ = ω, on a half-period hypercharge line, the coupling's Higgs is the untwisted copy's mode.
  - The overlaps C0 described (one number per channel times a tensor on M) become computable. That is the next arc
    to propose, rule first.
  - At ω, W50's couplings were degenerate. Whether these are is to be computed.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; P2, P3 and P4 as predicted; P1 failed as worded, a 0/0 at a symmetric
point, re-scored post hoc; the net term's minimum found in a labelled post hoc, after one failed launch fixed). A WEAVE
result on the ruled branch, given Λ, at τ = ω, with the content a dictionary datum. 0 of 19.

## W61. The couplings at the selected state: zero in the T ⊗ T reading, three degenerate generations in the T̄ ⊗ T reading (`the_couplings_at_the_selected_state.py`; `W61_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- The owner chose "W61: the couplings" (2026-10-13), after W60.
- W60's post hoc found the state the full potential selects: τ = ω, a half-period hypercharge line w, and the Higgs copy
  p* = 3w with a trivial local system, so with a normalizable massless mode.
- This arc computes the Yukawa couplings there, as overlaps of the zero modes, in both readings of the mass term.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W61_RULE.md`, 95c294b53). It disclosed the hand derivation behind its predictions.
- **The script.** Committed before its one run (ee19e5824), with no pre-run changes.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; every cell as predicted).**
- **Q1, the algebra (as predicted).** These use the record's ρ_Q and C_c (W21's second route), over all pairs of blocks
  and all three Higgs channels.
  - A coupling is single-valued exactly when c₁ + c₂ = p*, in both readings.
  - When it is, the T ⊗ T form is antisymmetric and the T̄ ⊗ T form is ±I.
  - The invariant form is ε, and the blocks' characters are as labelled.
- **Q2, the sectors (as predicted).** At each of the three half-period lines:
  - **T ⊗ T:** every Yukawa matrix vanishes (largest |Y| 9 × 10⁻¹⁶).
  - **T̄ ⊗ T:** every Yukawa matrix is diagonal with signs: diag(±1, ±1, ±1) times N(ω)/2 = 1.358.
  - The matrices are the same for up, down and lepton. So the three generations are degenerate in every sector, the
    mixing is the identity, and d and e are identical.
- **Q3, the numbers at ω (as predicted).**
  - W21's equivariance holds at ω for the numerator θ₃ alone.
  - N(ω) = 2.7152, the same with 96 and 48 points to 5 × 10⁻¹⁵.
  - The T ⊗ T integrand is 0 to 4 × 10⁻¹⁶ of |f⃗|² at 50 points.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; at the state W60's labelled post hoc found).**
- **The state the potential selects gives the zero modes no tree-level masses that tell the generations apart.**
  - In the record's T ⊗ T reading the selected Higgs does not couple to them at all. Each generation's zero mode is
    the one form F times a constant unit (W49's T = ℓ ⊗ M), and the units multiply to ±1, so every coupling is
    ±ε(F, F) = 0.
  - In the T̄ ⊗ T reading the couplings are the L² pairing: three equal masses, no mixing.
- **This is the multiplicity (W49) again, now with a state and a Higgs in place.** Any coupling built from the local
  system, the surface and a Higgs whose effective local system is trivial is a scalar on M (W49's reading).
  - W60's state supplies the Higgs. It cannot supply flavour.
- **What the hierarchy needs.** A datum that acts on M differently for the three units:
  - a flavour line in Aut(𝕎) = U(3) (C0, point 4; allowed, not forced);
  - or a Higgs whose effective local system stays non-trivial, which has no normalizable mode (C0, point 3);
  - or a state away from the one selected (τ ≠ ω, where W50's couplings in τ alone break the parity grading).
  - None of these is selected by anything computed so far.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, the hand derivation disclosed; one run; every cell as predicted). A WEAVE result
given Λ, at τ = ω on a half-period line, in both readings of the mass term. 0 of 19.

## Reading A1–W61 together (READING; the owner asked to pause and reflect, 2026-10-13)

Nothing here is computed. It reads the stretch from the owner's approved plan (A1, A2, A3, C0, D1) to W61, and names
one lead. Weave-type throughout; given Λ (GENESIS FK11).

1. **What the stretch established.**
   - **The field's states are allowed, not forced (A1).** τ and the Wilson lines are states, and the moves fix
     neither.
   - **A target (A2).** The double ratio 10.58 ± 0.11.
   - **An outside route (W58).** It reached the record's triplet T from the data side. Its generations are T's
     eigenlines, not the record's alike three, and its significance was overstated.
   - **Couplings as overlaps (C0).** They factorize as one fibre number times a tensor on M. A Higgs neutral under the
     common point gives zero, and a Higgs carrying a parity has no normalizable mode.
   - **The one-loop potential (W59).** Every sector with a closed form depends on τ only through log(y|η|⁴) or E(τ, 3),
     so τ goes to ω, to the cusp, or nowhere.
   - **The quark sector (W60).** The quarks resist the colour-centre line. The full potential puts the line on a
     half-period, where the matter feels nothing and one parity copy of the Higgs becomes massless.
   - **The couplings there (W61).** Zero in the T ⊗ T reading, three equal masses in the T̄ ⊗ T reading.
2. **The pattern: the dynamics picks the most symmetric state, and the most symmetric state has no flavour.**
   - Each selection lands on a symmetric point: τ at ω, the line on a half-period, the Higgs in one parity, one of
     three.
   - At such a point the weave's residual symmetry still acts on M, the generations' label, by the cube's rotations
     (W49). So every coupling the state supplies is a scalar on M: zero or equal.
   - The three generations are one zero mode carried three times (W49). Their masses can differ only through a datum
     that breaks M's symmetry by a large amount, as the data's ratios of 10⁻² to 10⁻⁵ require.
   - The Higgs's choice of one parity (S₃ to S₂) is a breaking, but at tree level it does not reach the couplings
     (W61).
3. **The universality behind τ.**
   - The trivial sector, the three parities jointly and the abelian lines give multiples of one function: E(τ, s) at
     s = 1 (log det) or s = 3 (six dimensions).
   - In two dimensions the matter's sector does too: (y|η|⁴)^{−1/4}, through the genus-3 cover (the D1 reading).
   - A sum of multiples of one function cannot hold τ at a generic point. It sits at ω or runs to the cusp.
   - The one sector whose six-dimensional τ-dependence is not known to be universal is the matter's.
     - Its periodic orbits give a multiple of E(τ, 3) again: odd windings have trace 0, and the rest add to −(31/1024)
       times E, by hand.
     - But the puncture's half flux adds diffractive orbits, which pass through the puncture. Their t-power differs
       from the periodic orbits', so their sum over the lattice is a different function of τ.
   - **So the owner's first question, what fixes τ, now has a precise form.** Does the puncture's diffraction make the
     matter's six-dimensional τ-dependence non-universal? If it does, sectors of opposite sign can hold τ at a generic
     point.
4. **Why a generic τ matters for flavour.**
   - Away from i and ω the weave's residual on T is only scalars (W50). Couplings in τ alone then break the parity
     grading and give mixing that is not a permutation (W50, N5).
   - The q-expansion also supplies a moderate small parameter: |q|^{1/4} is about 0.19 at Im τ ≈ 1.07, the regime of
     W58's preprint and of the cusp's first step of hierarchy (W53, W57).
   - So the symmetric selection kills flavour. A generic τ held by a non-universal term is the one door the computed
     dynamics has not closed.
5. **The d–e wall, after the stretch.** A1 named three exits:
   - free coefficients (the Standard Model frame);
   - Higgs content beyond the 27 (F-MC's frame);
   - a state of the field on the fibre. W59 to W61 closed this exit at the symmetric states: a half-period line splits
     nothing, and the colour-centre line is resisted.
   - It stays open at generic states, which are the same states as point 4's.
6. **What is chosen, what is selected, what is free.**
   - **Chosen** (dictionary data, GENESIS FK11): the content and its signs, the reading of the mass term, the hand.
   - **Selected, given a content:** τ at ω (or the cusp), a half-period line, one Higgs parity.
   - **Free:** all 19 of the 19 numbers (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.
7. **The proposed next arc (W62), rule first.**
   - Compute the matter sector's six-dimensional τ-dependence along the imaginary axis and the boundary arc. The
     lattice method of W60 generalises to rectangular τ = iy with a five-point stencil.
   - Compare it with the universal E(τ, 3).
   - Then ask whether a content with sectors of opposite sign holds τ at a generic point.
   - If the term is universal, the computed dynamics has no generic τ, and the flavour wall holds for every state the
     record can select. If it is not, the wall's one door opens.

**A correction to points 3 and 4 (2026-10-13, before W62's rule; worked by hand; `W62_RULE.md` checks it).**
- Point 3 listed "the abelian lines" among the sectors with a universal τ-dependence. That holds for the trivial sector
  and the three parities jointly (W59's K3c), not for one parity or one line alone.
- At the line W60 selected, the Higgs's copies sit at 0, (½, 0) and (0, ½). Their sum is (1/16) E(τ, 3) − S_{(½,½)}(τ),
  which is invariant only under the theta group. So the selected state (ω, a half-period) is not stationary in τ.
- Point 4: in W61's overlap reading the couplings at a half-period line are zero or degenerate at every τ, since its
  algebra does not use τ. A generic τ matters only where couplings are functions of τ alone (W50).

## W62. What holds τ at the selected state: the matter's six-dimensional term is not a multiple of E(τ, 3) but keeps its critical points, the Higgs's copies break the modular group, and every content on record runs to a cusp (`the_matter_and_the_shape.py`; `W62_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_matter_and_the_shape_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- The owner chose "W62, as recommended" (2026-10-13), after "Reading A1–W61 together".
- That reading asked whether the matter's six-dimensional term, through the puncture's diffraction, could hold τ at a
  generic point. Drafting the rule found a correction (points 3 and 4 of the reading, recorded under it): at the line
  W60 selected, the Higgs's copies are not parity-symmetric.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W62_RULE.md`, 90272b45b). It disclosed the correction, the comb identity, a paraxial estimate,
  the cusp coefficients, the design enumeration and a timing.
- **The script** (f89cb3a98), committed before its one run. Before the run: a smoke test of the gauge construction
  alone, as the rule allows (plaquettes on five tiny tori), and a line wrap (4b54d7b20). No logic changed.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread (b5810d240).
- **The post hoc** (labelled; script committed before its run; no new spectrum): A1 traced, D's shape fitted with
  extrapolation, the content window read against the cusp.

**The method.**
- One triangular lattice for every shape (W60's six-point Laplacian, unit spacing, the half flux at a triangle's
  centroid). The tori are its index-8400 sublattices with shapes on the imaginary axis (11, y from 1.031 to 2.021), on
  the arc between i and ω (6) and the hexagonal reference. With one lattice and one index, every local term of the heat
  trace cancels between shapes.
- The same shapes at index 4n = 33600 are the same tori at half the spacing.
- The periodic part is subtracted exactly as the comb −½ Σ_{Λ̂[2]} + ¼ Σ_{Λ̂[4]} of abelian twisted tori, in closed
  form. What is left, D, is the diffraction at the puncture.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; M0 to M6, A2 and A3 as predicted; A1 failed as worded).**
- **M0, the construction (as predicted).**
  - On every torus exactly one triangle has holonomy −1, with deviation 0.
  - Kramers pairs to 6 × 10⁻¹⁴.
  - W60's N = 96 torus, built by the new code, gives W60's lowest ten eigenvalues to 3 × 10⁻¹³.
  - The rank-1 operator matches the closed-form spectrum to 4 × 10⁻¹³.
  - λ_K t₀ ≥ 48 everywhere.
  - The other hexagonal sublattice agrees with the reference to 10⁻¹¹, so no orientation artefact.
- **M1, the control (as predicted).** The comb on the lattice matches (31/1024)[E(τ, 3) − E(ω, 3)] to 1.5 × 10⁻⁴ at n
  and 3.6 × 10⁻⁵ at 4n, against differences up to 0.401 (W59's units).
- **M2, the diffraction is there (as predicted).** max |ΔD| = 0.147 at 4n, 400 times the control's error.
- **M3, its sign and size (as predicted).** ΔD has the sign opposite to the periodic part's variation at every member.
  The ratio ΔD/ΔV_per is −0.477 on the arc and near i, falling to −0.37 at y = 2.02. The diffraction cancels about half
  the periodic part's τ-dependence near ω and i.
- **M4, not a multiple of E(τ, 3) (as predicted).** The best multiple is 0.0188 E(τ, 3), against the periodic part's
  31/1024 = 0.0303. Its largest residual, 0.0057 at y = 2.02, is 4.5 times that member's uncertainty.
- **M5, the critical points stay at i and ω (as predicted).** V_ρ rises along the axis from 0.0085 (y = 1.031) to 0.254
  (y = 2.021), and along the arc from ω toward i from 0.0011 to 0.0082. It is lowest at ω, with a saddle at i.
- **M6, convergence (as predicted).** n and 4n agree to 1% (−0.1455 against −0.1466 at y = 2.02). Extrapolations move
  ΔD by less than 1%.
- **A1, the Higgs's copies (failed as worded).**
  - The identity Σ_copies S = (1/16) E(τ, 3) − S_{(½,½)} holds at all five points to 2.5 × 10⁻¹².
  - S_{(½,½)} is unchanged by τ → −1/τ and τ → τ + 2 to 10⁻¹⁵.
  - Under τ → τ + 1 it changes by 3.06, 1.21, 0.24 and 1.09 at four points, but by 0 at ω. There ω + 1 = −ω̄ is ω's
    mirror image, and S_{(½,½)} is mirror-symmetric. The sample point was badly chosen; the post hoc (PH1) confirms the
    mirror identity there.
- **A2, the selected state is not stationary (as predicted).** At ω the Higgs's copies' boson term is −1.553 with
  gradient (−7.52, −4.34). It falls monotonically along the arc to −3.785 at i.
- **A3, the cusps (as predicted).** S_c/y³ at 8i equals κ_c to 4 × 10⁻⁵ of 2ζ(6) for every sector, and to 10⁻⁶ for the
  comb.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **The contents.** Every one is unstable at the cusp, for every line choice. Σ W κ in units of ζ(6), for the lines
  (½, ½), (½, 0) and (0, ½):
  - (a) W60's, the matter and the Higgs's copies: 9.57, 9.57, 1.70;
  - (b) with the gauge bosons: 57.6, 57.6, 49.7;
  - (c) with the graviton: 66.6, 66.6, 58.7;
  - (d) the matter alone: 5.45 for each.
  - Among the members the lowest total is at y = 2.02, toward the cusp, for (a) to (d). For (a) on the line (0, ½)
    the lowest member is ω, but its cusp coefficient is still positive: it rises first and then runs to the cusp.
- **The shape of D.**
  - E(τ, 2) alone fits ΔD to 0.56% rms; E(τ, 3) and E(τ, 2) together to 0.05%.
  - The paraxial estimate's E(τ, 2) shape correlates at 0.99998; its amplitude is 1.38 times too small.
- **The content window.** For a content of the trivial sector and ρ_Q alone, the ratio r = ΔV_ρ/ΔE₃ per axis interval
  rises from 0.0171 to 0.0208 between y = 1.03 and 2.02. It is about 0.0158 on the arc.

**Post hoc (labelled; from the stored values; no new spectrum).**
- **PH1.** A1 holds at every point where τ → τ + 1 is not the mirror: |S(ω + 1) − S(−ω̄)| = 0.
- **PH2, an observation, not a claim.** The E(τ, 2) coefficient of ΔD over 31/1024 is −0.980 at n, −0.988 at 4n,
  −0.995 extrapolated as O(h) and −0.990 as O(h²). So, within 1%, D = −(31/1024) E(τ, 2) + const and
  V_ρ = (31/1024)[E(τ, 3) − E(τ, 2)] + const. Whether that is exact is not established.
- **PH3, the window against the cusp.**
  - With the matter's weight W_ρ < 0 (fermions), a content of the trivial sector and ρ_Q is stable at the cusp only if
    W_triv/W_ρ > 31/1024.
  - Every measured r is below 31/1024, which is r's cusp limit. So a cusp-stable content of this kind rises from i up
    the axis and from ω to i on the arc: its minimum is at ω.
  - A content inside the window has a stationary point on the axis, but it is a maximum, and the content runs to the
    cusp. This assumes r keeps rising beyond y = 2.02, which was not computed.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; at the line W60 selected).**
- **The reading's question is answered.** The matter's six-dimensional τ-dependence is not a multiple of E(τ, 3): the
  diffraction cancels about half of the periodic part's variation, with an E(τ, 2) shape. But both functions have their
  critical points at i and ω only, and the sum keeps E's shape. So the matter cannot hold τ at a generic point.
- **Mixing the matter with the trivial sector gives no generic minimum either** (PH3): cusp-stable contents sit at ω,
  and contents with a stationary point on the axis have a maximum there and run to the cusp.
- **At the selected line the Higgs's copies break the modular group to the theta group.** The state W60 selected is not
  stationary in τ: the Higgs's term pushes τ from ω toward i (A2).
- **Every content on record runs to a cusp.** Its six-dimensional term is unbounded below as one cycle of the fibre
  shrinks.
  - The trivial sector's bosons and the matter's fermions both push τ to the cusp: in ρ_Q the shrinking cycle carries
    the unit i, so the double windings act like antiperiodic ones.
  - Holding τ needs net fermions in the trivial sector (W_triv/W_ρ > 31/1024) or bosons twisted along the shrinking
    cycle. That is a dictionary datum (GENESIS FK11), and even then the computed sectors put τ at ω, not at a generic
    point.
- **So the owner's first question, what fixes τ, has this answer: nothing computed holds τ for the contents on
  record, and no content of the computed sectors holds it at a generic point.**
- **For flavour.** At a half-period line W61's algebra does not depend on τ, so the overlaps are zero (T ⊗ T, the
  record's dictionary, as main confirmed in S118) or degenerate at every τ. The hierarchy needs a datum acting on M,
  which is main's B1644 question.
- **A caution from the audit lane** (P-108, relayed in S118): in the E₈ embedding the weak doublets have odd quaternion
  centre, so W60's parity Higgs needs a parent map. A1 to A3 hold sector by sector either way. The content read-outs
  (a) to (c) assume W60's parity Higgs.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; M0 to M6, A2 and A3 as predicted; A1 failed as worded at one sample
point, traced post hoc). A WEAVE result given Λ: every shape of a stated family, per sector, with contents as read-outs.
0 of 19.

## W63. B1644 and B1645 verified on the seat's own instruments: one generation exactly massless by Jacobi, the other two set by the Higgs's direction; the record's Higgs is a real unit direction, so m ≥ ½ (`b1644_verified.py`; `W63_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- The owner chose "Verify B1644 (Recommended)" (2026-10-14), after W62.
- Main's B1644 couples the zero modes to a Higgs in the adjoint of the quaternion structure (canonically M), read at the
  puncture. B1645 proved Y(a) = a Rᵀ + R aᵀ − (a·R)𝟙 and the half bound.
- Main asked (S120, P-115) whether anything on the seat's record fixes a Higgs direction near the isotropic cone, and
  whether W60's parity Higgs has a direction at the selected line.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W63_RULE.md`, 8d9713d43). It disclosed the residues and B1645's form by hand, and W60's Higgs as
  a direction.
- **The script** (3cdfa7ebc), committed before its one run with main's five printed S3c examples transcribed
  (`received/B1644_S3c_examples.json`). Disclosed in that commit: the seat expected V1's clause on W21's branch to fail
  as worded, since principal roots put two cuts at the puncture. The rule was left as committed.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; V2 to V6 as predicted; V1 failed as worded on one clause).**
- **V1, the residues (failed as worded on the branch clause only).**
  - The contour route (mpmath's jtheta, |z| = 0.04, 96 points) equals the duplication closed forms to 10⁻²⁸ or better
    at all ten τ.
  - The seat's R equals main's table exactly (R_i = −θ₂²/(2πη³), R_j = −θ₄²/(2πη³), R_k = iθ₃²/(2πη³)) to 10⁻³⁰.
    2πη³ = π θ₂θ₃θ₄ to 10⁻³⁰.
  - **The failed clause.** With principal roots per factor, as W61's Q3 evaluates F, the cross term's sign changes
    around the contour at every τ: two cuts meet at the puncture, as the seat expected before the run.
  - W21's own branch, fixed by continuation from its base point as `periods()` does, gives the sign +1 at all ten τ.
    That is main's branch.
- **V2, the structure (as predicted).** Y(e_i), Y(e_j), Y(e_k) and five complex Y(a), computed directly as residues of
  (C_p F)ᵀ ε Φ(a) (C_q F), are symmetric and equal a Rᵀ + R aᵀ − (a·R)𝟙 exactly at 30 digits. The smallest diagonal
  entry is 0.007 of the largest (at 4i).
- **V3, Jacobi (as predicted).** R·R = 0, Y(a) R = 0 and det Y(a) = 0 at 30 digits, at all ten τ.
- **V4, the half bound and the cone (as predicted).** Over 2000 real directions, m ∈ [0.5000000004, 0.996]. m = ½ in
  R's plane and 1 normal to it. m = 0 on 200 isotropic directions. Near the cone the log-log slope is 1 within 0.3%.
- **V5, W60's Higgs as a direction (as predicted).**
  - m(e_k) = ½ to 10⁻¹⁵ on the imaginary axis (i, 1.5i, 2.02i, 4i), and on all eleven of W62's axis members.
  - At ω all three units give m = 0.5657. On W62's arc m(e_k) falls from 0.540 near ω to 0.50002 near i.
  - So the selected line's Higgs (p* = (½, ½), direction k) gives masses 0 : 0.566 : 1 at ω, and 0 : ½ : 1 on the
    axis.
- **V6, main's numbers (as predicted).** Main's five S3c examples at 4i, regenerated from B1644's Fibonacci sphere,
  agree to 4 × 10⁻⁹.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **Toward the cusp,** R/|R| → (0, −1, i)/√2: R_i/R_j = 0.0075 at 4i and 1.4 × 10⁻⁵ at 8i, and R_k/R_j → −i. That is
  main's limit.
- **On W62's axis** m(e_i) rises from 0.62 to 0.89 and m(e_j) falls from 0.60 to 0.505, while m(e_k) stays ½.

  |a·a|/|a|² of about 0.004 to 0.005, 0.02 and 0.06 to 0.07 (context, not a test).

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; given B1644's coupling at the puncture, a hypothesis with P-108's
parent map owed).**

## Reading: the cusp's unit as a vector in M, main's path 4 (READING; the owner: "lets follow that lead properly", 2026-10-14)

Nothing here is computed. It reads main's P-113 ("the cusp's tagged unit as a vector in M rather than uniform … a
reading first") against W46–W48, B1644–B1646 and W62–W63. Hand derivations are marked; W64 would compute them, rule
first.
Weave-type throughout; given Λ (GENESIS FK11).

1. **The lead.**
   - GENESIS FK10's unit of end data at the weave's cusp is a tagged working postulate (v1.39, the owner's decision of
     2026-10-09). Given Λ and the unit, W46's index on the weave's surface is ±3.
   - Main's P-113 asks whether the unit could be a vector in M rather than uniform, so that one postulate gives both the
     ±3 and the datum acting on M that B1644 found missing.
   - P-115 and P-118 add two hopes: that it fixes the Higgs's direction near the isotropic cone (B1645), and that it
     stops τ's runaway to the cusp (W62).
2. **What the record already says about the cusp.**
   - The cusp's monodromy is the move L (W46). End data that L keeps and that respects Λ's parity grading has the form
     (m_fixed, m_pair, m_pair). The index moves by m_fixed + 2m_pair, so a generation-blind source gives multiples of 3
     (W46's reading).
   - Nothing on the weave's surface forces the unit (W47). Flat twists move the index by at most one. The unit is a
     non-flat source, Δ⁻¹ at the cusp. An outside source of 24 lattice-free oscillators gives three; the heterotic
     string gives one (W48).
   - The Higgs's direction must be near the isotropic cone a·a = 0 for a hierarchy (B1645). The record's own Higgs is a
     real unit direction (W63). The theta triplet R tends to (0, −1, √−1)/√2 at the cusp (W63's read-out).
3. **The cusp's geometry on M (by hand).**
   - The record's L is a ↦ a, b ↦ ab (`the_common_point.py`). Through the common point (a ↦ i, b ↦ j) it acts on the
     units by i ↦ i, j ↦ k, k ↦ −j: a quarter-turn about e_i. R is a quarter-turn about e_j, so the moves act on M
     through the cube's rotation group O.
   - L's eigenlines in M ⊗ ℂ are e_i (eigenvalue 1) and e_j ± √−1 e_k (eigenvalues ∓√−1). The last two are isotropic:
     (e_j ± √−1 e_k)·(e_j ± √−1 e_k) = 1 − 1 = 0. They are the only isotropic lines L keeps, and complex conjugation
     (the weave's CP, B1611) exchanges them.
   - So **the isotropic cone that B1645 says a hierarchy needs meets the cusp's eigenlines in exactly two lines**, and R
     tends to one of them at the cusp. That is why R_i → 0 there: the parity (0, ½) with unit i is the one L fixes.
   - On the cone, a conic ≅ P¹, the group O acts as the octahedral group on the sphere. Its special orbits have 6, 8 and
     12 points. The 6 are the isotropic lines kept by the three quarter-turns, e_j ± √−1 e_k and its two rotations, and
     the cusp's pair is among them.
   - **At the cusp B1645's ratio takes its two extremes on L's eigenlines:** m → 1 along e_i (W63: 0.995 at 4i; two
     equal heavy masses), and m = 0 along e_j ± √−1 e_k (one heavy generation, two massless).
4. **Two frames in which a unit "as a vector in M" can act. They are not one frame.**
   - **The surface frame** (W45–W48: the weave's surface M₁,₂ as the internal space; GENESIS FK11, given Λ). Here τ is a
     coordinate, integrated over, and the unit is end data of a bundle over the base.
     - On the fermions: units per L-eigenline (m₁, m₊, m₋) with total 3 for the chiral three. (1, 1, 1) is
       generation-blind. Any other split singles out eigenlines, and m₊ ≠ m₋ is CP-odd. What this does to couplings
       needs the overlap integrals over the surface, which are not on record.
     - On a Higgs in M: its sections near the cusp that the monodromy keeps up to a character lie on L's eigenlines. A
       character of order 4 puts the Higgs's direction at the cusp on the cone. Away from the cusp a section that is a
       modular form for M's representation leaves the cone by powers of q. The hierarchy would then be a power of |q|.
     - An example by hand, not a claim: (−θ₂^{2k}, −θ₄^{2k}, √−1 θ₃^{2k}) is exactly isotropic at k = 1 (Jacobi). At
       k = 2, a·a = −2θ₂⁴θ₄⁴ ≈ −32q, an isotropy defect of about 16|q|.
   - **The fibre frame** (W59–W63: τ a modulus, one value per vacuum, and a a constant vector).
     - Here no end datum acts on a at finite τ. The cusp is only where τ runs.
     - The lead can act only through the Higgs's potential. Main's sealed B1646 (D4) gives the dichotomy: a potential
       symmetric under SO(3) and a phase puts the minimum on the cone or on the real locus, never between. That is the
       textbook polar against ferromagnetic split of a complex triplet (the sign of the |a·a|² term).
     - On the cone the weave's group O then picks finitely many points, the orbits of 6, 8 or 12, and the cusp's pair is
       among the 6.
   - **Which frame carries the physics is a dictionary question** (GENESIS FK11), not the seat's to choose. τ's runaway
     (W62) lives in the fibre frame. P-113's unit lives in the surface frame.
5. **What it would tie together, and what it costs.**
   - **It would tie** FK10's unit (the ±3), the Higgs's direction (on or near the cone), CP (the choice between the two
     conjugate isotropic lines) and, in the surface frame, the hierarchy to the cusp's q-expansion.
   - **It costs:**
     - the surface frame (FK11) for the end-data route;
     - the Higgs in M at the puncture (P-108's parent map);
     - a character of order 4 for the Higgs, a choice among W47's flat twists;
     - a controlled departure from the cone, since exactly on it m = 0 gives no second-generation mass.
   - **Exactly on the cone** the Yukawa has rank one (B1645): one heavy generation and two massless. The observed
     m₂/m₃ needs |a·a|/|a|² of 0.004 to 0.07 (W63's read-out).
   - **Numerology risk.** With Im τ free, powers of |q| fit almost any small ratio. A test must fix the form and τ
     first.
6. **The literature, after the repo (a check made for this reading; the sources were read where marked, and nothing is
   banked).**
   - **Hierarchies near the cusp.** Novichkov, Penedo and Petcov (JHEP 04 (2021) 206, arXiv:2102.07488; read in full):
     near τ = i∞ an entry is suppressed by a power of ε = e^{−2π Im τ/N} at level N, the power fixed by residual
     charges. Their fits need Im τ ≈ 2.65 to 3.11. Petcov and Tanimoto (JHEP 08 (2023) 086, arXiv:2306.05730) need
     Im τ ≈ 2.5 to 3. Feruglio, Gherardi, Romanino and Titov (JHEP 05 (2021) 242, arXiv:2101.08718; read in full) get
     a rank-one charged-lepton matrix at τ = i, with the electron massless.
     - **A correction to point 4's example.** The theta triplet is a level-2 object, so its small parameter is
       e^{−π Im τ}, as the example's 16|q| with q = e^{iπτ} has it, not e^{−2π Im τ}.
     - **A terminology trap.** Some papers call ω "the cusp"; only i∞ is one.
   - **Isotropic against real vacua.** Ho (PRL 81 (1998) 742; read in full) and Kawaguchi and Ueda (Phys. Rep. 520
     (2012) 253; read in full) give the polar and ferromagnetic phases of a complex triplet. Since
     |a* × a|² = |a|⁴ − |a·a|², the sign of the |a·a|² term decides isotropic against real, as B1646's D4 states.
   - **A flavour precedent.** Barbieri, Hall, Kane and Ross (arXiv:hep-ph/9901228; read in full) force isotropic SO(3)
     flavon vacua such as (1, √−1, 0), for neutrino mixing. The check found no paper where a mass ratio is set by
     |a·a|/|a|², the link B1645 makes.
   - **The octahedral group on the cone.** Orbits of 6, 8 and 12 are confirmed (Knibbeler, Lombardo and Sanders,
     arXiv:1504.06777; read in full), and the 6 are e_j ± √−1 e_k and its rotations. A nuance: the degree-6 form
     vanishing on the 6 changes sign under a quarter-turn, so the true invariants have degrees 8, 12 and 18.
7. **The proposed arc (W64), rule first.**
   - **Common to both frames (exact):** L's and R's action on M from the record's lifts; the eigenlines and the
     isotropic ones; R's limit at the cusp equal to one of them; O's orbits on the cone; B1645's m at each special
     point.
   - **The surface frame:** the vector-valued modular forms for M's representation at low weight, with end data per
     L-eigenline at the cusp. Which Higgs zero modes exist, their direction at the cusp, and their isotropy defect as a
     function of Im τ.
   - **The fibre frame:** the potential's dichotomy (B1646, verified on the seat's code), the O-invariant terms on the
     cone, and the Yukawa spectrum at each special point.
   - **The read-out:** whether one end datum gives both the ±3 and a near-cone Higgs, and what Im τ the data's ratios
     would need (context, not a test).
   - The count stays all 19 of the 19 free (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

## W64. The cusp's unit as a vector in M, in both frames: main's residue triplet R is the one section with a single unit on the cusp's isotropic eigenline; the uniform unit gives the three but points the Higgs at a real direction; the weave's anisotropy puts the Higgs on the cusp's isotropic lines, where one generation is heavy and two are massless (`the_cusp_unit_as_a_vector.py`; `W64_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_cusp_unit_as_a_vector_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- The owner said "lets follow that lead properly" (2026-10-14), about GENESIS FK10's unit read as a vector in M (main's
  P-113), and then chose "Both frames". The reading came first (the dossier's "Reading: the cusp's unit as a vector in
  M"), with the literature checked after the repo.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W64_RULE.md`, 9fbf7c0e0). It disclosed the hand derivations: L's quarter-turn, R's law, R's
  end data, the dichotomy and the anisotropy on the cone.
- **The script** (2cf3e2e8e), committed before its one run. The pre-run review made the isotropic-line finder robust
  and declared the sympy symbols real; no cell was computed before the run.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed unread.
- **The post hoc** (labelled; script committed before its run). It traced K2's failure to an instrument error and
  re-scored K2, K4 and K5.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; K1, K3, K6 and K7 as predicted; K2, K4 and K5 failed as worded on one instrument error).**
- **K1, the moves on M (as predicted).** L and R act as quarter-turns about e_i and e_j and generate O (24 rotations).
  L's eigenvalues on e_i, v₊ = e_j − √−1 e_k and v₋ = e_j + √−1 e_k are 1, √−1 and −√−1. v₊ and v₋ are isotropic and
  conjugation exchanges them. The special isotropic lines number 26, in orbits of 6, 8 and 12. The 6 are the
  quarter-turns' fixed lines.
- **K2, R's law (failed as worded).** The S-law held: R(−1/τ) = (−iτ)^{−½} P R(τ) with P the i–j swap, to 4 × 10⁻³¹,
  and −P is a rotation. The T-law fit found no signed permutation (residual 0.88).
  - **The cause (post hoc, PH1).** R was built from mpmath's jtheta(2, 0, q), which takes the principal fourth root of
    the nome. That is θ₂(τ) only for −1 < Re τ ≤ 1. At τ + 1 with Re τ > 0 the run's R_i was off by exactly −√−1, at
    the five such τ, while it agreed with the directly summed series to 4 × 10⁻³¹ at τ itself.
  - **Re-scored with the direct series (PH2):** R(τ + 1) = e^{iπ/4} L⁻¹ R(τ) to 3 × 10⁻³¹. Both laws hold as predicted.
- **K3, R's end data (as predicted).** Along τ = iy, the leading exponents on (e_i, v₊, v₋) are 0.1250000000,
  −0.1250000000 and 0.3750000000. So R carries one unit on v₊ and none elsewhere. R/|R| → v₊/√2, with the e_i part
  falling as e^{−πy/2} and the v₋ part as e^{−πy} (slopes −π/2 and −π to 10⁻⁹).
- **K4, the Higgs's sections (failed as worded; it used K2's wrong representation).** Re-scored post hoc (PH3):
  - at weight −½ the dimension equals Σu for every placement u ∈ {0, 1}³, by collocation and by χ + Σu alike;
  - with u = (0, 1, 0) the one section is R: its q-expansion matches to 3 × 10⁻¹⁵;
  - the generic section points at the cusp along v₋ or v₊ for (0, 0, 1), (0, 1, 0) and (0, 1, 1), and along the real
    e_i whenever u_i = 1, the uniform unit (1, 1, 1) included.
- **K5, the fermions' sections (failed as worded, for the same reason).** Re-scored (PH3): at weight 3/2 the dimension
  is Σu for every placement, by both counts. The chiral three needs Σu = 3.
- **K6, the potential's dichotomy (as predicted).** |a* × a|² = |a|⁴ − |a·a|² holds exactly (sympy). The minimum of
  λ₂|a·a|² is isotropic (|a·a| = 2 × 10⁻¹⁰) for λ₂ = +1 and real up to a phase (4 × 10⁻⁹) for λ₂ = −1.
- **K7, the weave's anisotropy on the cone (as predicted).** O keeps exactly three phase-invariant quartics. On the cone
  A = Σ|a_u|⁴ is exactly ½, ⅜ and ⅓ on the 6-, 12- and 8-orbits, and lies in [⅓, ½] over 10 000 cone points. The minimum
  of |a·a|² + cA lands on a 6-orbit line for c = −0.1 and on an 8-orbit line for c = +0.1, each with overlap 1.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **On every one of the 26 special lines** the Yukawa has rank one (m below 10⁻¹⁵) at τ = i, 2i and ω: one heavy
  generation and two massless. The heavy mass lies between 1.0 and 2.0 times |R|.
- **S̃ = L R⁻¹ L acts on M** as the half-turn about e_i + e_j, not as −P, the half-turn about e_i − e_j that R's S-law
  carries.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; the surface frame and B1644's Higgs in M are hypotheses; GENESIS
FK10's unit is a tagged working postulate).**
- **The cone that a hierarchy needs is the cusp's own geometry.** The cusp's monodromy turns M a quarter-turn. Its two
  complex eigenlines are exactly the isotropic lines it keeps, and CP exchanges them (K1).
- **Main's coupling already carries GENESIS FK10's unit, as a vector in M.** The residue triplet R, whose isotropy makes
  a generation massless (B1644, B1645), is a vector-valued form of the matter's own representation. It is the one
  section with a single unit of end data at the cusp, placed on the isotropic eigenline v₊ (K3; K2 and K4 post hoc).
- **But one datum does not give both.** The chiral three needs three units (K5). With the uniform unit, the generic
  Higgs section points at the cusp along the real fixed unit e_i, not along the cone (K4, post hoc). The isotropic
  direction needs the unit on v₊ or v₋ and none on e_i. So P-113's single postulate for the ±3 and the flavour structure
  does not follow from end data alone.
- **In the fibre frame the weave's own anisotropy puts the Higgs on the cone** (K6, K7). It picks the cusp's six lines
  or the eight (1, ω, ω²)-type lines by the sign of one coefficient. On every such line the Yukawa has rank one.
- **So the lead gives the leading-order pattern, one heavy generation and two massless, from the cusp's geometry in both
  frames.** It also locates the second generation's mass in one missing datum: a departure from the cone of size
  |a·a|/|a|² ≈ m₂/m₃ (0.004 to 0.07). Nothing computed supplies it.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; K1, K3, K6 and K7 as predicted; K2, K4 and K5 failed as worded on one
instrument error, re-scored post hoc where all three hold as predicted). A WEAVE result given Λ and the stated
hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## W65. The second unit: read as a section of the matter's bundle, every Higgs lies at ω on one isotropic line, so the second generation is massless there; the departure from the cone is a function of τ, and the count does not drop (`the_second_unit.py`; `W65_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **W64 left one datum missing.** It located the second generation's mass in a departure from the cone that nothing
  computed supplied.
- **The owner's order** is "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers". The owner's goal "resume" (2026-10-14)
  continued the arc the seat had proposed.
- **What this arc reads.** The Higgs's direction in M, taken as a section of weight −½ in ρ_R (W64's sections) at the
  vacuum's τ. This is the hypothesis H-sec, stated in the rule.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W65_RULE.md`, cf00484e0), with its hand derivations disclosed.
- **The script** (6577097ca), committed before its run.
- **A failed launch, disclosed** (b2ffc42ed). mpmath's qr_solve crashed in the first solve, before any cell was computed
  and before any output was written; its Householder step is real-only. The solve now uses mpmath's complex QR, and the
  residual is computed directly.
- **The run.** One run (4 minutes); its output committed unread (a9bfb25b2).

**The read-out (COMPUTED; all six cells as predicted).**
- **H1, the instrument (as predicted).** The 40-digit collocation solves for E, R′ and R with relative residuals below
  5 × 10⁻⁴¹. Its R equals −2π times the direct theta series to 7 × 10⁻³⁹.
- **H2, the pairings (as predicted).** With Φ = E₄²/η¹⁸, and each section normalized by its leading coefficient, all of
  these hold to 10⁻³⁶ relative to |f||g|:
  - R·R = R′·R′ = 0 at every τ;
  - R·R′ = 2Φ, E·R = 4Φ and E·R′ = 112Φ;
  - E·E = (j − 1280)Φ.

  So the Gram matrix of {E, R, R′} under the dot product is Φ times [[j − 1280, 4, 112], [4, 0, 2], [112, 2, 0]], with
  integer entries.
- **H3, the special points (as predicted).**
  - At ω, E, R and R′ are parallel to 7 × 10⁻³⁸, on one 8-orbit line.
  - At i all three lie in the plane spanned by e_i + e_j and e_k (to 3 × 10⁻⁴¹).
    - R(i) lies on the line of (1, 1, −√−1 √2), and R′(i) on its conjugate (1, 1, √−1 √2). Both are 12-orbit lines.
    - E(i) is not isotropic: |E·E|/|E|² = 63/65.
  - At 8i, E, R and R′ point along e_i, v₊ and v₋. The sines of the angles are 7 × 10⁻²⁵, 1.0 × 10⁻⁵ and 3 × 10⁻¹⁵.
- **H4, the second generation at ω (as predicted).**
  - With main's coupling R, m(ω) is below 3 × 10⁻³⁸ for every placement's combination, for E and for R′.
  - Away from ω and τ₀, the smallest m over E and the four αR + βR′ is at least 7 × 10⁻⁶, the value at 4i.
- **H5, the cusp (as predicted).** For all four (α, β), the slope of log m between 5i and 6i is −π to 4 × 10⁻⁷.
  m_E(8i) = 0.99999.
- **H6, the first generation does not mix (as predicted).** Over 475 pairs of Higgs sections at the sample's points, the
  massless row and column of the mixing matrix are (1, 0, 0) to 2 × 10⁻³⁶.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **E's cone point.** j₀ = 1280 is real, so τ₀ = 0.1422 + 0.9898√−1 lies on the unit arc, and so does its mirror −τ̄₀.
  There E is isotropic and m_E = 0.
- **m_E on the imaginary axis.** It rises from 9/14 at i to 0.99999 at 8i, so it never reaches 0.004 to 0.06 there.
- **m_E on the arc from i to ω.**
  - It falls from 9/14 to 0 at the mirror of τ₀, rises to 0.97 near τ = −0.26 + 0.97√−1, and falls to 0 at ω.
  - It equals 0.02 and 0.06 near the mirror of τ₀ (Re τ from −0.13 to −0.15) and near ω (Re τ from −0.44 to −0.47),
    and 0.004 near ω at τ = −0.486 + 0.874√−1.
  - The 41-point grid missed the 0.004 crossings beside the mirror of τ₀: its smallest sampled value there was 0.008.
- **Near ω, m grows as |τ − ω|².** The slope of log m against log δ is 1.995 for E and for R + R′.
- **At the cusp, m e^{πy} → 2|α/β|.** It is 2.0000 at y = 6 for all four (α, β). At 5i and 6i, m was the same for
  β/α = 1, −1 and √−1.
- **The overlap of R and R′** (Hermitian, normalized):
  - 0 at i, since the conjugate pair is orthogonal, and 0 at the cusp;
  - at most 0.0195 on the axis;
  - rising along the arc from 0 at i to 1 at ω.
- **The uniform combination** gives m from 0.583 at i to 0.99999 at 8i.

**A consequence by hand (post hoc, from H2's integers; not computed).**
- The Gram determinant is −4(j − 1728)Φ³ = −4E₄⁶E₆²/η⁷⁸.
- O lies in SO(3), so det[E, R, R′]² equals it, and det[E, R, R′] = ±2√−1 E₄³E₆/η³⁹.
- So the three sections are independent everywhere except at i (a simple zero, where H3 found all three in one plane)
  and at ω (a triple zero, where they are parallel).

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; B1644's coupling and H-sec are hypotheses; Review 63's P-122 scopes
the identification B1644 rests on).**
- **The modular curve's three special points carry O's three orbits of special lines.**
  - At the cusp, R and R′ sit on the conjugate pair of the four-fold axis (the 6-orbit).
  - At i, they sit on the conjugate pair of a two-fold axis (the 12-orbit).
  - At ω, every section sits on one three-fold line (the 8-orbit).

  W64's two signs of the anisotropy are the cusp's and ω's.
- **At ω the second generation is massless for every Higgs section.** Under H-sec, τ = ω (W60, W62) gives masses
  0 : 0 : m₃. W63's 0 : 0.566 : 1 at ω came from a constant real direction, not a section.
- **The departure from the cone is a function of τ, fixed by end data.**
  - It vanishes at ω, where m is quadratic in |τ − ω|.
  - For E it also vanishes at τ₀, where j = 1280.
  - Toward the cusp the two-unit Higgs gives m ≈ 2|α/β| e^{−π Im τ}, the level-2 small parameter.
- **The count does not drop.**
  - E alone gives one m₂/m₃ = m_E(τ) for all three sectors at the vacuum's one τ. The data's 0.004, 0.02 and 0.06
    cannot all hold.
  - The two-unit Higgs adds one number per sector (|α/β|, at least on the axis).
  - Nothing computed fixes τ.
- **One coupling for every sector gives no first-generation mixing** (H6). A Cabibbo angle needs different couplings per
  sector, for example R against R′, whose overlap runs from 0 at i to 1 at ω.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run after one disclosed failed launch; all six cells as predicted). A WEAVE
result given Λ and the stated hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## W66. The parameter-free census: with the Higgs and the coupling both among the one-unit sections, every sector's Yukawa depends on τ alone, and no assignment fits at any τ (`the_parameter_free_census.py`; `W66_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **W65 left one test open.** It made the second generation's mass a function of τ. The one-unit sections E, R and R′
  carry no free coefficient.
- **The test.** Take B1644's form Y(a; c) = a cᵀ + c aᵀ − (a·c)𝟙 with the Higgs a and the coupling c both among them
  (the hypothesis H-pair, with H-sec). Then each sector's Yukawa depends on τ alone, up to its scale.
- **Why it matters.** If one τ fits more observables than its two real numbers, the count drops. This is the owner's
  order: "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers".

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W66_RULE.md`, 44b13a4a4).
- **The script** (32cf6e169), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run (3.5 minutes); its output committed unread (93b5bc596).

**The read-out (COMPUTED; all three cells as predicted).**
- **The instrument.** The double-precision sections agree with the 40-digit ones to 1.2 × 10⁻¹⁵ at W65's sample.
- **C1, the rank (as predicted).** At W65's 19 sample points away from ω and E's cone points, at 40 digits:
  - RR, R′R′ and RR′ have m₂/m₃ below 10⁻³⁸;
  - ER and ER′ have m₁/m₃ below 10⁻³⁹, null vectors R and R′ (sines below 10⁻³⁷), and m₂/m₃ of at least 0.014;
  - EE has m₁m₃ = m₂² to 3 × 10⁻³⁹.
- **C2, the live assignments (as predicted).** Every assignment that is not a permutation of (EE, ER, ER′) has
  χ²_min above 7.81. The smallest of them is 260.
- **C3, the verdict (as predicted).** No assignment passes. The smallest χ²_min of all 216 is 259.9 at a = 0.10
  (3 degrees of freedom; the pass mark is 7.81).
  - *Correction (2026-10-14, found while reading W69).* This line first said 309.6. The JSON's smallest of all 216 is
    259.9, from ER / EE / EE, a non-permutation in which the down and lepton sectors share EE. 308.6 is the best
    permutation. The verdict is unchanged. It is 951 at a = 0.05 and 66.5 at a = 0.20, and none passes at either.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **The six permutations.** Every one of them has its minimum within 0.04 of ω or of ω + 1, and all ten refinements
  reach it. The best two are mirror images: ER′ / ER / EE at 308.6 and ER / ER′ / EE at 309.6, both at
  τ = −0.475 + 0.880√−1. At ER′ / ER / EE's minimum:

  | observable | there | data |
  |---|---|---|
  | m_c/m_t | 0.0104 | 0.0037 |
  | m_s/m_b | 0.0106 | 0.0187 |
  | m_μ/m_τ | 0.0208 | 0.0589 |
  | \|V_us\| | 0.210 | 0.225 |
  | \|V_cb\| | 0.105 | 0.042 |

  The others run from 440 to 739.
- **The first generation there.** The lepton sector's EE gives m_e/m_τ = 4.3 × 10⁻⁴ (data 2.8 × 10⁻⁴). The CKM's
  |V_ub| = 0.0057 and |V_td| = 0.016 (data 0.0037 and 0.0086).
- **Two at a time.** With up = ER, the up ratio and the Cabibbo angle alone cannot be met at one τ either. The best
  z(m_c/m_t)² + z(V_us)² is 27.5: m_c/m_t = 0.0047 and |V_us| = 0.141, at τ = −0.5 + 0.885√−1.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; B1644's form, H-sec and H-pair are hypotheses).**
- **The parameter-free sections cannot spread the three sectors' ratios.**
  - All three ratios vanish together at ω and grow together from there.
  - At every permutation's minimum they lie within a factor of 2 of each other. The data need a factor of 16, from the
    up sector's 0.0037 to the leptons' 0.059.
  - The fit's best compromise sits near ω, and |V_cb| comes out 2.5 times too large there.
- **So the data do not fix τ through these sections, and the count does not drop.** Under H-sec and H-pair, a fit needs
  free coefficients per sector (W65's two-unit Higgs). That keeps the count where it is.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; all three cells as predicted). A WEAVE result given Λ and the stated
hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## W67. What fixes τ: the weave's own quartic, evaluated on the parameter-free Higgs sections, fixes τ at ω or sends it to the cusp, and every vacuum it selects has the second generation massless or degenerate (`the_potential_fixes_tau.py`; `W67_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_potential_fixes_tau_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- **The owner's order** is "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers". W66 found that the data cannot fix τ
  through the parameter-free sections, so this arc asked what fixes τ from the inside.
- **The candidate.** W64 found that O keeps three phase-invariant quartics on M. Under H-sec (W65) the Higgs's
  direction is a section a(τ), so the potential's shape V̂(τ) = λ₂ q₂ + c q_A becomes a function on the modular curve.
  Here q₂ = |a·a|²/|a|⁴ and q_A = Σ|a_u|⁴/|a|⁴. The new hypothesis H-pot reads the potential this way.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W67_RULE.md`, 5ae3d24a6). By hand it disclosed the bound: q_A ≥ ⅓, with equality exactly when
  all |a_u| are equal. So τ = ω is a global minimizer for λ₂ ≥ 0 and c > 0.
- **The script** (5c80b8b00), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run (2.7 minutes); its output committed unread (5a9945901).
  - It printed numpy overflow warnings. Trial points above y = 8 are caught before evaluation, so the likely source is
    trial points below the real axis, the only place the q-series overflow.
  - No recorded value is affected. Checked after the run: the JSON holds no non-finite value, and every recorded τ lies
    in the domain.
- **The post hoc** (labelled; script 27f5deaf5 before its run; output 3f4963569) checked F1's and F3's failures at
  40 digits.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; F2 as predicted; F1 and F3 failed as worded on R′ alone).**
- **F1, R and R′ (failed as worded, on R′).**
  - **R as predicted.**
    - q₂ is below 10⁻³⁰ on the grid.
    - q_A is ⅓ at ω and ⅜ at i, to 10⁻¹⁶.
    - Away from ω, q_A − ⅓ ≥ 3.6 × 10⁻⁴ and ½ − q_A ≥ 9.7 × 10⁻¹¹, with q_A(8i) = 0.4999999999.
    - Its two refined local minima are at ω and ω + 1.
  - **R′ failed on two counts.**
    - ½ − q_A came out −5.6 × 10⁻¹⁶ at one grid point, a roundoff on a plateau.
    - Its refined local minima include many points at y > 5 where q_A is ½ to double precision. They also include two
      genuine minima at q_A = ⅓ away from ω: near 0.5 + 1.0347√−1 and near ±0.2146 + 0.9767√−1.
- **F2, E (as predicted).**
  - q₂ is below 10⁻³⁰ at ω, at τ₀ and at its mirror.
  - Away from them by 0.02, q₂ ≥ 9.6 × 10⁻⁴.
  - q_A(E(ω)) = ⅓, and both q₂ and q_A are 1.0 at 8i.
- **F3, what fixes τ (failed as worded, on R′).**
  - **R as predicted.** The minimum is at ω for every λ₂ with c > 0, and runs to the cusp with c < 0.
  - **E as predicted** at ω for c > 0 and λ₂ > 0.
  - **R′ failed.**
    - With c > 0 the minimum value c/3 is reached at ω and also at R′'s two other 8-orbit points. The search landed on
      one of those: a tie, not a lower value.
    - With c < 0 the refinement stopped at y = 6.39, below the rule's y > 7 mark for the cusp.

**The post hoc (labelled; W67's JSON unchanged).**
- **PH1, R′'s extra 8-orbit points, at 40 digits.** A root of R′(τ) × w for the nearest 8-orbit line w lies at
  τ = 0.5 + 1.03471√−1 and at τ = −0.214626 + 0.976696√−1.
  - At both, q_A − ⅓ is about 10⁻³¹ and R′·R′ vanishes to 10⁻³⁹. The direction is accurate to about 10⁻¹⁶, the
    precision of w.
  - Klein's j there is −171.98425172440 and 843.98425172440.
  - **Identified by inspection, after the run:** j = 336 ∓ 192√7, to 3 × 10⁻¹⁴ and 8 × 10⁻¹³. It is a conjugate pair in
    ℚ(√7), not proved.
- **PH2, the plateau, at 40 digits.**
  - ½ − q_A(R′(iy)) falls strictly: 2.7 × 10⁻¹³ at y = 4, 4.5 × 10⁻²³ at y = 6.39, and 1.1 × 10⁻²⁹ at y = 8.
  - For R it falls from 2.8 × 10⁻⁵ at y = 4 to 9.7 × 10⁻¹¹ at y = 8.
  - So R′ runs to the cusp with c < 0, as predicted. The census stalled where the remaining gap is below double
    precision.
- **PH3, the masses at R′'s extra points.**
  - Every pair of isotropic sections has rank one there (m below 10⁻³⁹).
  - The pairs with E give m₂/m₃ from 0.23 to 0.52.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **E with c < 0.**
  - The minimum sits at τ₀ for c = −0.1 with λ₂ ≥ 0.1, and for c = −1 with λ₂ ≥ 1.
  - Otherwise it lies on the cusp's plateau (y ≈ 6.95), where E points along e_i and q_A = q₂ = 1 to double precision.
  - q_A(E(τ₀)) = 0.481.
- **All three |E_u| are equal only at ω** (and ω + 1), so E has no tie with λ₂ = 0.
- **The masses at the fixed points.**
  - At ω every pair has m₂/m₃ below 10⁻¹⁵.
  - At 8i the isotropic pairs have rank one, while EE, ER and ER′ give m₂/m₃ = 1.0, 0.99999 and 1.0.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; H-sec and H-pot are hypotheses).**
- **The weave's own potential fixes τ.**
  - **For main's residue triplet R as the Higgs's direction:** at ω for c > 0, uniquely, and at the cusp for c < 0.
    That is the sign of one coefficient, as W64's K7 found for the direction on M.
  - **For R′:** at ω and at two more points with j = 336 ∓ 192√7 (identified), or at the cusp.
  - **For E:** at ω, at τ₀ (j = 1280), or at the cusp.
  - R reaches the 8-orbit lines only at ω, as the octahedral picture of X(4) predicts for a degree-one map. R′ reaches
    them at two more points, as a map of higher degree would.
- **Every vacuum it selects has no hierarchy.** With B1644's coupling R, the second generation is massless at every
  selected point:
  - at ω, every pair has rank one;
  - at τ₀, at R′'s extra points and at the cusp, the Higgs is isotropic.

  The exception is E at the cusp, where m₂ = m₃. The minima of the potential and the rank-one points coincide,
  because both are set by the cone.
- **So τ can be fixed from the inside, but only where the second generation vanishes.** The count stays all 19 of the
  19 free (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; F2 as predicted; F1 and F3 failed as worded on R′ alone, a tie at two
further 8-orbit points and a double-precision plateau, both checked in a labelled post hoc). A WEAVE result given Λ and
the stated hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## W68. Residual charges at ω: the weights −½, 3/2 and 7/2 put the sections on the three eigenlines of ω's stabiliser, and one pairing gives the full hierarchy δ² : δ : 1 from one displacement (`the_residual_charges_at_omega.py`; `W68_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **W67 fixed τ where the hierarchy vanishes.** So the masses must come from a displacement δ = |τ − ω|.
- **W65 and W66 found one power,** δ², at weight −½. This arc asked whether the other weights at which ρ_R is a
  multiplier system give other powers. It is the residual-symmetry mechanism of Novichkov, Penedo and Petcov, read on
  ρ_R.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W68_RULE.md`, 9942c9d09).
- **A correction to G4 before any code** (46fd667a1, disclosed in the rule). As first written, G4 included the pairs of
  two isotropic sections, which W66 had found have rank one.
- **The script** (0a6e61c12), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run (7 minutes); its output committed unread (c4dcd30c2).

**The read-out (COMPUTED; all four cells as predicted).**
- **G1, the sections (as predicted).**
  - At −½ and 3/2 the one-dimensional placements are exactly the three one-unit ones.
  - At 7/2 the dimension is 1 + Σu, so only u = (0, 0, 0) is one-dimensional.
  - That gives seven parameter-free sections: E, R and R′ at −½; three at 3/2; one at 7/2. Their 40-digit solves have
    residuals below 6 × 10⁻⁴¹.
- **G2, the eigenlines at ω (as predicted).**
  - All three weight −½ sections lie on ℓ_ω = (−ω, 1, ω²).
  - All three weight 3/2 sections lie on the real axis (1, −1, −1).
  - The weight 7/2 section lies on ℓ_ω² = (−ω², 1, ω).
  - Every sine to its line is below 3 × 10⁻³⁷. Only R and R′ are isotropic at every τ.
- **G3, the pairs at ω (as predicted).** All 28 pairs follow their two lines, to 10⁻³⁷:
  - rank one for two isotropic lines;
  - m₁ = 0 and m₂ = m₃ for an isotropic line with the axis;
  - m₁ = m₂ = m₃ for two on the axis.
- **G4, the powers near ω (as predicted).**
  - E with the weight 7/2 section: p₁ = 2.000 and p₂ = 1.000.
  - R and R′ with it: m₁ ≡ 0 and p₂ = 1.000.
  - ER and ER′: m₁ ≡ 0 and p₂ = 2.000.
  - RR, RR′ and R′R′: m₂ ≡ 0.
  - Each power holds to 10⁻³ between δ = 10⁻⁴ and 10⁻³.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **The patterns (p₁, p₂) that occur:**

  | pattern | pairs |
  |---|---|
  | (δ², δ) | E with the 7/2 section; the 7/2 section with itself |
  | (δ⁴, δ²) | EE |
  | (0, δ²) | ER, ER′ |
  | (0, δ) | R or R′ with the 7/2 section |
  | (δ³, degenerate) | E with each 3/2 section; one 3/2 section with the 7/2 section |
  | degenerate (m₂ = m₃ at ω) | the rest of the pairs with a 3/2 section |
  | (0, 0) | RR, RR′, R′R′ |

- **The coefficients at δ = 10⁻⁴.**
  - E with the 7/2 section: m₂/m₃ = 1.8774 δ and m₁/m₃ = 52.87 δ².
  - R or R′ with it: m₂/m₃ = 1.8774 δ.
  - EE: m₂/m₃ = 26.43 δ² and m₁/m₃ = 698.8 δ⁴.
- **The dimension table** at 7/2 is 1 + Σu for every u, and at 3/2 it is Σu (W64). The collocation and χ + Σu agree on
  all 24.

**A consequence by arithmetic (after the run, from the read-outs; not a cell).**
- For E with the 7/2 section, m₁m₃/m₂² → 52.87/1.8774² = 15.000 (to 10⁻⁹) as τ → ω.
- For EE and for the 7/2 section with itself it is exactly 1: each is a scalar times a complex orthogonal matrix (W66).
- The data give m₁m₃/m₂² = 0.54 (up), 2.7 (down) and 0.081 (leptons).
- So near ω, no pairing with the full hierarchy m₁ ≪ m₂ ≪ m₃ gets the first generation's mass right in any sector. The
  pairings with m₁ ≡ 0 give none, and the other pairings with three nonzero masses (E with a 3/2 section) have
  m₂ ≈ m₃ near ω, so they have no second-generation hierarchy.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, GENESIS FK11; B1644's form, H-sec and H-pair′ are hypotheses).**
- **The three weights carry the three residual charges at ω.** −½ sits on one isotropic line, 3/2 on the real axis and
  7/2 on the other isotropic line: the three eigenlines of ω's order-3 stabiliser, one per weight.
- **One displacement from ω gives different powers to different pairings.**
  - E with the 7/2 section gives the full hierarchy δ² : δ : 1.
  - The weight −½ pairs give 0 : δ² : 1, and R with the 7/2 section gives 0 : δ : 1.
  - So the second generation's mass can be δ or δ² by the sector's pairing. The data's m_μ/m_τ ≈ 0.06 and
    m_c/m_t ≈ 0.004 are δ and δ² for δ ≈ 0.06, but that is context, not a fit.
- **The first generation does not come out.** Every pairing with a full hierarchy fixes m₁m₃/m₂² near ω, at 15 or 1,
  and the data's values are 0.08 to 2.7.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19. Whether one δ fits the second
  generations and the CKM is W69's question, rule first.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, corrected before any code; one run; all four cells as predicted). A WEAVE result
given Λ and the stated hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## W69. The weighted census: every pairing of the seven parameter-free sections across the weights −½, 3/2 and 7/2, fitted at one τ; none fits, and the best (χ² 100) uses W68's δ¹ and δ² pairings near ω (`the_weighted_census.py`; `W69_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **W68's opening.** It found that the weights put the sections on the three eigenlines at ω, so one displacement δ can
  give different sectors different powers. This arc ran W66's census over all 28 pairs of the seven sections: 21 952
  assignments, each fitted at one τ.
- **What would change the count.** A pass would let two observables fix τ and leave three as predictions. Because of
  the number of assignments, the rule made a pass a candidate only: the count drops only if a later arc confirms an
  unfitted prediction.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W69_RULE.md`, b7ed55153). It disclosed that the script was drafted before that commit; the script
  had not run.
- **The script** (ab979de70), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run (6.9 minutes); its output committed unread (f808fa136).
