## Reading: where the field comes from (the owner's "did we clarify the field? should we brainstorm?"; nothing computed)

The owner asked, after W69, whether the field (the Higgs) is clarified, and whether to brainstorm. This reading is the
brainstorm's first pass. The record was searched first, and nothing here is computed.

1. **What is clarified (W64–W69).**
   - **The field's direction among the generations,** read as a section of the matter's bundle (H-sec). It is fixed
     by end data and τ: three one-unit sections with integer pairings (W65), on the three eigenlines at ω by weight
     (W68).
   - **What fixes τ:** the weave's own quartic, at ω or at the cusp (W67).
   - **Why one generation is heavy:** at ω every such Higgs lies on one isotropic line (W65).
   - **How a hierarchy can grow:** a displacement from ω, with powers set by the pairing (W68).
   - **A no-go:** the parameter-free versions fail the data (W66 at 260, W69 at 100). Every version that fits has free
     numbers per sector, so the count never drops.
2. **What is not clarified.**
   - The field's existence, its charge, its coupling form (B1644) and its potential are hypotheses. So are "the Higgs is
     a section" (H-sec) and the coupling range (H-pair, H-pair′).
   - The first generation never comes out: m₁ = 0, or m₁m₃/m₂² fixed at 15 or 1 against the data's 0.08 to 2.7 (W68).
3. **What the record already rules out** (the repo, searched first).
   - **A field invariant under the weave's whole group is a scalar on M** (W49; C0, point 1; Schur). So the joint action
     alone gives no flavour. Flavour has to come from a state that breaks the group, which is τ (W50).
   - **A Higgs built on one member couples only to that member** (B1513's P5; main's B1444).
   - **Dimensional transmutation is blocked:** κ is conserved and does not run (B167; argued, not proved).
   - **27 ⊗ 27 holds no adjoint,** so mass operators are cubic (B974).
   - **Every isotropic coupling leaves one generation exactly massless** (Jacobi; B1302, B1644). B1362 shows small
     corrections cannot lift that, since the breaking must be of order one.
4. **Five candidate origins, as the record leaves them.**
   - **(a) The field as the cusp's condensate: the field from the weave's own end.**
     - The audit lane's stationary cusp condensate lifts 58 of 112 channels, keeps colour and breaks the weak SU(2),
       through the puncture's −1 in E₈ (the reading of W24–W32, point 3). It is the only object on record that does
       what the Higgs does.
     - It lives where GENESIS FK10's unit and τ's runaway already live: at the cusp.
     - If the field is this condensate, its existence and its charge are derived from an end, not hypothesized.
     - Owed: its mirror test, never run (is its mirror image gauge-equivalent?), and its direction in M. Does it pick
       the cusp's isotropic line, W64's leading order?
   - **(b) The weave-shared field: summed over every thread.**
     - The owner's rule and W56's standard require a field shared by all threads, and no arc has built one.
     - Schur makes its invariant part scalar on M. So it could fix the field's existence and normalization, while the
       flavour stays τ's.
     - It might also fix W65's per-sector ratio α/β, if each thread weights the sections differently. Never computed.
   - **(c) End data at both ends: the cusp and the puncture.**
     - The puncture carries the parity Higgs's boundary class (C0, point 3; GENESIS GAP2's kind), and P-108's parent map
       is owed.
     - The puncture's datum could fix α/β, turning W65's free number into a derived one.
     - The fork was deferred twice in the owner's rulings, and joint end data has never been computed.
   - **(d) The field from the matter: a bilinear of the fermions' sections.**
     - The QCD template (the 2026-10-13 research): a condensate, not an input.
     - On the weave, the cross product M ⊗ M → M is O-equivariant. So a bilinear of the fermions' weight 3/2 sections
       (with the uniform unit, W64) is a weight 3 section in ρ_R's family, carrying the fermions' own end data.
     - It would be a composite Higgs direction with no new datum. No arc has tried it.
   - **(e) The first generation's own mechanism.**
     - Every structure tried gives m₁ = 0 or a fixed ratio, and B1362 needs a breaking of order one.
     - The record's open lead is an instanton or exponential sector (L201). Nothing has been computed.
5. **What each would change in the count.**
   - (a) and (d) would derive the field's existence. They lower the count only if the field's flavour content comes
     with it.
   - (b) and (c) target the free numbers directly: W65's α/β per sector. They are the routes that can lower the count
     under the owner's rule.
   - (e) is needed in every case, for m_u, m_d and m_e.
6. **The seat's recommendation.**
   - (a) first: it is the only lead on record for the field itself, it sits where the weave's other cusp data already
     sit, and its test is concrete and owed.
   - Then (c), for the free numbers. (b) is likely to give only a normalization, by Schur.
   - The owner chooses.


## W70. The cusp condensate's charge map: under the unique admissible hypercharge, the audit lane's own cusp condensate is the neutral component of a Higgs doublet and leaves SU(3)_c × U(1)_em; the same hypercharge tells the three quaternion units apart (`the_cusp_condensate_charge_map.py`; `W70_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_cusp_condensate_charge_map_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- **The owner chose the cusp condensate** as the field's origin. The owner then asked how a field can be forced into
  existence, and `philosophy/P024_the_field_from_the_end.md` wrote the passage: a bare end admits no finite description,
  so the end must carry a field.
- **The audit lane's packets were read first.** Its stationary cusp condensate keeps colour and breaks the weak SU(2),
  but "no physical hypercharge is assigned".
- **This arc did the charge map,** in the lane's registered E₈ embedding. It is P024's first test.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W70_RULE.md`, cb5269d1e).
- **The script** (d76cafa3d), committed before its run. The run was held until P024 was written.
- **The run.** One run (9 seconds); its output committed before the read-out (f0133e132).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact; all five cells as predicted).**
- **N1, the branching (as predicted).**
  - The lane's SU(6) chain is a Cartan matrix, and the six weights have the standard Gram matrix.
  - The puncture acts on the 6 as (1, −1, 1, −1, 1, −1).
  - The 112 p-odd roots are exactly the weak doublets, and their 40 colour singlets carry the 20 weights of Λ³6, twice
    each.
- **N2, the admissible hypercharges (as predicted).** Of 469 candidates, exactly six pass the five Standard Model field
  conditions: the six permutations of (1/6, −5/6, 2/3) over the three quaternion blocks.
- **N3, the generations (as predicted).** Every admissible hypercharge gives the three blocks (b, a, ab) three different
  charges.
- **N4, the Higgs-like condensates (as predicted).** For each admissible hypercharge, exactly four of the 112 condensate
  roots are neutral colour-singlet doublets with |Y| = ½: two with Y = +½ and two with Y = −½.
- **N5, the lane's own condensate (as predicted).**
  - α = e₃ + e₅ (the lane's e₄ + e₆) has T₃ = +½ and SU(6) weight ws₀ + ws₁ + ws₄.
  - It is neutral, with Y = −½, under exactly one admissible hypercharge: (1/6, 2/3, −5/6) on the blocks (b, a, ab).
  - Under the other five its charge Q is −½, −1, 3/2, 2 or 1.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- **The unbroken Standard Model subgroup at α, under its neutral hypercharge:**
  - all six colour roots and both colour Cartans;
  - no weak root;
  - one more Cartan direction, ½(weak axis) + Y = T₃ + Y = Q.

  That is SU(3)_c × U(1)_em.
- **The union over the six admissible hypercharges:** 24 distinct roots are ever neutral and Higgs-like.

**What it shows (a READING; given the audit lane's registered embedding and its stationarity results, H-charge, and
Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **The cusp condensate can be the Higgs.** Under the unique admissible hypercharge (up to permuting the three
  quaternion units), the condensate class at the weave's own end contains fields with exactly the Higgs's quantum
  numbers. The lane's own condensate is the neutral component of a Y = −½ doublet, the quantum numbers of H_d, and it
  leaves SU(3)_c × U(1)_em. This is the first place the record finds a field with the Higgs's charges arising at the
  weave's end, rather than assumed.
- **P024's first test passes:** the forced end can carry the Higgs's charges.
- **Forced to choose, not forced which, twice over.**
  - Each admissible hypercharge has four neutral Higgs-like condensates.
  - The hypercharge itself is unique only up to permuting the three units, so choosing it breaks the weave's
    permutation of the units.
- **The same hypercharge tells the three quaternion units apart** (N3). In the gauge embedding the three units carry
  hypercharges 1/6, −5/6 and 2/3, so they are not three alike generations. The generations have to live in the matter's
  sections (W64–W69), not in the gauge embedding's 6.
- **What stays open.**
  - P024's second test: whether a field-free twist gaps every channel.
  - A global completion that keeps U(1)_em; the lane's published one leaves G₂.
  - The global mirror test.
  - The Yukawas and the scale.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19. A derived Higgs charge is structural, not
  a number among the 19.

**Post hoc: the hypercharge's normalisation (labelled; written after the read-out, at main's S122 ask "Main needs
the charge normalisation"; the script `the_cusp_condensate_charge_map_posthoc.py` committed before its run, fc7789ccd;
its output committed before the read-out, 735fb0513; W70's JSON unchanged).**
- **PN1.** All six admissible hypercharges have |Y|² = 7/3 against |T₃|² = ½, so k_Y = |Y|²/|T₃|² = 14/3. The Cartan
  norms and the sums over the 240 roots agree.
- **PN2, the control.** The standard SU(5)-type hypercharge −⅓(e₀ + e₁ + e₂) + ½(e₃ + e₄) lies in the lane's SU(6),
  with coefficients (1/6, 1/6, 1/6, 1/6, 1/6, −5/6) on the 6's weights.
  - It passes the five field conditions, and its k_Y is 5/3.
  - It is not block-scalar: it splits the ab block, so the weave's quaternion holonomies break it.
- **PN3, context (data, not a test).** With the Standard Model's one-loop running from M_Z (B915's PDG 2024 inputs),
  α_Y⁻¹/α₂⁻¹ is 3.32 at M_Z and falls as the scale rises. It reaches 5/3 at 1.0 × 10¹³ GeV, and 14/3 only at
  0.9 MeV, below M_Z.
- **Reading.** The flat quaternion background fixes the hypercharge's normalisation.
  - Keeping Y unbroken by the holonomies forces k_Y = 14/3; the standard 5/3 is broken.
  - If the embedding fixes the couplings' ratio at tree level, as one E₈ at level one does, then sin²θ_W = 1/(1 + k_Y)
    = 3/17 at the embedding's scale. With only the Standard Model's content above M_Z, that ratio is never reached.
  - So the lane's embedding needs extra matter below its scale, or no tree-level relation between the couplings. This
    is context for the lane's global completion, not a verdict.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run, held for P024; all five cells as predicted; exact). A WEAVE result given
the stated embedding and hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## W71. The field-free end: flat end data that gap every channel exist, the double twist, an R-holonomy or a weak Wilson line, and each gives up the dictionary, the R-symmetry or U(1)_em; with SU(3)_c × U(1)_em kept and gauge data only, the end stays bare (`the_field_free_end.py`; `W71_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **P024's second test.** The passage reads the field as forced by the end: a bare end admits no finite description,
  so the end must carry a source. It fails if a field-free datum can describe the end.
- **The audit lane's packets were read first** (the lane's head a3e744d99).
  - At the bare end, the 112 p-odd channels of every ordinary-spin slot are gapless.
  - The lane bounded nine R-twists, and its double twists leave no ordinary-spin slot.
  - Every all-channel gap on the lane's record uses a non-flat flux or the condensate.
  - Flat data other than the twists were not tested.
- **This arc is the census of field-free end data.** "Field-free" means flat, with no condensate and no gauge curvature
  at the end, and the forced quaternion holonomies kept.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W71_RULE.md`, 7375d296c).
- **The script** (b6bd5a00e), committed before its run. Fixed in review before that commit: the U(1)_em read-out had
  counted only the weak part. Hypercharge lies in the family SU(3), so a family Wilson line with a non-trivial
  peripheral holonomy breaks it too.
- **One failed launch.** The command wrapped the run in the time utility, which this container lacks,
  so the script never started and wrote nothing.
- **The run.** One run (2 seconds); its output committed before the read-out (8ee68df50).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact, with a numerical second route; all five cells as predicted).**
- **E1, the twists: the lane's table checked (as predicted).** With no flat datum added there are 448 gapless pairs
  untwisted, 224 for each single twist and 0 for each double twist, the same for all four spin lines.
  - Every gapless pair is an untwisted slot on a p-odd channel.
  - The drift is 0 exactly on the untwisted slots, and the twisted slots' threshold is 1/4.
- **E2, gauge Wilson lines (as predicted).**
  - The counts by peripheral class are:
    - 448 at q trivial;
    - 64 for a generic q in the family torus, and 64 in F₄'s torus;
    - 0 for q_w = −1, for a generic weak q, and for a generic q in the whole torus.
  - The 64 are 16 per slot: the colour-singlet weak doublets with one SU(6) index from each block. They have
    hypercharge 0 under every admissible Y.
  - The lemma checks: a non-commuting pair in SU(2) has zero commutant in su(2) (one exact pair, five random), and
    every admissible Y has zero weak component.
  - So every class with count 0 breaks U(1)_em.
- **E3, R-holonomies (as predicted).**
  - Untwisted, with generic R-holonomies in both factors: 0, with every gauge symmetry kept.
  - With the quaternion point placed in both R factors: 544, the 136 p-even channels in each slot.
  - A single twist with a generic R-holonomy in the factor it leaves whole: 0.
  - A double twist leaves no R factor whole, so it admits no peripheral R-holonomy.
- **E4, the model operator (as predicted).**
  - For k = 0, the smallest singular value of ∂_t + d + k e^t on [0, T] matches √((π/T)² + d²): 0.314, 0.157 and
    0.0785 for d = 0 at T = 10, 20 and 40, and 0.506 and 1.003 for |d| = ½ and 1 at T = 40.
  - For θ = ½ and ¼ it is 5.513 and 3.424 at every T: confined.
- **E5, the verdict (as predicted).**
  - Field-free data that gap every channel: the four double twists; generic R-holonomies, untwisted or with a single
    twist; and the three weak gauge classes.
  - No gauge-only class gaps every channel and keeps U(1)_em.
  - The script's list also names the double twists under each R class. A double twist admits no R-holonomy, so those
    entries are the double twists again.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- The forced holonomies' commutant has dimension 55 by characters (χ(−1) = 24, χ(g₄) = 28), as in the lane's module.
  It is F₄ × SU(2)_w.
- Hypercharge lies in the span of the family Cartans, for every admissible Y.
- A single twist with the quaternion point in the factor it leaves whole has 272 gapless pairs: the p-even channels of
  its two untwisted slots.

**A refinement of the rule's reading (written after the run; no cell changes).**
- The rule's prose said that with gauge data only and U(1)_em kept, 16 channels per untwisted slot stay gapless. The
  16 belong to the classes that keep the weak SU(2), and those break U(1)_em through hypercharge.
- Among the six gauge classes, only the trivial one keeps U(1)_em, and there all 448 pairs stay gapless.
- By the same lemma, the holonomies that keep SU(3)_c × U(1)_em lie in an abelian torus: the weak ones in U(1)_T₃, and
  the others in Y's centraliser within the family SU(3). So their peripheral holonomy is trivial.
- **So with SU(3)_c × U(1)_em kept, no flat gauge datum changes the end at all.**

**What it shows (a READING; given the audit lane's embedding and parent, and Λ, GENESIS FK11).**
- **P024's passage fails as written.** "A bare end admits no finite description" is confirmed: the forced data alone
  leave 448 gapless pairs. "So the end must carry a field" does not follow. Three kinds of field-free datum describe the
  end, and each gives something up:
  - the double twist changes the dictionary. It also doubles R, so a symmetric mass is allowed (the lane);
  - a generic R-holonomy breaks the parent's R-symmetry, and with it supersymmetry;
  - a weak Wilson line breaks the weak SU(2) and U(1)_em. In four dimensions it is the vacuum value of a gauge field's
    internal components, so it is a field after all.
- **What survives is a restricted form.** Three conditions:
  - the dictionary is Λ's (ordinary spin, untwisted);
  - the R-symmetry carries no peripheral holonomy;
  - SU(3)_c × U(1)_em is kept.

  Given all three, no flat datum describes the end, so the end needs a non-flat one. The cusp condensate is such a
  datum, and it keeps SU(3)_c × U(1)_em (W70).
- **The weak SU(2) cannot survive a describable end** in the gauge sector. Every gauge datum that gaps the end breaks
  it, and the condensate breaks it too.
- **None of the three conditions is the principle's.** Λ is a tagged working postulate (GENESIS FK11), the R-symmetry
  belongs to the lane's parent, and U(1)_em is data. The moves cannot decide the gap, since it depends only on the
  twist and the peripheral classes, which the moves preserve.
- **So whether the field is forced reduces to the dictionary,** GENESIS FK11's open question, as the hand and the
  count did before it.
- **The scope.** This is the lane's two-dimensional end. The four-dimensional surface operator, zero modes and
  chirality are not tested.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run after one failed launch; all five cells as predicted; exact, with a
numerical second route). A WEAVE result given the stated embedding, parent and Λ. 0 of 19.

## Reading: the end's field against main's B1649, by frame; and the surface frame's overlap (READING; nothing computed; 2026-10-14, after W71)

**Why.** After W71 the seat proposed W72: does B1649's massless first generation survive when the Higgs is odd under
the puncture, as the cusp condensate is? Reading for it first (main's B1642 and B1649, the audit lane's field map, W45,
W46, W64 and W68) showed that the question is not yet posed. Main's S122 relay assigns the seat a better-posed one.

**The frames disagree on the Higgs's puncture parity.**
- **In Λ's frame,** B1642's coupling pairs two zero modes F_p = C_p F, both valued in ρ_Q. A cubic invariant in
  ρ_Q ⊗ ρ_Q ⊗ V needs V ⊂ ρ_Q ⊗ ρ_Q = 1 + three characters, which is even under the puncture. So the Higgs's internal
  factor is even. That is B1649's S ∈ End(ℂ²), and its first-generation zero holds there.
- **In the audit lane's frame,** the puncture's −1 is the weak centre on every root (the lane's field map, a3e744d99).
  So a Standard Model Higgs is odd, the cusp condensate is odd (W70), and B1649's even S is a weak singlet or triplet.
  There the Standard Model's Yukawa must pair the odd stalk (Q, L) with an even one (u^c, d^c, e^c).
- **That frame's matter is not built.** W21's ordinary-spin zero modes grow like z^{−½} at the puncture. The lane's spin
  norm makes that borderline divergent, so in the lane's frame the triplet lives in the twisted forms, and the even
  stalk's zero modes for the singlets are not on either record. This is main's P-108 (the parent map).
- **So W72 as first proposed waits on P-108.** This settles one of P-129's four premises, the Higgs at the puncture, by
  frame. In Λ's frame the premise is forced; in the lane's frame it fails.

**The surface frame's overlap (main's S122 §2, assigned to this seat).**
- Main proposes that in the surface frame, where τ is integrated over, a hierarchy is an overlap integral over the
  surface, not a power of |q| at a chosen Im τ. The record has the surface frame's pieces:
  - the three chiral modes F_i, F₊, F₋ at weight 3/2 in ρ_R, one unit at the cusp each (W64's K5, W68);
  - the residue triplet R at weight −½;
  - B1644's coupling.
- **By hand, two exact facts.**
  - A dot product in ρ_R pairs only e_i with e_i and v₊ with v₋. Both pairs have exponents ≡ ¼ (mod 1), so every
    dot product of two ρ_R forms has the multiplier i.
  - B1644's coupling between two matter modes, F_αᵀ Y(a; R) F_β, has two dot products. So it carries η¹²'s multiplier,
    and its q-exponents lie in ½ + ℤ. It has no constant term, so no residue at the cusp: a holomorphic, cusp-localized
    Yukawa vanishes identically.
- **What remains is the Petersson pairing.** Written as a · G_αβ, the coupling defines a vector G_αβ of weight 5/2 in
  ρ_R ⊗ χ_i, where χ_i is the character with T ↦ i. The canonical four-dimensional overlap projects G_αβ onto the
  Higgs's own surface mode h of that type, Y_αβ = ⟨G_αβ, h⟩.
  - By W45's dimension formula, by hand, that space has dimension 1. It is cuspidal, since every exponent is
    non-zero.
  - The pairing converges after the x-integral, because h has no principal part.
  - So τ drops out and the shape of the Yukawa is parameter-free.
- **What it cannot give yet.** The three modes grow at the cusp (their units), so their Petersson norms diverge. Only
  basis-free facts about Y_αβ are physical without a normalisation, chiefly its rank, which decides B1649's zero in this
  frame. Mass ratios need the modes normalised, which needs a source at the cusp: P024's question again, now at the
  surface's end, and GENESIS FK10's unit.
- **W72, proposed:** compute the multipliers on the record's sections, the Higgs's surface mode, the Petersson Yukawa
  and its rank, rule first.

## W72. The surface frame's Yukawa: τ drops out and the first generation's zero is lifted (rank 3), but no mass ratio is defined, because the three modes are not normalisable (`the_surface_yukawa.py`; `W72_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_surface_yukawa_posthoc.py`, `the_surface_yukawa_posthoc2.py`)

**Why.**
- **Main's S122 relay, §2, assigns it.** In the surface frame τ is integrated over, so a hierarchy is an overlap
  integral, which neither record had.
- **The reading came first** (0c3082d4a). The puncture-odd Higgs version of W72 waits on main's P-108. In the surface
  frame, B1644's coupling has no residue at the cusp, and the canonical overlap is the Petersson pairing with the
  Higgs's surface mode.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W72_RULE.md`, edf50223a).
- **The script** (104ae600d), committed before its run. Pre-run review raised the product series' cap from exponent 14
  to 30.

## W73. The masses under the end's normalisation (H-end, chosen after W72 was read): one parameter-free set of ratios, 1 : 0.019 : 0.0004, which fails the data as a whole; the lightest generation is the v₊ mode, where R's unit sits (`the_end_normalisation.py`; `W73_RULE.md`)

  grow exponentially.

  sector's 0.0187) was seen before the rule. So this arc is post hoc in its choice of normalisation, and is recorded so
  wherever it is cited.

  above 5%, so the closeness is not significant.

**The read-outs (no prior).**

  m_e/m_τ = 2.8 × 10⁻⁴ (a factor 1.4). Against m_u/m_t = 7 × 10⁻⁶ it is a factor 53 too large.

which was chosen post hoc).**

## W74. The surface frame is CP-invariant: its Yukawas are real, so with one coupling θ̄ = θ (I-18's Yukawa half paid in this frame), and the same symmetry forbids the CKM phase (`the_surface_cp.py`; `W74_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **The strong phase θ̄ is one of the 19.** The record's I-18 reads it through the object's Chern–Simons 2-torsion.
  B1246 priced earning it at the dictionary and the Yukawa phase, since θ̄ = θ + arg det M.
- W72's Yukawa came out real to 10⁻¹³.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W74_RULE.md`, cf80a886f). It disclosed W72's stored Y and the hand argument.
- **The script** (4da98fa23), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run; its output committed before the read-out (9048aa9a4).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact where stated; all three cells as predicted).**
- **V1, the involution (exact).** For a section f, set f*(τ) = C · conj f(−τ̄) with C = diag(1, 1, −1).
  - C L C = L⁻¹ and C P C = P.
  - The S-phase condition holds for ρ_R at weights −½, 3/2 and 7/2, and for ρ′ at 5/2. As controls, it fails at ½
    and at 1.
  - C conj B[l] = B[l] on all three eigenlines.
  - So the map f ↦ f* preserves every space of sections the surface frame uses. With each one-unit space
    one-dimensional and normalised by a real coefficient, every section has real coefficients.
- **V2, the reality in the data.** W72's Y has |Im Y| at most 1.5 × 10⁻¹³ of its largest entry at Y₀ = 2.0, and
  9 × 10⁻¹⁷ at Y₀ = 1.25. h's stored coefficients have |Im| at most 3 × 10⁻³⁴.
- **V3, the consequences.** arg det(Y Y) = 2 × 10⁻¹³. Two identical sectors give the CKM matrix 1 to 8 × 10⁻¹⁶, and
  J = 0.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- det Y is 1.23 × 10¹⁰, positive, with eigenvalues −76 597, −57.4 and +2 788.
- W73's H-end form has eigenvalues −74 233, −28.7 and +1 438, with positive determinant.

**What it shows (a READING; given Λ, B1644's coupling, the surface frame with GENESIS FK10's unit, and H-surf).**
- **The surface frame is CP-invariant.** The modular curve's orientation reversal τ ↦ −τ̄, together with C, maps every
  section the frame uses to itself. Its Yukawas are therefore real.
- **So in this frame the Yukawa half of I-18's price is paid.** With the record's one coupling, arg det(M_u M_d) =
  2 arg det Y = 0, so θ̄ = θ. With different real couplings per sector it would be 0 or π. I-18 then rests on the
  dictionary alone, as it read before B1246. It is not earned: the dictionary half stands.
- **But the same symmetry forbids the CKM phase.** J = 0 here, and the data need J ≈ 3 × 10⁻⁵.
- **So whatever splits the sectors must also break CP.** By hand, for main's P-128, not computed:
  - a flat line at a half-period is CP-even and keeps J = 0;
  - a generic complex line breaks CP and can give J ≠ 0, but generically gives arg det M ≠ 0 as well.

  That is the strong-CP problem in this frame's terms: the phase the data need in J, and must not have in θ̄, both
  come from one place.
- **The count stays all 19 of the 19 free** (18 if I-18 is earned). 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; all three cells as predicted; exact where stated). A WEAVE result given
the stated hypotheses. 0 of 19.

## Reading: the field at the puncture is the fibre's own uniformization, and the two fields are one kind (READING; nothing computed; 2026-10-14, evening, after chat1's field relay)

*(Corrected 2026-10-14 by the audit lane's audit, dd2a54119; see W81. "One kind" is not earned. The lane's q, r are
spinor-valued, in ad(P) ⊗ S with S² = K, while an ordinary Higgs field lies in End(E) ⊗ K. The direct conversion
√Ω q fails D̄ by −iE/(4y²) at the same connection, and a change of connection that repairs it fails the lane's original
moment map and changes the curvature squared by 4. A map may still exist, but it has to be built. The lane's vacuum
equations below have the self-duality shape. That shape does not make its field Hitchin's.)*

**Why.** chat1's field relay reaches a Higgs field in Hitchin's sense: the threads' departure from the common point
while keeping the cusp. chat1's sealed test then found that it is not the audit lane's condensate. Reading the lane's
own proof of the condensate shows the two are one kind of object.

**The lane's condensate is Hitchin's uniformizing Higgs bundle, carried into E₈.**
- **Its vacuum equations are Hitchin's.** The lane's graded-condensate proof (`weave_graded_condensate_2026_10_08`)
  sets "F/dvol = −v/2 = −H/4 and [qhat, qhat dagger] = H/4", so that "all vacuum residuals Dbar q, Dbar r, [q, r], mu
  vanish". With r = 0 these are D̄q = 0 and F + [q, q†] = 0: the self-duality equations.
- **Its background is the uniformizing one.** The lane builds it so: "Induce the E8 bundle directly from S by the
  cocharacter v. A = s v and qhat = E/2", with S a spin line, S² = K, and (E, H = 2v, F) an sl₂ inside a regular A₂.
  That is Hitchin's construction of the uniformizing Higgs bundle, K^{½} ⊕ K^{−½} with Φ = E, pushed into E₈ through
  this sl₂.
- **So, through non-abelian Hodge, its flat connection is the fibre's own hyperbolic uniformization, composed with
  sl₂ → E₈.** At the cusp the lane's formulas are A_x = −H/(4y) and q = E/(2√y): the nilpotent-orbit model, a
  nilpotent residue with parabolic monodromy.

**chat1's flavour field is the same kind of object, elsewhere.** chat1's O1 to O4 (under H-RES, with no weight) read the
departure as a tame Higgs field whose residue at the puncture is nilpotent: a null a in M, with parabolic monodromy.
It lives on the common point's bundle, not the split uniformizing one (chat1's referee: the uniformizing bundle "lies
outside O1's space"), and in M, the weave's quaternion SU(2), not in the lane's A₂.

**What this says (READING).**
- **Both fields are one geometric fact: a cusp is a nilpotent residue** (chat1's O3). The electroweak field at the
  puncture (P024, W70, W71) is the fibre's uniformization through an sl₂ on puncture-odd roots. The flavour field
  (B1644 to B1649, chat1) is a nilpotent residue in the weave's own SU(2).
- **That also answers the owner's question in a new way.** The field at the edge is the shape of the edge itself: the
  hyperbolic cusp's uniformization, made into a gauge field. Every thread keeps the cusp, so every thread carries it.
- **What is not forced: the sl₂.** Which sl₂ of E₈ carries the uniformization decides whether the field is electroweak
  or flavour, puncture-odd or even. That is the dictionary again (P-108; GENESIS FK11). Forced to exist, not forced
  which.
- **A test this suggests (not run).** Do the lane's sl₂ (H = 2v) and the weave's quaternion SU(2) commute in E₈? If
  they do, both fields can be present at once, with product monodromy, and the join chat1 asks for becomes the pair.
  If they do not, one excludes the other. This is exact root arithmetic, for a rule.
- **The test, decided by hand (added the same evening; no script).** They do not commute.
  - The lane's sl₂ is built on β = e₃ + e₅ (W70's α) and γ = −e₄ − e₅, with H = 2v and v = e₃ − e₄, the weak axis.
  - β is odd under the puncture, so the quaternion Cartan acts on it (H_Q·β = ±1), and [H_Q, E_β] ≠ 0.
  - The puncture-odd part of the 248 is 2_Q ⊗ (a 56-dimensional module) (the audit lane's branching). So the
    electroweak condensate is a **doublet of the weave's SU(2)**, and the flavour field in M is its triplet.
  - *Refined 2026-10-14 (the lane's audit; W81).* Under the continuous SU(2) the 248 is 55 + 52·2 + 27·3 + 2·4, so
    "2_Q ⊗ 56" holds for Q8 only: the odd part is 52 doublets and 2 quartets. The root β itself lies in the doublets
    (W81, B4), so the sentence above holds for the condensate.
  - The two cannot sit as independent commuting uniformizations. They meet through invariants instead. The lowest one
    is the portal shape H†Φ_M H: two condensates with the flavour field between them. It is invariant under the weave's
    SU(2), the weak SU(2), hypercharge and the puncture. That is chat1's two-condensate option.
  - Whether the weave's action contains that term is the audit lane's action question.
- **0 of 19.**

## W75. chat1's scale check on this seat's instruments, and the field's size at the puncture: the weave's chiral threads carry Chern–Simons content in mirror pairs, and at weight zero the field at a genuine cusp is the uniformization, with pointwise size 1/(2R) and no free number (`the_puncture_scale.py`; `W75_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **chat1 sealed and ran a check of the scale** (`chat1/web-seat` @ c1986e33d → 28bb46c83), at the owner's question
  "isnt the scale there? the right scale needed for us? compatible with the rest?". Every cell held as sealed, and a
  labelled post hoc found the field's pointwise norm at a genuine cusp to be exactly ½.
- **The owner's rule is to verify load-bearing mathematics with the seat's own code.** Two parts carry weight here:
  - the mirror pairs, which re-scope B1012's k-blindness;
  - the local model, which decides whether the field at the puncture has a free size.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W75_RULE.md`, 9bc8f8eca). It disclosed chat1's whole arc and the seat's hand derivations.
- **The script** (b6a56e401), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run, 6.7 s; its output committed before the read-out (47fd112c3).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact where stated; all four cells as predicted).**
- **U1, the mirror pairs (a census of all 745 threads under chat1's stated rule).**
  - For every thread W, the bundle b++swap(W) is isometric to b++W (`is_isometric_to`, no failures).
  - The reversed copy's CS equals −CS(W) mod π² to 10⁻⁸, and CS(swap W) to 10⁻⁶, on all 745.
  - chat1's K1 count is reproduced: 690 threads have |CS| > 10⁻⁶.
  - *Instrument note:* for cusped manifolds SnapPy computes CS by one algorithm (Zickert's). The reversal runs it on
    the mirrored triangulation, and the isometry test is the independent leg.
- **U2, the local model by the ODE (fibre-level, exact).**
  - In the log frame, with θ = E₂₁ dz/z and h = diag(e^{u(L)}, e^{−u(L)}), Hitchin's equation reduces to
    u″ + e^{−2u} = 0. The reduction rests on ΔL = 0, |∇L|² = 4/|z|² and the bracket.
  - Its three branches solve it: L − L₀, sinh(a(L − L₀))/a and sin(a(L − L₀))/a.
  - L(λz) = L − log|λ|², so the shift L₀ is a rescaling of z.
  - h = diag(L, 1/L) and h = diag(L − ⅕, 1/(L − ⅕)) solve the full equation directly.
- **U3, the identification and the monodromy (fibre-level, exact).**
  - The uniformizing bundle, K^{½} ⊕ K^{−½} with Φ = E₂₁ dz, becomes θ = E₂₁ dz/z with h = diag(L/c, c/L) in the log
    frame.
  - It solves Hitchin's equation at c = 1 (curvature −4), and not at c = 2 (curvature −1, the control).
  - On every circle |z| = r, the flat connection's φ-component is iN with N = [[−1/L, −1/L²], [1, 1/L]], N² = 0 and
    N ≠ 0. So exp(−2πiN) has trace 2, and with the log frame's −1 the trace is −2.
- **U4, the size (fibre-level, exact).**
  - The residue's norm in h is 1/L.
  - On unit vectors of R²·4|dz|²/(|z|²L²), of curvature −1/R², the field's pointwise norm is 1/(2R): ½ at R = 1 and
    1 at R = ½, chat1's two numbers.
  - In the canonical coordinate z = e^{2πiw}, L = 4π Im w, and the cusp metric is |dw|²/(Im w)². The horocycle at
    height Y has length 1/Y.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- 55 threads are swap-symmetric, and none has CS = π²/2. So all 55 have CS = 0.
- U1 took 3.5 s.
- N at L = 1, 2 and 10 is [[−1, −1], [1, 1]], [[−½, −¼], [1, ½]] and [[−1/10, −1/100], [1, 1/10]].

**What it shows (a READING; under H-RES and §91's reading; no four-dimensional statement).**
- **B1012's k-blindness is a fact about one thread.**
  - The weave's chiral threads carry Chern–Simons content, in mirror pairs. A weave sum weighted by e^{ik CS} over W
    and swap(W) alike is real, but it depends on k.
  - So B1015's premise, "the object is provably blind to the quantized level k", holds for m004 and is to be scoped to
    it. A2 = c = 6σ stays external: k multiplies CS and σ multiplies the volume. Since |e^{−σVol + ik CS}| =
    e^{−σVol}, chat1's K3 makes the weave weighted by its whole action diverge at every k and σ.
- **B1224's bit survives at the weave's level.** B1224 found that amphichirality makes CS 2-torsion: the β-odd
  numbers (θ_QCD, δ_CKM, δ_PMNS) are a bit, not a value. The weave is not amphichiral thread by thread, but its
  content comes in mirror pairs. So any weave sum taken over W and swap(W) alike is real, with phase 0 or π. W74 found
  the same in the surface frame (J = 0). A nonzero J or δ needs something that is not mirror-symmetric.
- **At a genuine cusp the field has no free size** (fibre-level).
  - At weight zero the local model is unique up to a rescaling of z, and the cusp's parabolic generator fixes that
    rescaling (z = e^{2πiw} with w ↦ w + 1).
  - Its pointwise size is half the inverse curvature radius: the uniformization's own size. Three routes agree:
    chat1's post hoc (½), the audit lane's q̂ = E/2, and U4.
  - *Caveat (2026-10-14).* The lane's q̂ is spinor-valued in its (1,1) parent (q = cE/√y), while a Higgs field is a
    one-form (cE/y). The agreement is of magnitudes, not yet of types. The lane's sealed audit (e0f51c494) tests the
    identification, and §91's reading waits on it.
  - *Settled (2026-10-14, the lane's audit dd2a54119).* The lane's q, r live in ad(P) ⊗ S with S² = K, and an ordinary
    Higgs field in End(E) ⊗ K. The size-preserving conversion fails D̄ at the same connection, and changing the
    connection breaks the lane's moment map. So the three routes agree on a magnitude across two types, and the
    identification is not earned (W81).
- **The record's κ = −2 is the half-canonical frame's −1 on a unipotent monodromy.** The trace −2 is forced for the
  punctured torus, since the commutator does not depend on the lift. U3 shows it is what the nilpotent model gives in
  the frame (dz)^{±½}. This adds to §91's reading: the field at the puncture is the uniformization, and its sign at
  the puncture is the record's.
- **So the electroweak condensate's size is the fibre's unit.** If the fibre's curvature radius is A1's ℓ, its size is
  ℓ's, and B1015 says no dimensionless number flows from A1. The SM specification ledger already types v as box C,
  the dimensionful numbers the reader supplies (B1226). W75 gives that typing a mechanism in this frame.
- **A hierarchy would be a depth.**
  - The residue's norm is the horocycle's length over 4π: e^{−d}/(4π) at a distance d from the horocycle of length 1.
    That is a warp factor, as in Randall–Sundrum, with the cusp as the throat.
  - The electroweak hierarchy would need d = ln(M_Pl/v) ≈ 37 to 38, with the reduced or the full Planck mass, between
    where the observer's units are set and where the field's are. The rule's hand figure, d ≈ 39, is corrected here.
  - Nothing on record forces a depth. GENESIS FK10's unit sits at the modular cusp, not at the puncture (§90).
- **The count.** The Standard Model's one dimensionful number (v) would be the unit, not a new free number. The
  dimensionless Higgs number (the quartic, m_H/v) stays free. 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; all four cells as predicted; exact where stated). U1 is a census of
every thread under a stated rule, and its claim (the mirror pairs) is about all threads at once: weave-type, as a
verification of chat1's sealed census. U2 to U4 are fibre-level: the shared fibre's end, the same on every thread,
not a quantity of the joint action. 0 of 19.

## W76. The weave's β-odd bit: every mirror-symmetric thread to length 16 has Chern–Simons exactly 0, so wherever the weave carries B1224's bit, the bit is 0 (`the_weaves_bit.py`; `W76_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **The owner's order is "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers".** The SM specification ledger types the
  β-odd numbers (θ_QCD, δ_CKM, δ_PMNS) as box D. B1224 proved them a bit on m004, and the ledger registers the bit
  reading as L192, never tested.
- **W75 carried the bit to the weave,** as the reality of mirror-paired sums. A 2-torsion CS is 0 or π²/2. W75's
  read-out found 0 on all 55 mirror-symmetric threads to length 12, while B1224's census had π²/2 on half its
  amphichiral manifolds.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W76_RULE.md`, 64a460512; one line rewrapped before any code, c77645d54).
- **The script** (08572e315), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run, 13.7 s; its output committed before the read-out (ead34fd17).

**The read-out (COMPUTED; all four cells as predicted).**
- **B0, the instrument.** 8 798 threads (every Lyndon word of length 2 to 16), all geometric, no exception. b++LR has
  |CS| = 3 × 10⁻¹⁵.
- **B1, the 2-torsion (control).** 248 threads are mirror-symmetric, and every one has 2CS ≡ 0.
- **B2, the bit.** All 248 have CS = 0. None has π²/2. The largest |CS| among them is 6 × 10⁻¹⁴.
- **B3, no stray torsion.** No thread that is not mirror-symmetric has 2CS ≡ 0, nor |CS| < 10⁻⁶.

**The read-outs (no prior).**
- Mirror-symmetric threads occur only at even length: 1, 1, 3, 6, 15, 29, 65 and 128 at lengths 2 to 16.
- By kind: 210 have swap(W) equal to a rotation of W reversed, 12 a rotation of W itself, and 26 both.

**What it shows (a READING).**
- **Wherever the weave carries a β-odd bit, the bit is 0.** The weave's own mirror-symmetric threads never carry
  π²/2, to length 16. This is a census, not a proof; the seat found no proof by hand.
  - *Corrected 2026-10-14 by chat1's T-SIGN* (sealed at 50352be3a; every cell held). The bit is exactly the sign of
    the monodromy: 120 of 120 swap-symmetric minus threads carry π²/2 (to length 14), and flipping the sign shifts CS
    by exactly π²/2 (224 of 224 words to length 10). So the 0 found here is the positive weave's. It is GENESIS GM5d's
    positivity, a convention of the grammar, not a selection the weave makes. If the sign move (GENESIS GM5b) is in
    the weave, the bit is the sign. θ̄ = 0 below is then "given positivity" as well as given L192's dictionary.
  - *Noted 2026-10-14 by W86 (from the scale auditor's report):*
    - Main's B1479 (PROVED, 2026-10-06) had the amphichiral half first, to length 12. On 34 of 34 words the + state
      sits at CS = 0 and the − state at ¼, and the two signs share a volume on all 379 words. T-SIGN extends it to
      length 14 and adds the shift on every word.
    - "GM5b" above is this branch's GENESIS v1.10 filing. Main's GENESIS v1.14 (B1482) makes the sign a move of its
      own, as this dossier's header already says.
- **For θ̄ (I-18), not earned.** In the surface frame with one coupling, arg det M = 0 (W74). If θ is read as the
  weave's bit (L192's dictionary), θ̄ = 0, which the data allow. Two things are still owed:
  - that dictionary (B813 refuted the value dictionary; the bit dictionary is untested);
  - a sector split (main's P-128) that gives J ≠ 0 and keeps arg det(M_u M_d) = 0.
- **For δ_CKM, the bit reading fails the data.** δ_CKM is about 1.14 rad, neither 0 nor π. The CKM phase is not a bit
  of the weave, and must come from what splits the sectors (W74).
- **So the strong-CP problem, in this frame, is one test.** Does the split that gives J leave arg det(M_u M_d) at 0?
  That is a cell for main's P-128 seal.

**Post hoc (labelled; `the_weaves_bit_posthoc.py`, committed before its one run).** chat1's root-share check stores
Vol and CS for all 31,040 b++ threads to length 18, on its own run (`chat1/web-seat` @ f8fe26015).
- **PH1.** On that data the threads with CS = 0 are exactly the 514 mirror-symmetric ones (266 at length 18). None has
  π²/2, and no other thread has 2CS ≡ 0. So W76's census reaches length 18.
- **PH2.** The two seats' CS values agree exactly on the 8 798 common threads. Both used SnapPy 3.3.2, so this is a
  reproduction across seats, not a second algorithm.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; all four cells as predicted). A weave census of thread quantities: every
thread under a stated rule, with a claim about all mirror-symmetric threads at once; not a joint-action quantity.
0 of 19.

## Reading: the count after the field, by kind (READING; nothing computed; 2026-10-14, after W76)

**Why.** The owner's order: "we look what fixes τ then we count free numbers". The field now has its existence (given
Λ), its charge (W70), its coupling's shape (W72), its CP (W74) and its size (W75). This reading counts the Standard
Model's nineteen numbers again, by the kinds of the SM specification ledger (B1226), against what the weave's frames
now say. The arcs cited win where this summary disagrees.

| kind (B1226) | numbers | what the weave's frames say now | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A, mixing angles | 3 CKM angles | One coupling for every sector gives V = 1 (B1649 on one fibre; W73). The CKM needs a four-dimensional family in every frame (W44). | free; the sector split is owed (P-128) |
| B, gauge couplings | g₁, g₂, g₃ | Scale-anchored, the reader's (B811). W70's admissible hypercharge has k_Y = 14/3, never reached above M_Z (post hoc, context). | free |
| C, charged-fermion masses | 9 | The ratios: parameter-free in the surface frame (W72, rank 3) but not normalisable; one set under H-end (post hoc) fails the data (W73); the τ frames fail (W53, W66). The scales are the reader's. | free |
| C, the Higgs's vacuum value | v | In the uniformization reading the condensate's size is the fibre's unit (W75): the reader's unit, no new number. The hierarchy would be a depth, not forced. | the unit |
| C, the Higgs's mass | m_H (the quartic) | Not computed. The quartic needs the weave's action (§92's ask to the audit lane). | free |
| D, the strong phase | θ̄ | The weave's bit is 0 wherever it is carried (W76), and arg det M = 0 with one coupling (W74). | 0, given L192's dictionary and a split that keeps arg det M = 0; not earned |
| D, the CKM phase | δ_CKM | The weave gives only a bit, and the data's 1.14 rad is not one. It must come from the split, which must break CP (W74). | free |

**What it says.**
- **Still 0 of 19.** No number is earned.
- **The nearest is θ̄.** It needs two owed things and no free input: L192's dictionary, and a sector split that keeps
  arg det(M_u M_d) at 0 while giving J ≠ 0. Main's P-128 seal can test the second directly.
  - *Corrected 2026-10-14 (chat1's T-SIGN):* the weave's bit is 0 because of GENESIS GM5d's positivity, which is a
    convention, so θ̄ = 0 here is also "given positivity". *(Noted by W86: main's B1479 had this first, to length 12.)*
- **One number has become the unit.** v's size is the fibre's own, if the uniformization reading holds. That moves it
  from "unsourced" to the reader's unit, which is the box the ledger already gave it. It does not count as derived.
- **Everything else waits on one input: what splits the sectors.** With one coupling the frame is rigid, with no free
  number, and it fails the data (V = 1, J = 0, one mass pattern). The split is where the mixing, the CKM phase and
  the three mass patterns must come from, and main has sealed none of it yet. That is why the blind test matters.

## W77. The audit lane's odd complementary form, reviewed on this seat's instruments: its brackets, spin connection, cross form and coefficient norms reproduced exactly, and its identity and inequality (1) hold on 40 numerical test sections (`the_odd_form_review.py`; `W77_RULE.md`)

**Why.**
- **The audit lane asked for it.** Its relay `PHYSICAL_TO_GENESIS_MAIN_SM_2026-10-14_ODD_DOMAIN_FORM.md` (to main and
  this seat) reports a conditional odd form estimate and asks for "an independent analytic review" and a receiving
  read or reproduction. The seat had mis-rowed that push as carrying no relay to it (corrected in the ledger, §95).
- **The estimate is on the path to the four-dimensional index,** the duty W71 left to the lane. It is not an index or a
  count.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W77_RULE.md`, in ebcf56967). It disclosed what was read and the hand derivations, with one
  sign in R2 settled by hand before the commit (the lane's convention gives the spin connection's linear part as
  +½ Σ (div E_j) c_j).
- **The script** entered 90457883a, a commit that also carried a wording fix (its message says so), before its run.
- **The run.** One run, 26 s; its output committed before the read-out (ec2e60d9e).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact where stated; all five cells as predicted).**
- **R1, the brackets (exact).** By the structure equations from the lane's coframe, the only nonzero brackets are the
  four the lane states, with their partners: [E₁, E₂] = −E₁/ℓ, [E₂, E₃] = E₃/(2ℓ), [E₁, E₄] = −E₃/ℓ and
  [E₂, E₄] = −E₄/(2ℓ).
- **R2, the spin connection (exact).** By the Koszul formula, Σ c_i ω_i = −c₂/(2ℓ) + c₁c₃c₄/(4ℓ), with nothing else.
  Its linear part is +½ Σ (div E_j) c_j, with div E₂ = −1/ℓ. So the lane's "density" term is the spin connection's
  trace part, and its mixed term is the cubic part.
- **R3, the cross form (exact).** In the chiral gamma representation (c₁ = iγ₄, c₂ = iγ₁, c₃ = iγ₂, c₄ = iγ₃),
  normal-ordered with R1's brackets, {V, H} = K₃E₃ + K₄E₄ with the lane's K₃ and K₄, for every β. There is no
  second-order term, no horizontal derivative and no constant.
- **R4, the coefficients (exact).** K₃ and K₄ are skew-Hermitian and anticommute with Γ_V = ic₃c₄. K†K has eigenvalues
  {1/ℓ², 1/ℓ², 0, 0}, so each norm is 1/ℓ.
- **R5, the test sections (numerical; ℓ = A = 1).** On 20 random smooth sections (two or three Fourier modes, Gaussian
  profiles away from the box's edges, the physical density ℓ²A/y²) at β = 0 and ¾:
  - the identity ‖Dψ‖² − ‖Vψ‖² − ‖Hψ‖² = ⟨ψ, Fψ⟩ holds to 3 × 10⁻¹⁰ relative on all 40;
  - inequality (1) and the cross bound hold on all 40.

**The read-outs (no prior).** The smallest margin of (1) is 38.7‖ψ‖², and the largest cross ratio
|⟨ψ, Fψ⟩|/(2√2‖ψ‖‖Vψ‖) is 0.15. These sections are smooth bumps, not near-extremal ones, so (1) is far from tight on
them. This is a consistency check, not a stress test.

**What it shows (a READING; the lane's hypotheses throughout).**
- **The lane's algebra is reproduced on this seat's instruments:** the brackets by a second route, the spin connection,
  the cross form in a second representation, and the coefficient norms. The inequality chain to (1) was also checked
  by hand.
- **Its integration by parts holds numerically in the physical norm,** on smooth sections away from the puncture.
- **Not reviewed:**
  - the domain claims (the core, the critical profiles, the closure). They were read, and no gap was found as written;
  - the upstream gap constant c, the bound on [D, P], compactness and the global bundle;
  - the half-pole component. The test sections avoid the puncture, so it was not exercised.
- **So the estimate stands as the lane wrote it, conditional on the same hypotheses,** now with a second seat's
  algebra and numbers under it. It is still not an index, a kernel or a count.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; all five cells as predicted; exact where stated). A review of another
seat's estimate: neither a weave nor a thread result. 0 of 19.

## W78. Main's B1637 ask: every structure with a sector at weight 3, searched up to Im τ = 10. This seat does not reproduce main's (5, 7, 3) fit, and a labelled post hoc finds the two seats' weight-3 models differ in the up sector (`the_weight_three_refined.py`; `W78_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_weight_three_refined_posthoc.py`)

**Why.**
- **Main asked for it.** Main's B1637 fits T ⊗ T (5, 7, 3) at χ² = 1.86 near τ ≈ 0.36 + 6.79i, and (3, 7, 7) at 6.72.
  This refutes W54's sentence "every structure with a sector at weight 3 fails". Main asked the seat to check (5, 7, 3)
  independently.
- **W54's box stopped at Im τ = 6,** so it could not have found main's fits at any grid resolution. This arc searches
  all sixteen weight-3 structures in the window Im τ ∈ [5, 10], with W54's model unchanged.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W78_RULE.md`, a73cf802b). Its control's S-check was moved near the unit circle before any code
  (65873c510, disclosed): at the far points −1/τ lies below the forms' series domain.
- **The script** (ba09a2c3f), committed before its run.
- **The run.** One run, 74 minutes on four processes; its output committed before the read-out (a330373d4).
- **A labelled post hoc** (2d36be065, before its run; output ba82e2573): this seat's model at main's τ, and each sector
  alone at weight 3.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; N0, N3 and N4 as predicted; N1 and N2 FAILED as worded).**
- **N0, the control (holds).** The shape observables change by at most 1.4 × 10⁻⁹ under τ → τ + 1 at the far points,
  and 1 × 10⁻¹² under τ → −1/τ near the unit circle.
- **N1 and N2, (5, 7, 3) (FAILED).** The grid route reaches χ² = 190.7 at τ ≈ −0.49997 + 5.48i, against main's 1.86.
  The minimum sits at the window's x-bound, which the bounded search cannot cross although x = ±½ are identified.
  Refined from main's τ with the nearest grid point's α, the search stays at main's τ at 825.6.
- **N3, (3, 7, 7) (holds).** 915.8 at τ ≈ 0 + 8.19i: it does not fit here. Main has 6.72.
- **N4 (holds).** No structure with a quark sector at weight 3 fits in the window (all 772 to 1122).
- **Every structure's minimum (a = 0.10).** (5, 5, 3) 121.6; (7, 5, 3) 136.6; (7, 7, 3) 164.3 (main 14.7); (5, 7, 3)
  190.7 (main 1.86); every structure with a quark at weight 3 from 772.8 to 1121.7. None fits.

**The post hoc (labelled; it decides between a missed fit and a different model).**
- **At main's τ, with 200 starts per part** (the quark and lepton parts separate at fixed τ):
  - (5, 7, 3): the quark part reaches 508 and the leptons at weight 3 reach 100, a total of 608 against main's 1.86;
  - (3, 7, 7): the quark part reaches 1036, with the leptons at 0.
- **Each sector alone at weight 3, τ free in Im τ ∈ [4, 8], 60 starts:**
  - the down quarks fit exactly (5 × 10⁻³⁰, Im τ 5.11; main 5.09);
  - the leptons fit exactly (2 × 10⁻²⁹, Im τ 5.90; main 5.85);
  - the up quarks do not: the best is 1.78, pinned at the window's edge Im τ = 8. Main fits them exactly at Im τ 6.66.
  - An exact single-sector fit at weight 3 is a two-dimensional family (the complex ratio of two coefficients and τ,
    against two mass ratios), so the small d and e differences mean nothing. The up sector's failure in the window
    does.

**What it shows (a READING).**
- **The seat cannot reproduce main's weight-3 fits, and the reason is not only the search.** At main's own τ, heavy
  multi-start fits of this seat's model stay far above main's χ². The up sector alone at weight 3 has no exact fit
  below Im τ = 8 here, where main's has one at 6.66.
- **So the two seats' weight-3 models differ,** most visibly in the up sector, while the weight-5 and weight-7 fits
  agree (W54 and B1637 find the same exact fits near Im τ 3.4). That is a fact about the instruments, to be resolved
  between the seats before either weight-3 statement is banked.
  - *Corrected the same day by W79.* The models are the same representation and the same space. The difference is
    main's evaluator, whose precision falls like |η|⁻¹⁸ near the cusp. The conjugation hypothesis below is refuted
    (W79's C1).
- **A hypothesis, not tested:** a convention difference in the weight-3 forms, for example T against its conjugate. That
  exchanges the q-exponents λ ↔ 1 − λ, and would move the up sector's exact fits deeper toward the cusp, as seen here.
- **What stands.** W54's sentence holds on this seat's model in the window to Im τ = 10. Main's refutation holds on
  main's model. Which model is the record's is the question for main. Every fit on either model has P ≥ 21, so the
  count is unchanged: all 13 flavour numbers free under this ansatz. 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, with one disclosed correction before any code; one run; N0, N3 and N4 as
predicted; N1 and N2 failed as worded; a labelled post hoc). A WEAVE result on the ruled branch, given Λ. 0 of 19.

## W79. The two seats' weight-3 models compared: the same representation and the same space, and main's forms lose precision near the cusp like |η|⁻¹⁸, so its fits near Im τ 6.7–6.8 are most likely numerical artifacts (`the_two_seats_weight_three.py`; `W79_RULE.md`; post hoc `the_two_seats_weight_three_posthoc.py`, `the_two_seats_weight_three_posthoc2.py`)

**Why.**
- **W78 left the seats apart.** This seat's model does not reproduce main's weight-3 fits (B1637), even at main's τ.
  Main's code is in the repository, so the seat compared the two models directly.
- **A correction owed first.** In its report after §97 the seat said plain conjugation could not explain the gap. That
  was wrong: the forms for ρ̄ have q-exponents 1 − λ, a different space. The rule says so.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W79_RULE.md`, 3fadfa197). One disclosed correction came before any code (73ce9d21a): main's
  import chain needed its whole verification tree.
- **The script** (fe4f48f39). Its first launch failed before any science: `git archive` ran from the dossier
  directory, the pathspecs matched nothing, and git exited 128. It was fixed to run from the repository root
  (3151f4819). An intermediate `top` pathspec, which git rejected, was never committed.
- **The run.** One run, 29 s, with main's code extracted read-only (2 588 files at 60c5470ae). Its output was
  committed before the read-out (cf9569fda).
- **Two labelled post hocs,** each committed before its run: 490c4df68 with output d1ce34753, and c145c88df with
  output 23eb03fb2.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; C3 as predicted; C1 and C2 FAILED as worded).**
- **C1, the triplets (FAILED as worded).** This seat's ρ_T is isomorphic to main's ρ_θ, not to its conjugate. The
  T-exponents are equal (⅛, ⅜, ⅞), and so are the characters at all seven words. The conjugation hypothesis of §97 is
  refuted.
- **C2, the coupling action (FAILED as worded, on the instrument).**
  - This seat's forms obey Y(τ + 1) = X Y Xᵀ to 10⁻¹⁵.
  - Main's individual basis forms miss ρ^{−T} Y ρ^{−1} by a relative 2.0, and every phase variant by at least 0.08.
  - The two 9-dimensional exponent multisets are equal.
  - Main's basis is not T-diagonal, so a form-by-form check was the wrong instrument for it (post hoc below).
- **C3, the decisive fit (holds).** At main's single-sector τ, this seat's model fits the down quarks (2 × 10⁻⁹) and the
  leptons (0) exactly, and not the up quarks (116).

**The first post hoc (labelled).**
- **The same space.** The intertwiner P (P ρ_θ = ρ_T P) is unique and unitary up to scale (10⁻¹⁵). Carried by
  Y ↦ P̄ Y P̄ᵀ, main's weight-3 span equals this seat's: the principal angles are 3 × 10⁻¹¹ at τ = 0.1 + 3i and
  6 × 10⁻¹⁴ at −0.2 + 1.7i, but 6.5 × 10⁻⁴ at 0.3 + 6.6i. The control carrying, P Y Pᵀ, gives angles near π/2.
- **The phases.** Main's forms follow the −1 phase only roughly, with errors of 0.08, 0.12 and 0.28 at weights 3, 5
  and 7. This seat's forms follow +1 exactly.

**The second post hoc (labelled; it tests a hypothesis read from main's construction).**
- **The hypothesis.** Main's forms are η⁻¹⁸ times polynomials in the theta squares, with coefficients from a
  double-precision SVD. Near the cusp the polynomials cancel down to the size of η¹⁸, so their error should grow like
  10⁻¹⁶ |η|⁻¹⁸ ≈ 10⁻¹⁶ e^{(3π/2) Im τ}.
- **PH3.** The angle between the two spans at Im τ = 2, …, 8 is 2.5 × 10⁻¹³, 2.8 × 10⁻¹¹, 3.1 × 10⁻⁹, 3.5 × 10⁻⁷,
  3.9 × 10⁻⁵, 4.3 × 10⁻³ and 0.48. Its slope in log against Im τ is 4.7109, against 3π/2 = 4.7124. The angle is a
  steady fifth of 10⁻¹⁶ |η|⁻¹⁸.
- **PH4.** At main's up-sector τ (−0.0737 + 6.6640i), main's own forms, built on this machine with 300 starts, fit the
  up masses only to χ² = 191, not main's 4 × 10⁻²³. The lightest ratio stays at 1.6 × 10⁻⁴, against the data's
  7.3 × 10⁻⁶. Projected onto this seat's span, the fit moves by 7 × 10⁻⁴ relative and scores 118.

**What it shows (a READING).**
- **The two seats fitted the same model.** The triplets are isomorphic and the weight-3 spaces are equal wherever both
  evaluators are accurate. W78's reading, that the models differ, is corrected.
- **Main's evaluator loses precision near the cusp exactly as |η|⁻¹⁸.** By Im τ = 6.7 it keeps two or three digits,
  and by 8 none. This seat's q-series evaluator has no such division, and agrees with main's to 10⁻¹³ where main's is
  accurate.
- **So main's weight-3 fits near Im τ 6.7–6.8 are most likely numerical artifacts.** Those are (5, 7, 3) at 1.86,
  (3, 7, 7) at 6.72, and the up sector alone at 6.66. Each needs a mass ratio near 10⁻⁵ from forms good to about
  10⁻³ there, and main's own code does not reproduce the up-sector fit at main's τ on this machine. The same applies
  to main's masses-only 20.6 near Im τ 7.1. This is a reading from the evidence, not a proof: main can settle it by
  re-running those fits with an evaluator that does not divide by η¹⁸ (q-series of the forms, or exact coefficients
  in high precision).
- **What stands then.** W53's exclusion and W54's sentence "every structure with a sector at weight 3 fails" stand on
  this seat's model up to Im τ = 10 (W78). The exact fits at weights 5 and 7 near Im τ 3.4 stand in both searches,
  where main's evaluator is good to about 10⁻⁹. The count is unchanged. 0 of 19.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, one disclosed correction before code; one failed launch disclosed; one run; C3 as
predicted; C1 and C2 failed as worded; two labelled post hocs). A comparison of two seats' instruments for one ansatz.
0 of 19.

  sector alone does fit exactly at weight 3 on precise forms, deeper in the cusp than main found it. W78's "no exact
  fit below Im τ = 8" agrees.
- **The read-out (7, 7, 3).** Main's 14.7 sat at Im τ 5.51, where main's evaluator keeps about four digits (W79). On
  precise forms the same optimizer reaches 202.

**What it shows (a READING; on the ruled branch, given Λ, under main's ansatz).**
- **B1637's weight-3 fits were artifacts of main's evaluator.** All of them fail on precise forms under main's own
  optimizer and seeds. The exact fits at weights 5 and 7 near Im τ 3.4 stand in both searches.
- **So B1637's corrections to W53 and W54 are withdrawn on this seat's side, pending main.**
  - W53's masses-only exclusion stands at 163, this seat's minimum; main's optimizer on precise forms reaches only 319.
  - W54's "every structure with a sector at weight 3 fails" stands for the 13.
  - The geometry's weight is excluded for every sector again. Each note carries a dated withdrawal.
- **Two cautions.**
  - Both searches are local, 60 starts each. A failure of the search is not a proof that no fit exists.
  - An exact fit of one sector alone is not a fit of a structure, since every sector must share one τ. The up sector's
    exact fit at 8.19 does not reopen any structure.
- **For main's selection principle** (its item 2 to chat1): the near-cusp fits it cites above Im τ ≈ 5 are gone. The

- **The count is unchanged:** every fit has P ≥ 21. 0 of 19.
