seat's fits on this seat's evaluator, under that seat's own optimizer. 0 of 19.

## W81. The audit lane's typed-field relay checked: under the continuous SU(2) the 248 is 55 + 52·2 + 27·3 + 2·4, and the electroweak condensate's root lies in the doublets (`the_lane_su2_branching.py`)

**Why.** The audit lane's audit of §91–§93 and W75 has landed (dd2a54119, its relay of 2026-10-14 to this seat, chat1
and main). Two of its points correct this seat's readings.
- **The types.** The lane's q, r live in ad(P) ⊗ S with S² = K, and an ordinary Higgs field lives in End(E) ⊗ K.
  - The size-preserving conversion √Ω q fails D̄ by −iE/(4y²) at the same connection.
  - Changing the connection repairs that equation, but fails the lane's original moment map and changes the curvature
    squared by 4.
  - So equal coefficients ½ do not earn the identification. The lane cites Hitchin (arXiv:2404.12981, §§1–3,
    Prop. 2) and Xie and Yonekura (arXiv:1310.0467, §2.1). This seat has not re-derived it; it is recorded as the
    lane's computation.
- **The doublet.** The lane replays the full character under the continuous SU(2): 55 singlets, 52 doublets, 27
  triplets and 2 quartets. So §92's "2_Q ⊗ 56" is a statement about Q8 only.

This arc checks the second point with this seat's own E₈ code, and asks whether the condensate's root is a doublet.

**The order.** The script, with its predictions and priors in the docstring, was committed before its one run
(e178ff2ff). Its output was committed before the read-out (23f217074). The run took 0.4 s.

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact; B1, B2 and B4 as predicted; B3 FAILED as worded).**
- **B1 holds.** The H_Q weights of the 248 give exactly 55 singlets, 52 doublets, 27 triplets and 2 quartets. That is
  an SU(2) of embedding index 3: the 2 of ρ_Q times 1₃, inside the lane's SU(6). The lane's character reproduces.
- **B2 holds.** The odd part has dimension 112: 56 copies of Q8's 2, and 52 doublets plus 2 quartets of the continuous
  SU(2).
- **B3 FAILED as worded.** H_Q(β) = +1 in this seat's convention, against the lane's −1. The magnitude agrees. The sign
  is the orientation of H_Q (this seat's (1, −1, 1, −1, 1, −1) on the lane's weights of the 6), which the doublet
  question does not see.
- **B4 holds.** For β = e₃ + e₅ (0-indexed; W70's α) no path E_iE_j or F_iF_j runs through roots, so
  E_Q² e_β = F_Q² e_β = 0. The same holds for −β. So β lies in the doublets. The quartets' extreme weights sit on the
  four roots ±(e₃ + e₆) and ±(e₄ + e₆).

**What it shows (a READING).**
- **§92's sentence holds for the condensate, not for every odd direction.**
  - The electroweak condensate's root is a doublet of the weave's continuous SU(2). So the portal shape H†Φ_M H is the
    lowest invariant meeting, as §92 said.
  - But 8 of the 112 odd directions sit in two quartets. So "the odd part is 2 ⊗ 56" holds for Q8 only.
- **The identification with Hitchin's uniformizing Higgs field is withdrawn.** This covers §91, the Reading after W74,
  and W75's "three routes agree", which carry dated notes. W75's computations stand, and the lane reproduced U2 to U4.
  What falls is the reading that the lane's condensate and the ordinary Higgs field are one kind. P024's addendum
  carries the same note.
- **The lane answered §92's ask.** The term is in its action.
  - In a local weight-one comparator the potential is f² + 2ft² + t⁴ + 2u²t². The quartic coefficient is 2, and there
    is no standalone ut² at r = 0.
  - A background r = (v + u)H_Q induces 4vut², whose sign follows v.
  - It is not a stationary full E₈ solution, and not a selected flavon.
- **The lane's ask in return.** It asks this seat to define Φ_M as a bulk spin or K field, a fermion bilinear or a
  defect residue, to carry its kinetic, gauge, R and domain map, and to compute its stationary normalized vertex.
  Relay §100 says what the record already says about that choice.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the script first; one run; B1, B2 and B4 as predicted; B3 failed as worded, a sign convention).
A check of the audit lane's character, and a dated withdrawal of this seat's identification. 0 of 19.

## W82. Two checks for main's frame tribunal: lemma A holds with both its halves, and the 112 hold one neutral SU(2)_Q-doublet condensate of each hypercharge sign, conjugate to each other (`the_tribunal_checks.py`)

**Why.** Main's frame tribunal relay (2026-10-14, passed to this seat by the owner ahead of main's S125) asks the seats
to attack its table, its lemma A and its candidate frame F4. It also asks this seat whether two condensates of
opposite hypercharge exist in the lane's 112. Two of these are cheap exact checks.
- **Lemma A (main's, by hand).** Wherever the moves act, a parallel gauge charge is the same on all three generations,
  since the moves permute the units transitively.
- **The hook (main's, from §92).** Two doublets of opposite hypercharge would induce a complex flavon direction whose
  isotropy defect is det[H_u, H_d]². One doublet induces only a real direction, so m₂/m₃ ≥ ½ (B1645).

**The order.** The script, with its predictions and priors in the docstring, was committed before its one run
(bc65d6d3b). Its output was committed before the read-out (4d67f5fe3).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact; every cell as predicted).**
- **L1, lemma A's two halves, by explicit 3 × 3 matrices on the units.**
  - The holonomy's V₄ (the inner automorphisms of Q8 on the units, sign pairs) alone leaves a commutant of
    dimension 3: diagonal charges.
  - The moves' S₃ on the labels alone leaves 2: a + bJ, a democratic charge that splits 1 ⊕ 2.
  - Together they leave 1: scalars. A non-transitive control (V₄ and one transposition) leaves 2.
- **L2, the hook's condensates.** Under each of the six admissible hypercharges of W70, the electrically neutral
  colour-singlet weak-doublet condensates with |Y| = ½ are four roots, ±r₁ and ±r₂. Two have Y = +½ and two Y = −½,
  as W70 found. All four lie in the doublets of the continuous SU(2) of the quaternion Cartan (W81's test), with
  H_Q = ±1. Under the admissible Y where the lane's β is neutral, r₂ = β.

**What it shows (a READING).**
- **Lemma A survives, with its proof completed.** A parallel charge commutes with the holonomy, whose V₄ makes it
  diagonal on the units. The moves then make the diagonal constant. Transitivity alone does not suffice: it allows a
  democratic split, an unalike assignment. So the lemma holds wherever the holonomy is the record's Q8 and the moves
  act on the units through S₃.
- **The 112 hold one condensate, not two.** As complex fields, ±r₁ and ±r₂ are one weak doublet with Y = −½ carrying a
  weave-doublet index, and its conjugate with Y = +½.
  - So a single condensate gives H_u = εH_d* in the weave's index. Then v_a = d†σ_a d is real, and by B1645
    m₂/m₃ ≥ ½.
  - Main's two-condensate hierarchy needs two independent complex fields, for instance the lane's q and r, one taking
    each hypercharge sign. Their misalignment det[u, d]² is then a free datum. Nothing on record fixes it.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the script first; one run; every cell as predicted). Two checks for main's tribunal; the rest of
this seat's attack is relay §101. 0 of 19.

## W83. Main's tribunal item on the lane's frame, answered by a dichotomy: every hypercharge lies in the lane's su(6), and it survives the moves only if they act on the family index other than through O (`the_completion_dichotomy.py`)

**Why.** Main's tribunal lists, among what could overturn its draft, "a gauge completion of the moves that keeps
SU(2)_w and Y, or a proof that none exists in the lane's E₈ frame". In that frame colour is E₆'s commutant, and the
family 3 ⊗ 2_q sits in SU(6) ⊂ E₆ beside SU(2)_w. The lane's own completion keeps the moves but cuts SU(2)_w to its
Cartan and leaves no trace-free Y (kernel 0).

**The order.** The script, with its predictions and priors in the docstring, was committed before its one run
(40a0a129a). Its output was committed before the read-out (c2a524d39).

**The read-out (COMPUTED, exact; M1 as predicted; M2 FAILED as worded in one sub-cell).**
- **M1 holds.** The roots of e₈ orthogonal to colour's two roots and the weak root number 30. With the rank-5 Cartan
  they form an A₅, and the lane's chain is inside it. So the commutant of colour and SU(2)_w is the lane's su(6), and
  every hypercharge lies in it.
- **M2.** On the 6 = 3 ⊗ 2_q, the trace-free Y commuting with the holonomy 1₃ ⊗ Q8 and with the moves' action on the
  family index:
  - through O (signed permutations of the blocks): **0**, as predicted. This is lemma A in the lane's frame.
  - through S₃ only: **1**, the democratic split, as predicted.
  - trivially: **8, not the predicted 2.** With nothing acting on the family index the commutant is the whole su(3) of
    that index, and the block-diagonal Y the prediction had in mind are only its Cartan. The prediction undercounted;
    the dichotomy below does not change.

**What it shows (a READING; in the lane's frame, given its embedding).**
- **The dichotomy.** A completion of the moves that keeps colour and SU(2)_w can keep a hypercharge only if the
  moves act on the lane's family index other than through O. The completion's freedom is the centralizer of colour
  and SU(2)_w, which is SU(6) up to centres. Within SU(6) the implementers' action on the family index is constrained
  only by the relations.
- **Why that matters.** The moves act on the weave's three, the units M, through O (W49), because O is Aut(Q8). So:
  - if the lane's family index is the weave's three, then D1 and D3 cannot both hold in the lane's frame, for any
    completion that keeps colour and SU(2)_w. That is the "none exists" main asked for, conditional on the
    identification;
  - if they do hold together, the lane's family index is not the weave's three, and the three generations must come
    from elsewhere. That is the dictionary question again (P-108).
- **The parent's specification gains a precise form of its clause 1.** The parent's family index must be the weave's
  three, on which the moves act through O; and its hypercharge must then lie outside any su(6) whose 6 is 3 ⊗ 2_q.
  Lemma A forces it to be family-blind.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the script first; one run; M1 as predicted, M2 failed as worded in one sub-cell). An answer to one
of main's tribunal items, by a dichotomy. 0 of 19.

## W84. chat1's thread frame checked: its numbers reproduce at 60 digits, and its Lagrange law tends deep in the cusp to the q-powers that the units' exponents set, m₂/m₃ ∝ |q|¹ and m₁/m₃ ∝ |q|^{3/2} (`the_thread_frame_checked.py`; `W84_RULE.md`)

**Why.** chat1's thread frame (chat1/web-seat @ 39bfc86fe) is built on this seat's W72 forms. Under its hypothesis
H-thread, the couplings are cycle integrals along the vacuum thread's closed geodesic. chat1's Lagrange law,
ln(m₂/m₃) = 7.289 − 7.332·y_max over 59 threads, has a slope within 0.002 of 7π/3. The owner's rule asks that
load-bearing math be verified, and the slope asked whether it is a constant.

**The order.**
- **The rule first** (`W84_RULE.md`, 8bd67016f). It disclosed chat1's banked ratios, the seat's local slopes from them,
  and a texture argument by hand. Four over-long lines were rewrapped before any code, disclosed (4c2f9f0cd).
- **The script** (6029910e2). One run, 9 min 51 s, with its output committed before the read-out (39dd01d0f).

**The method (as the rule fixed it).** The W72 module's own forms, evaluated at 60 digits at each point's image in the
fundamental domain. The seat's own trapezoid integrator over one period of each thread's closed geodesic, with nodes at
half steps from chat1's, N = 512 (N = 1024 as the control). The masses are the singular values of
(K^{−½})ᵀ Y K^{−½}.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; every cell as predicted).**
- **V1, reproduction.** At LR, LLRR, LLR, LLRLR and LLLR, ln(m₂/m₃) and ln(m₁/m₃) agree with chat1's banked values to
  5 × 10⁻¹¹ at worst. h's collocation residual is 10⁻⁴¹.
- **V2, the family L^n R** (n = 1 to 8, y_max = √(n² + 4n)/2 from 1.12 to 4.90):

  | n | y_max | ln(m₂/m₃) | ln(m₁/m₃) | K's largest normalised off-diagonal |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | 1.118 | −0.998 | −4.649 | 2.9 × 10⁻² |
  | 2 | 1.732 | −5.688 | −8.009 | 4.9 × 10⁻⁴ |
  | 3 | 2.291 | −9.789 | −12.701 | 1.6 × 10⁻⁴ |
  | 4 | 2.828 | −13.377 | −17.508 | 1.9 × 10⁻⁵ |
  | 5 | 3.354 | −16.769 | −22.264 | 1.9 × 10⁻⁶ |
  | 6 | 3.873 | −20.094 | −26.938 | 1.8 × 10⁻⁷ |
  | 7 | 4.387 | −23.395 | −31.620 | 1.6 × 10⁻⁸ |
  | 8 | 4.899 | −26.685 | −36.398 | 1.5 × 10⁻⁹ |

  - **V2a holds.** The local slopes of −ln(m₂/m₃) are 7.64, 7.33, 6.68, 6.45, 6.41, 6.42 and 6.43: a spread of 1.23,
    not a constant.
  - **V2b holds.** The last is 6.431, 2.4% above 2π.
  - **V2c holds.** The local slopes of −ln(m₁/m₃) are 5.47, 8.39, 8.95, 9.05, 9.01, 9.10 and 9.34. The last is 0.9%
    below 3π.
  - **V2d holds.** F₊ carries at least 0.9997 of the heaviest state at every n.
  - **V2e holds.** K's normalised off-diagonal falls by about ten each step, to 1.5 × 10⁻⁹ at n = 8.
  - **V2f holds.** N = 1024 and N = 512 agree exactly at n = 8.

**What it shows (a READING; given H-thread, a hypothesis; thread results, one thread at a time).**
- **chat1's thread frame is sound on this seat's forms.** Its numbers reproduce, and its integrals converge.
- **The Lagrange law is not linear in y_max with slope 7π/3.**
  - Its slope falls from about 7.6 to about 6.4 along L^n R, so the 7.33 chat1 fitted over y_max ≤ 3 is an average.
  - Deep in the cusp the law tends to the texture's q-powers. K becomes diagonal, K_aa ∝ |q|^{−2e_a}, with the units'
    exponents e = (7/8, 1/8, 5/8) for (F_i, F₊, F₋). So Ŷ is a Froggatt–Nielsen texture whose charges are those
    exponents. F₊, the shallowest unit, is the heaviest state, m₂/m₃ ∝ |q|^{2(5/8 − 1/8)} = |q|¹ and
    m₁/m₃ ∝ |q|^{2(7/8 − 1/8)} = |q|^{3/2}, with |q| = e^{−2π y_max} = e^{−πλ}, where λ is the Lagrange value.
  - **One residual is not resolved.** The m₂/m₃ slope sits at 6.41–6.43 from y = 3.6 to 4.6, about 2% above 2π, and
    is still drifting slightly upward. A fit with a 1/y term gives 6.37. So this family fixes the scale (2π and 3π)
    and the mechanism, but not the exact limit.
- **What it means for the values.** Given H-thread, a vacuum's hierarchy is set by structure, the units' exponents,
  and by one discrete datum, the thread's Lagrange value. It holds no continuous free number. But one thread gives
  every sector the same ratios, as chat1 says. The sectors' differences need sector-dependent units (other weights or
  representations), and those are not on record.

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first, with a disclosed rewrap before any code; one run; every cell as predicted). A
check of another seat's computation on this seat's forms, and the law's deep-cusp limit. Thread results under a
hypothesis. 0 of 19.

## Reading: on the punctured fibre a bulk Higgs carrying parity couples only through the puncture's residue, and needs one end datum there (READING; nothing computed; 2026-10-14, for main's B1651)

**Why.** Main's B1651 (sealed, not yet run) shows that no flat or harmonic bulk Higgs couples the zero modes on the
closed fibre, and lists "the punctured, parabolic version of this statement" as owed. The record already has that
version, in this seat's C0 reading (item 3). B1642 disclosed two hypotheses: "the Higgs's localization at the puncture
and its coupling through the residue". The same question in Dolbeault form, written out below, reduces the two to one
for a bulk Higgs carrying parity: an end datum at the puncture.

**Weave or thread?** Weave-type: the zero modes are the weave's T on the fibre every thread shares (W21, W49), read at
one τ in the fibre frame. Given Λ (GENESIS FK11) for T as the generations.

**The setting.** E is the fibre, an elliptic curve, and p is the puncture. χ is one of the three parity characters. It
is non-trivial and 2-torsion, and trivial on the puncture loop, since [a, b] maps to 1 in any abelian group. The zero
modes' pairing in that channel is α = v dz, with v = c_r θ_r(0)θ_r(z)/θ₁(z) (B1642's R2, with the characters of its
R5). It is a section of K(p) ⊗ χ, with a simple pole at p.

1. **The parabolic version is on record (C0, item 3).** H¹(F₂; χ) = ℂ, and a cocycle's value on the puncture loop,
   (1 − χ(b))z(a) − (1 − χ(a))z(b), maps it isomorphically onto the boundary. So the parabolic part, the image of the
   compactly supported classes, is 0, and so is the L² cohomology. No parabolic or L² bulk Higgs couples, so B1651's
   step 2 holds on the punctured fibre as well.
2. **Each pairing is fixed by its residue.** In 0 → K ⊗ χ → K(p) ⊗ χ → χ_p → 0, both H⁰ and H¹ of K ⊗ χ vanish (K is
   trivial and χ is not). So the residue Res_p : H⁰(K(p) ⊗ χ) → χ_p is an isomorphism. Each channel has one pairing up
   to scale, and its residue is non-zero. B1642's R3 computes it.
3. **The overlap is a function of the Higgs's class only with a framing at the puncture.**
   - C0 (item 2) shows that the Higgs has type (0, 1) and must carry a parity: H ∈ Ω^{0,1}(χ), with gauge H ↦ H + ∂̄φ.
   - Stokes's theorem on E minus a small disc about p gives ∫ ∂̄φ ∧ α = −2πi φ(p) Res_p α, since φα is single-valued
     and d(φα) = ∂̄φ ∧ α away from p.
   - Res_p α ≠ 0 (item 2). So the overlap ∫ H ∧ α depends on the representative of H's class, unless the gauge
     functions vanish at p. That condition is a framing at the puncture.
   - On the closed fibre there is no such term, and the overlap is a function of the class automatically. A framing at
     any other point does not help, because the term sits at p, where α has its pole.
4. **Framed, the bulk's one coupling is the puncture's residue.**
   - By Serre duality the framed Higgs classes, H¹(O(−p) ⊗ χ), are dual to H⁰(K(p) ⊗ χ), which is one-dimensional.
   - By 0 → O(−p) ⊗ χ → O ⊗ χ → χ_p → 0, with H⁰ and H¹ of O ⊗ χ zero, they are the fibre χ_p: one value at the
     puncture.
   - A representative is ∂̄ψ, with ψ equal to 1 near p and 0 outside a small disc. It is smooth, and supported in an
     annulus about p, as close to p as one likes. Its coupling is −2πi Res_p α.
   - So a framed bulk Higgs with values h_χ at p couples as −2πi Σ_χ h_χ Res_p α_χ, over the three channels. That is
     the residue coupling of B1642 and B1644, with the Higgs's values at the puncture as the coefficients.
   - **B1642's two hypotheses become one.** For a bulk (0, 1) Higgs carrying parity, the localization at the puncture
     and the coupling through the residue follow from the framing.
   - **C0's open bullet** asked whether a boundary class's profile is bounded at the puncture. In this reading it is:
     smooth, and supported as near the puncture as one likes.
5. **The flat class is not bounded.**
   - C0's flat class has the harmonic (0, 1) representative v̄ dz̄, closed since v̄ is antiholomorphic. It grows like
     1/|z| at p.
   - Its overlap with α diverges like log(1/ε), with a coefficient proportional to |Res_p α|².
   - So read flat, the coupling needs a cutoff at the puncture. That is again an end datum, and the residue sets the
     leading term.
6. **What it means for B1651's step 4 (a reading for the tribunal).**
   - **For a point Higgs (B1651's P1),** the three covariant loci stand: the puncture, the half-period orbit, and both.
     "The puncture is not forced" holds there.
   - **For a bulk Higgs carrying parity,** items 1 to 4 force the locus. Every coupling that is a function of the
     Higgs's class factors through the residue at the puncture, and the half-periods carry no bulk class.
   - **The price is one end datum at the puncture:** the framing or, read flat, a cutoff. It is the kind of datum W47
     found nothing on the weave's surface supplies (GENESIS FK10 at the cusp, GAP2 at the fibre's puncture).
   - **So the choice B1651 names is between two kinds of field,** not between points:
     - a bulk Higgs, at the puncture by force, with one end datum;
     - point Higgs fields, each needing its own source. The record has no such source at a half-period (§105).
   - This makes precise the reading after W71, that in Λ's frame "the Higgs at the puncture" is forced. It is forced
     as the locus, at the price of the framing.

**Status.** READING. Items 2 to 5 are proved by hand, from Riemann–Roch and Serre duality on an elliptic curve,
Stokes's theorem, C0's item 3, and B1642's R2, R3 and R5. Nothing is computed by a script. 0 of 19.

## W85. Main's B1651 checked on this seat's own theta series, blind to main's run: every cell holds at 60 digits; L and R carry the pairing's triplet exactly with no Klein element, and the twisted moves share exactly the four body diagonals (`the_b1651_cells_checked.py`; `W85_RULE.md`)

**Why.** The owner's order P-136 reads "B1651, then the frame tribunal, before any further number-chasing", and
B1651's cells U3 to U5 carry the reading the tribunal will lean on. By the owner's rule on load-bearing math, the seat
checked them on its own instrument, as §107 had said it would.

  is 2.4 s.

  (20:12:01Z). The seat's watcher saw only the new head. Main's output was opened after this run's output was
  committed, as the rule required.

**The read-out (COMPUTED; every cell as predicted; the control holds).** The thetas are the seat's own series at 60
digits, with the nome root explicit. Against mpmath's jtheta at |Re τ| < ½ they agree to 2.6 × 10⁻⁶¹. Five random τ
(seed 85) and seven points: the puncture, the three half-periods and three generic points.
- **V0, the multipliers.** The units i, j and k carry (+, −), (−, +) and (−, −) under z ↦ z + π and z ↦ z + πτ, to
  1.7 × 10⁻⁶⁰: the three non-trivial characters.
- **V1, isotropy.** |v·v|/|v|² ≤ 3.1 × 10⁻⁶¹ at all seven points. It is an identity, as B1651 says.
- **V2, the half-periods.**
  - At π/2, πτ/2 and π(1 + τ)/2 the triplet is (0, −1, i), (i, 0, 1) and (i, −1, 0) up to a scalar, to 1.6 × 10⁻⁶¹,
    at every τ. Exactly at those points no Klein element is needed.
  - The negative control holds. The puncture and the generic points move with τ: the best overlap over V₄ between two
    τ falls short of 1 by 3.8 × 10⁻⁴ to 3.1 × 10⁻³.
- **V3, L.** v(z | τ + 1) = e^{iπ/4} Ad(g_L)⁻¹ v(z | τ) to 2.6 × 10⁻⁶¹ at all seven points, including the residue
  triplets at the puncture. The transformed half-periods lie in B1651's classes (τ/2 and (1 + τ)/2 exchanged).
- **V4, R.** At −z/(τ + 1) on the fibre τ/(τ + 1), the triplet is proportional to Ad(g_R) v(z | τ), to 1.6 × 10⁻⁶¹,
  with no Klein element.
  - The scalar is one number at the six regular points (spread 7 × 10⁻⁶¹).
  - At the puncture the scalar is that number times −1/(τ + 1), the residue's extra weight. The seat checked this by
    hand on the first τ, as an arithmetic read-out.
  - The half-periods' classes go as predicted (½ and (1 + τ)/2 exchanged).
- **The read-outs beside V3 and V4.** They agree with B1651's own read-outs.
  - At the puncture and the generic points the other power passes only with the move's axis unit: i for L at s = +1,
    j for R at s = −1.
  - At the half-periods the attaining sets add the image line's own half-turn.
- **V5, the group (exact).**
  - Ad(g_L) and Ad(g_R) generate 24 rotations. The elements that fix the three axes are exactly V₄, and the image on the
    axes is S₃.
  - Over the 16 V₄-twisted pairs exactly four lines are common eigenlines, each of joint dimension 1 and real: the body
    diagonals (ρ, σ, 1). They come from the four half-turn pairs (j·L, i·R), (j·L, k·R), (k·L, i·R) and (k·L, k·R),
    as B1651 found.
- **V6, the seat's own record.** Ad(g_L) and Ad(g_R) are W64's banked L and R on M, which come from the common point's
  moves. Ad(g_L)⁻¹ is W64's post hoc τ + 1 matrix (PH2).

**What it shows.**
- **B1651's U2, U3, U4 and U5 hold on a second instrument,** with conventions fixed independently and 60 digits where
  main's run had about 16 to 30.
- The tribunal can lean on B1651's statement that no constant direction in M is covariant, and that modulo V₄ only the
  four body diagonals are.
- **The two instruments meet the same trap.** W64's K2 once failed on mpmath's principal nome root, the trap B1651's
  explicit root avoids. Both instruments now build θ₁ and θ₂ with e^{iπτ/4}.
- **What it does not touch.** B1651's premise P1 (an even, single-point Higgs) is tested by neither instrument. For a
  bulk Higgs carrying parity, the reading before this section shows that the puncture is forced as the locus, with one
  end datum.

*(Noted 2026-10-14 from the audit lane's review of W85, eb80c5e8c. V2's and V4's criterion is 1 − overlap, so
10⁻⁴⁰ bounds the phase-optimal distance between the unit vectors only by about 1.4 × 10⁻²⁰, since that distance squared
is 2(1 − overlap). The measured 1.6 × 10⁻⁶¹ corresponds to about 6 × 10⁻³¹. V3 is a vector residual, 2.6 × 10⁻⁶¹.
Sampled points at working precision check the identities. They do not certify them; the identities rest on the
thetas' transformation laws, by hand. The lane reviewed the code and did not re-run it.)*

**Status.** COMPUTED (the rule first; one run; every cell as predicted; one wrong timing in a commit message, disclosed
here). A check of main's B1651 on this seat's own code. 0 of 19.

     word: no hit at LR.

## Reading W24–W29 together (READING; the owner asked to contemplate before verifying further)

Nothing here is computed, and nothing here is a result of W30 or W31: their values go in their rules. The order follows
the owner's approved plan of 2026-10-08: contemplate, close the arc, then W30 (step 4) and W31 (the ℤ₅ flux).

1. **Every route has ended in one free 𝔽₂, the hand.**
   - The sign of the index ±3 (W22, W28).
   - A Lagrangian half (W25).
   - The mirror (W26).
   - The semion against the anti-semion (W28, I3).
   - ζ against ζ̄ (W29).
   - What does not depend on the hand is an asymmetry in the weave's own group: the index on 𝕎 is odd, never 0 (W22,
     W24 D0, W28), and the flavor triplet T is complex (W21).
   - That asymmetry is not yet a gauge chirality: W25 shows that the weave's bundles give none without a choice.
   - **A question for main, not a reclassification:** is F-MC's chirality bit the orientation's convention, so that
     what is missing is the dictionary alone, which fields carry 𝕎 (GENESIS FK11)?
2. **What a flux must be to keep the whole Standard Model** (a structural reading; W31 computes it).
   - Holonomies that keep the Standard Model lie in its centraliser in E₈, the bundle factor of W17, W23 and W29 times
     U(1)_Y (sm:B1384's handoff check).
   - Their commutator lies in the bundle factor. For a twist-eater it is central there, so it is of order 5.
   - W29's ℤ₆ flux, e^{2πiY}, therefore cannot keep the hypercharge, which is W29's lemma seen from the other side.
3. **The binary polyhedral groups along the moves** (READING).
   - On a ℤ_p clock-and-shift pair the moves act through SL(2, 𝔽_p).
   - p = 2: SL(2, 𝔽₂) ≅ S₃. The qubit's lifts reach 2O only together with the Paulis (W28).
   - p = 3: SL(2, 𝔽₃) ≅ 2T, McKay E₆, F-MC's prime of norm three.
   - p = 5: SL(2, 𝔽₅) ≅ 2I, McKay E₈ (B206).
   - W30 and W31 compute the p = 3 and p = 5 lifts. The McKay pairings are a reading, not a step.
4. **The count is read from how the moves split the local solutions.**
   - A sector with puncture holonomy e^{2πiα} on r channels has index −rα + dim Λ₊. The move-invariant Λ₊ are sums of
     the lifts' pieces.
   - The qubit ⊗ ℂ³: −3, −1, +1, +3, and ±3 under naturality (W28). These hold with or without the bare sign move
     (W29 post hoc, P3).
   - ℤ₆: −1, 1, 3, 5 under L and R. Only −1 or 5 if the bare sign −I is a move (GENESIS GM5b, FK4; W29 post hoc, P3).
   - So which moves the grammar allows (the sign, GM5b; the swap, GM5c) decides the counts. GENESIS keeps both forks
     open.
5. **The Standard Model's ℤ₆ cannot be the fibre's flux with the whole Standard Model unbroken** (W29; point 2).
6. **F-MC's trit and the weave's three (a question for main, with no map proposed).**
   - THE_CLAIM §1 counts the VEV acceptance as "ONE TRIT" (B1030): a ℤ/3 label, the 27's three 9-blocks against the
     three surviving SU(3)'s, triality-transitive.
   - The weave's three parities are permuted by S₃, none distinguished (W1, Theorem G).
   - Equal labels in different places are not an identification (B1231's discipline). The question is whether a map
     between the two exists, not a claim that they are one.
7. **A correction to keep straight.** In F-MC the hypercharge direction and the global form are DERIVED (B862, B864,
   B991). F-MC's five typed inputs are:
   - two 𝔽₂ bits (time's arrow; chirality);
   - one scale;
   - one Lie type J;
   - one rank-closing VEV direction.

## Reading W24–W32 together, with the audit lane's packets and main's S88 (READING; the owner: "lets be brave")

Nothing here is computed. It reads the arcs since the first contemplation, main's S87 and S88 (B1607, B1609), and the
audit lane's five sealed packets of 2026-10-08 on its branch `audit/physical-bridge-2026-09-05` (the puncture sheaf,
the form fermions, the companion roster, the curved action, the cusp condensate). The owner asked what we are not
seeing.

1. **The hands are conventions unless something forced orients the records.**
   - Curie's principle (the record's P012): a symmetric principle does not output an asymmetric choice. The Standard
     Model and its mirror or CP image are one theory with the labels exchanged.
   - Main's S88 (B1609): the three's hand is the records' orientation, not the McKay orientation. Main's next question
     is whether anything the principle forces picks a sheet of the orientation double cover.
   - **The brave form of the answer:** forced to choose, not forced which. The flat, symmetric point has no gap at the
     cusp (the audit lane). A physical end needs a stationary condensate. If that condensate distinguishes the two
     orientations, every physical end picks a hand, and the two choices are mirror images. That is how a derived
     chirality looks in physics: a spontaneously chosen vacuum, not a selected law.
2. **The three are the three even spin structures of the fibre torus; the odd one is the troublemaker.**
   - A torus has three even spin structures and one odd one; the moves permute the even ones and fix the odd one.
   - The odd one carries a chiral mode that cannot be switched off (W22: its index is odd, never zero).
   - So frames that force it in give 3 + 1 or 1: F-HE's rank five (W32's four is the three plus the odd one). Frames
     that exclude it give three: E₆ with 𝕎 (W27). The audit lane's roster shows the same split, three alike spin lines
     and one trivial.
   - The question "why three" becomes "why does the odd spin structure carry no generation". In the ℤ₂ × ℤ₂ orbifold
     the record already cites (W24), the odd one is the untwisted sector and the generations come from the three
     twisted ones.
   - **Corrected by W33 (the same evening, the rule committed first).** As a law across the frames this point fails.
     The doublet blocks are label-blind (χ_p ⊗ ρ_Q ≅ ρ_Q), so the counts see how many doublets a sector has, not
     which spin structure labels them. What survives is the precise form: the zero parity's doublet in the parity
     doublets' sector makes four.
3. **The counts are sheaf Euler characteristics; a particle count needs an end that gaps the cusp.**
   - The audit lane justified the record's formula as a sheaf Euler characteristic: parabolic weights ½ and
     Riemann–Roch on the compactified curve give χ = d − 3.
   - At the complete cusp, ordinary spinors have zero in their essential spectrum (not Fredholm). Twisted one-forms
     keep W21's triplet with a finite norm and a gap. So the dictionary is likely a topological twist, with W21's form
     triplet as its healthy part.
   - Every roster read so far is self-conjugate (as W25 found). The missing physical step is a mechanism, an end on the
     same action that gaps the continuum: GENESIS GAP3's source. The audit lane's stationary cusp condensate lifts 58
     of 112 channels. It keeps colour and breaks the weak SU(2), whose centre in E₈ is the puncture's −1 (checked here
     by the branching: both act as −1 on (3, 2, 6), (3̄, 2, 6̄) and (1, 2, 20); W25's chi248 at −1 in SU(6)′ is 24, an
     involution with centraliser E₇ × SU(2)).
4. **Three may be a consistency number, not an index (untested).** Three generations is the minimum for a CP phase in
   quark mixing (Kobayashi–Maskawa). The weave allows at most three alike things and carries a complex structure on
   them (W21's T, ω). A lower bound and an upper bound would meet at three without any index.
5. **Rejected: the three as colour.** It fits on the surface: colours are exactly alike, the moves act as SU(3)'s Weyl
   group, and Λ²(D ⊕ P) repeats SU(5)'s 10. But colour must commute with the fibre's holonomy, which tells the three
   parity lines apart, so colour would be broken to its torus. The 10-pattern is SU(5) branching.

**The riddle restated.** The hands were never derivable and need not be: what is derivable is that a physical end
must choose one. The count's real question is the odd spin structure. Beneath both sits the physical step the audit lane
is building: an end on the weave's own action that gaps the cusp.

**Tests this reading proposes (none run here).**
- Does the stationary cusp condensate distinguish the two orientations, that is, is its mirror image gauge-equivalent
  to it? If it is not, the end chooses the hand. (The audit lane's action; relayed.)
- Is "three exactly when the odd spin structure is excluded" a law across the record's frames?
- Does the weave's natural flavour structure carry a phase no rephasing removes?

- **In the owner's terms (READING).**
  - The three is the shared records', not any thread's: every thread acts on the same three parities.
  - One odd-trace thread already cycles them as an irreducible triplet; every thread GENESIS's SE1 admits is such a
    thread. A thread with even trace breaks it.
  - What the weave adds is that it is one triplet for all threads; a group on it that no thread has, S₄, which is real;
    and Theorem S: three alike and separate need the third tick.
- **What W6 settles, and what it does not.**
  - The frame that reads a generation (sm:B1515's F-HE) is built on one thread's own holonomy, so the content is read
    thread by thread, and it is a thread result.
  - The weave decides how it is shared, by Theorem G, and the census reads it on every thread by rule.
  - The synthesis against the physics is `docs/THREE_GENERATIONS_AND_THE_WEAVE.md`.

## Files

- `moves_and_parities.py` → `moves_and_parities.json`: W1, every word to length 8.
- `the_common_point.py` → `the_common_point.json`: W2–W4. sympy's `solve` drops roots it cannot write in radicals, so
  the points are read from Gröbner bases. The quaternion closures are numerical on a group of 48 elements.
- `the_lines_census.py`: W6's census, one JSON line per thread. Its record is `the_lines_census.json`, with the order-3
  pass in `the_lines_census_order3.json`.
- `the_lines.py` → `the_lines.json`: Theorem G checked on every member orbit.
- `the_lines_content.py` → `the_lines_content.json`: what each parity carries.
- `the_mixing.py` → `the_mixing.json`: the weave's group on the triplet and the mixing of its subgroups (READING).
- `the_weaves_laws.py` → `the_weaves_laws.json`: the trichotomy along the wave, the triplet's group (T_d, O_h, real), and
  Theorem S, for `docs/THE_WEAVES_LAWS.md`.
- `draft_chiral_counts/DESIGN_FOR_REVIEW.md`: the design sent to main. It was sealed as sm:B1552 on the owner's ruling,
  ahead of main's review (W11).
- `the_hand_theorem.py` → `the_hand_theorem.json`: W15, Theorem H's ingredients on the generators, the tick-3 act on every odd-trace state to length 8, and the hand rule from the determinant on all 758 states to length 12.
- `the_weave_extension.py` → `the_weave_extension.json`: W15, the weave's own extensions: the census to length 6, two further primes, and length 8 under the reduced rule.
- `W15_PREDICTION.md`: the mod-16 prediction, committed before its test; `the_prediction_test.py` → `the_prediction_test.json`: its second part, F-CI on ±LLLLLRRR with the control ±LLLLLLLR.
- `the_common_point_mod_3.py` → `the_common_point_mod_3.json`: W16, main's B1601 on this seat's route (the point at 1 200 digits or more, the field checked exactly, the ideal at the primes of the norm gcd), 37 geometries.
- `the_weaves_five_tick1.py` → `the_weaves_five_tick1.json`: W17, the weave's SU(5)′ five on the thread itself, every odd-trace state to length 8.
- `W18_PREDICTION.md`: the per-parity prediction, committed before its test; `the_parity_generations.py` → `the_parity_generations.json`: W18, the five's sectors on the forced cover by Shapiro (32 states) and directly at the resolving tick (twelve states).
- `the_parity_generations_special.py` → `the_parity_generations_special.json`: W18, post hoc: the whole line of gluing classes on every carrier over GF(73) and GF(97), two special classes on each.
- `W20_RULE.md`: the rule, committed before the census; `the_weaves_count_in_e6.py` → `the_weaves_count_in_e6.json`: W20, the weave's Euler characteristic with coefficients in E₆'s 27 under every SL(2) in E₆.
- `the_weaves_count_orbifold.py` → `the_weaves_count_orbifold.json`: W20's third route, Brown's formula over the elliptic elements, with the traces at the square and hexagonal tori.
- `W21_RULE.md`: the rule, committed before the run; `the_holomorphic_triplet.py` → `the_holomorphic_triplet.json`: W21, the Hodge–Riemann form on V and on the spin doublet by the cup product, its controls, and which triplet is holomorphic (`--controls` runs the controls alone).
- `the_e8_frames_on_the_fibre.py` → `the_e8_frames_on_the_fibre.json`: W23, the record's E₈ frames on the fibre over every bundle built from the common point's blocks.
- `the_couplings_verified.py` → `the_couplings_verified.json`: W38, main's B1615 and B1616 recomputed (a verification, not blind).
- `CONVENTIONS.md`: the dossier's conventions, enforced by `tests/test_weave_conventions.py`; `tests/test_weave_regeneration.py` reruns the fast scripts against their stored outputs.
- `W55_RULE.md`: the rule, committed before the code; `the_gauge_side_and_the_sectors.py` → `the_gauge_side_and_the_sectors.json`: W55, the down–lepton tie's exact bound at three allowances and its threshold allowance, the Kronecker lemma on 400 cases, and the Standard Model frame's tie read from W54.
- `W56_RULE.md`: the rule, committed before the code; `the_higgs_and_the_generation_index.py` → `the_higgs_and_the_generation_index.json`: W56, the E₆ identity from the trinification cubic on 200 cases with its control, the coupling forms of the two generation-type placements by weight, the census of the Higgs placements at the geometry's weight, and their ranks.
- `W57_RULE.md`: the rule, committed with the second-scale data before the code; `the_field_of_maximality.py` → `the_field_of_maximality.json`: W57, the seed's measure against anarchy on 10⁶ draws each, the ladder, the pseudo-data, the marginals, the read-outs; `the_field_of_maximality_posthoc.py` → `the_field_of_maximality_posthoc.json`: W57 post hoc (labelled), nearest-neighbour typicality and the shares.
